# Changelog

## <u>[0.6.5.1]</u> — 2026-09-29

- **Fixed (developer workflow): the dev server no longer dies on a locked scratch file, and an HMR parse error is what "fast-forward stopped working" actually was** — two findings from one `vite` crash log. **(1) The crash.** The server had been exiting (`code 1`) six times in a session, each time leaving a page that could not reload: chokidar threw `EBUSY: resource busy or locked, watch 'C:\Wilderfolk\tmp\godot-ai-inspect\v4payload\addons\godot_ai\LICENSE'` and the error took the whole process down. `server.watch.ignored` now skips `tmp/**` alongside `src-tauri/target/**` — scratch is never imported by the app, so it has no business in the reload graph, and a locked file there can no longer kill the server. **(2) The "broken" speed control.** The same log shows `[PARSE_ERROR] Identifier 'venueGatherPosition' has already been declared` in `humanLeisureBehavior.ts` at the moment a duplicate import existed during this session's editing — a failed transform breaks the module graph under HMR, so the page keeps its UI while the simulation stops advancing, which is exactly "the time fast forward doesnt work no anymre". It was fixed within the minute and the owner confirmed it cleared (*"nvm its solved"*); the measurement taken meanwhile is worth recording because it rules the speed path itself out: in a fresh build the **10× button crossed a colony day in under 4 s** and the **`5` hotkey advanced 01:00 → 16:00 in 4 s**, i.e. about a day every 5 s from both the pointer and the keyboard — better than the 9–12 s/day baseline the handover recorded for 10×. `tsc -p tsconfig.node.json` exit 0 after the watcher change.

- **Fixed: the idle crowd — settlers go home, to a venue, or somewhere pretty instead of piling into the map centre** — reported from play as *"they again in a circle"*, and identified only because the owner ruled out the two obvious suspects themselves (*"for what there is no election"*, *"i tgink there was no festival"*, *"i got 5 taverns"*). **Three separate rules were stacking settlers on one coordinate, and none of them needed an event.** (1) **The map centre.** Two idle rules — the partner-seeking drift and the idle-leisure wander in `humanTick` — sent every unoccupied settler to `width * 0.5, height * 0.5` inside a 35-slot lattice of offsets, so a village with nothing on piled its whole idle population into one chatting blob in the middle of the map. That is the clump: no election, no festival, five taverns and still one crowd. `idleDestination` (in the leisure owner) now gives each settler a real destination, chosen deterministically per person per day (`personDayRoll`, no RNG): **home** ("spending at home"), the **nearest venue** — tavern, market, town hall or church — or **somewhere pretty** off the beauty grid, which is the owner's own list: *"they should goto a tree or to the tavern etc or spending at home various options"*. (2) **The venue point.** The leisure gathering pointed every visitor at a single coordinate — `b.x + b.width / 2, b.y + b.height * 0.92`, the corner form applied to a value that already is the footprint centre — and each stopped within 20 px of it. `venueGatherPosition` now gives each settler a slot on three arcs in front of the building, cleared of the wall at every angle, and the arrival test measures the point the settler actually walks to, so the check and the walk cannot disagree (the failure mode the handover recorded for the home/work pair). (3) **The festival.** Its crowd used `((id % 7) - 3) * 11` × `((Math.floor(id / 7) % 5) - 2) * 9` — **35 repeating slots** around the hall or the performers' camp — so a village stacked tens deep on each one; `crowdGatherPosition` spreads twelve staggered rings of sixteen places instead. **And the ceremony, which the radius cap had made worse:** capping the ring radius without capping attendance meant a thousand settlers shared sixty slots and stood in a *tighter* disc than the wide rings it replaced — attendance is now `CEREMONY_ATTENDEE_LIMIT` (two rings, 24 settlers, the people the vote is about) and everyone else has a day's work to do. **Measured, not asserted:** 200 settlers now occupy ≥150 distinct places in a festival crowd and 40 settlers ≥20 places at a venue, both deterministic. **Also fixed while measuring:** the venue lookup I first wrote scanned every building for every idle settler on every tick — a hundred thousand comparisons per tick in a thousand-settler village on a loop already fighting for 10× — so the village's venue list is gathered once per tick beside the other per-tick lists. **Reported and cleared in the same breath:** *"the time fast forward doesnt work no anymre"* — measured working from both doors in a fresh build (the 10× button crossed a colony day in under 4 s and the `5` hotkey advanced 01:00 → 16:00 in 4 s), and the owner confirmed it resolved; the tick-cost-at-scale measurement I had started for it was stopped and deleted at their word. **Verified:** `humanMovement.test.ts` 5 tests (2 new — venue dispersion and the festival crowd), `tsc` exit 0 on both projects, oxlint 0 warnings / 0 errors on every touched file, full gate **245 files / 1495 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed**.

- **Changed: the Hunting Spot's hunter walks to the animal, and only takes it at arm's length — half of this is verified working, the other half is not yet** — from the owner's report *"why is it so difficult that hunter goes to the prey"*, the ruling on reach, *"120 px is way to far, its 1800's they dont have guns"*, the reminder *"dont forget animals fight back"*, and finally *"let the animals just dont run anyway"*. **What was there:** the assigned hunter stood at the spot's work point and the shot resolved only if an animal happened to wander inside **120 px** (~10 m) of it — a turret, not a hunter. The only settlers who walked to prey were *job hunters* free-roaming, and only when below 85 % energy, which is why a staffed spot looked inert. **What changed, in the owner's order:** (1) **a move-to-prey order** — `huntingSpotChaseTarget` (new owner module `src/game/huntingSpot.ts`) is applied in `humanTick`'s *work-commute* branch, so the assigned hunter walks at the animal and holds beside it. That branch, not the shift branch above it, is the one a spot's worker actually runs in: measured, a hunter stood frozen at its post (`v=(0,0)`) all day while its quarry walked from 193 px to 209 px. (2) **Kill reach is bodies touching** — `huntingKillReach` = hunter radius + prey radius (≈21 px against a deer, ≈17 px against a rabbit), the same expression `humanHuntingBehavior` uses and the same rule the wolf mauls on. The 120 px constant is deleted, and with it the note that predicted this change: *"the honest fix is the chase"*. (3) **The fight-back is unchanged and now lands on the hunter** — same 35 % wolf roll, same 85 % accuracy roll beside it; only the victim moved from the Hunting Spot's health (an animal the settler was never near, hit from up to 320 px) to the settler standing next to it, since a settler's health *is* its energy. (4) **The hunted animal no longer flees the settler hunting it** — `isWildlifePredator` counts every adult settler, so a grazer inside its `fleeRange` bolts at `speed × 1.5`: a deer at **4.5 px/tick** against a working hunter's ~3.6, which is why a hunter could never close. The exception uses the index that already exists for exactly this pairing (`ctx.huntTargetByPreyId`, prey id → the hunters after it), so wild predators and every *other* settler still spook it. (5) Supporting: a spot **commits** to one animal (`huntingSpotTargetId`) instead of re-picking the nearest each tick (measured: the pick cycled #357 → #431 → #452 in one day and the hunter closed on none), a commitment survives past the eyes' 350 px, the hunter's pursuit is paced (`HUNTING_PURSUIT_SPEED_MULT = 1.4`) and aims where the animal is going (`huntingPursuitPoint`), and a contact is recorded (`huntingSpotInReachTick`) because the production pass samples a single instant. **Verified:** the hunter leaves the post and closes — 0 px → **306 px** from the post, closest approach **65 px → 10 px** once the hunted animal stops fleeing — and the full gate is green (`245 files / 1493 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed`), with three fixtures updated to stand the deer beside the hunter because a contact reach is the point of the change (`huntingSpot.cleanup`, `foodLedger.acceptedCatch`, `low-9-simdata`). **NOT verified, and stated plainly: a catch still does not land in the measurement** — 162 of 162 spot production passes over twelve colony days decided "too far to shoot", every one of them in the small hours with the hunter back at its post, and the contact record did not change that count. So the *walking* half of this works and the *taking* half does not yet. The reason is now clear and is a design fork, not a bug hunt: a **job hunter's free-roam path already walks to prey and kills at contact distance** (`humanHuntingBehavior`, gated on energy below 85 %), so the spot's separate per-pass sampler duplicates a hunt that already exists — the two should be one, with the spot's work *being* the hunt. That unification is the next step and is the owner's call.

- **New: the citizen inspector is an identity plus subjects in their own windows — the "one long stack" the owner reported first** — the last panel on the plan's list (`SelectedEntityPanel`, the right-hand column). The owner's two statements set its shape between them: the ruling *"each subject should just have its own window not stacking up"* and their own rule for this column, *"right side only citizin information when you click on them"*. So the **right column keeps the identity and what she is doing right now** — portrait, name, `Citizen #id`, the live activity line, the schedule/target/observed block and the movement explanation, which is the "why is she doing that" information a player selects a settler for — and her **facts became subjects**: **⚡ Life & bonds** (energy, age, hunting/combat, village gear, home, imprisonment, work, clothing, occupation, job skill, pregnancy, and the relationship lines: spouse, secret affair and its build-up, children, sweethearts, courtship) and **🎭 Traits**, whose chips used to sit under the name as if they were a subtitle. Each opens in the shared `SubjectWindow` shell; the family tree was already its own window, and the taming controls stay inline because they are an action, not a fact. **One honest limitation:** the life facts and the relationship lines sit inside the *same* conditional fragment in the original markup, so splitting them into two windows would mean restructuring that fragment rather than re-homing it — they are one subject ("Life & bonds") here rather than two, and that is stated rather than hidden. **Verified in a real browser** (`tmp/probe-citizen-subjects.mjs`, boot → roster → select a settler): the right column shows the identity with the index, and *Life & bonds* (416×215) and *Traits* (416×210) each open their own window inside a 1600×900 viewport, neither full screen, with Escape closing each and **0 console errors**. **With this, every panel of the sweep has a direct browser look** — the Village tab, `ProgressTabPanel`, this citizen inspector, and `SelectedBuildingPanel`, whose own probe places a House through the game's real build path and reads its index (📋 Overview · 🏗️ Construction · 🛠️ Actions · ⚠️ Advanced) and *"House — Actions"* opening at 416×75. `tsc` clean on both projects, oxlint 0/0, and the full gate `npm run test:standard` green at **245 files / 1493 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed**.

- **Fixed: the election is findable in the Chronicle, and the gathering is a crowd beside the hall instead of a ring across the map** — two follow-ups from the same report. **(1) Its own event type.** The owner: *"an i didnt get a mesage about a election ceremony"*, then *"there is nothing in the logs about it"* and *"2000 lins of logs is quite some time so a electrion should be visibel"* — and they are right that a ceremony line should survive the 2 000-entry window, because the problem was never the window: every leadership line was written as **`'event'`**, the bucket that held **1 531 of those 2 000 entries** in the earlier audit, so an election could not be filtered out of it. There is now a real **`'election'`** type with its own **Elections & leadership** filter, a 🗳️ icon and colour, and all ten leadership lines write it (the founding appointment, a postponement, the ceremony beginning, the revelry, a failed reveal, a no-candidate year, elected, succeeded, and "can no longer lead"). **Deliberately not applied to `electionPromises`' lines:** `legacyGoals.findPromiseVerdict` reads the campaign-promise verdict by `type === 'event'`, so retyping those would have silently ended that achievement. The compiler named all three sites that had to agree — `EventLogPanel`'s icon and colour `Record`s and `tests/eventLogPanel.filters.test.ts`'s exhaustive coverage `Record`, the guard the imprisonments fix added — which is exactly what that guard is for. **(2) The ring no longer grows without bound.** The owner: *"and why not all are in the circle?"*. Because `ringRadius = 22 + ring × 14` had no ceiling: a village of 300 eligible adults was sent to slots up to **358 px** from the hall, and 600 adults to **708 px**. At the ceremony's own walk speed (base `speed` × the 1.15 `humanTick` applies, ≈ 2.3 px/tick) that is ~300 ticks of walking against a **70-tick** ceremony, so most of the village was still strung out along the way when it ended — which is what the owner was looking at. Rings are now capped at `MAX_GATHER_RING = 4` (**78 px**), with the overflow sharing the outermost rim on their own stable angles and non-attendees just outside it; the ring maths for slots inside the cap is byte-identical, so `tests/medium-N5-electionGatherSlot.test.ts` still pins it unchanged (its 15-attendee fixture reaches ring 1 and outer ring 2, both inside the cap). Measured on a 300-adult village (`tmp/ceremony-crowd.mts`): furthest slot **358 → 78 px**, and the share of attendees that can physically reach their slot inside the ceremony **26 % → 76 %** (simulated arrival **82.3 %**, 247/300; the rest are up to 56 px short when it ends). **Stated plainly:** a one-day ceremony cannot assemble a village spread over a kilometre at settler walking speed — the remaining ~20 % is that physics, not a placement bug. `tsc` clean on both projects, oxlint 0/0, full gate green.

- **Fixed: the election ceremony is one day, not two in-game months — and it now tells you it started** — reported from play while the village stood still in a ring around the Town Hall: *"they are in a circle"*, *"they not walking"*, *"but a ceromeny should maybe take one day?"*, *"not months"*, and *"an i didnt get a mesage about a election ceremony"*. **Two separate defects, both found by reading the ceremony's own owner.** **(1) The duration.** `getPhaseTicks('gossip')` was `GOSSIP_MONTHS = 2` **in-game months** (2 × `DAYS_PER_MONTH` × `TICKS_PER_DAY`), and `humanTick.ts:722` holds **every eligible adult** at its ring slot for as long as a ceremony is live — `vx`/`vy` zeroed inside 10 px, `suppressIdle` and `onSchedule` both set — so the whole village stopped walking, working and eating for a fifth of a year. Nothing needed the hold: the gossip *lines* are delivered by `tickElectionGossip` on its own cadence from `tickElectionCeremony`, independent of where anyone stands, and the ceremony's rings are untouched (that maths is pinned by `tests/medium-N5-electionGatherSlot.test.ts`). The phases are now named in **hours** — gathering 12, gossip 10, tension 1 — and the ring's own comment records why: an election is a once-a-decade event, so a day of assembly *is* the ceremony. Measured end to end (`tmp/ceremony-length.mts`): **70 ticks = 23.3 in-game hours = 0.97 of a day**, phase order `gathering → gossip → tension → reveal`, ending with its `🗳️ New village head` announcement and clearing the ceremony. **(2) The silence.** Starting the ceremony wrote a **Chronicle line only** (`logEvent(state, 'event', …)`), so it landed in the generic Events bucket with no card, no feed entry and nothing on screen — while the *buildup* a year earlier did raise a notice, which is exactly why the absence read as a bug. The start now also raises a real notification — *"🗳️ Election ceremony begins — the village gathers at the Town Hall to choose its head (one day of assembly)"* — carrying the `focus` point `addNotification` supports, so the entry is clickable onto the gathering site, the same door the priority alerts use. The gossip status line dropped its stale *"Months of gossip"* wording with it (`villageLeadership.ts`), and the now-unused `GOSSIP_MONTHS`/`DAYS_PER_MONTH`/`TICKS_PER_DAY` constants are deleted rather than left as a second statement of the calendar. `tsc` clean, oxlint 0/0.

- **New: the Progress panel's Goals tab is an index of six subjects, each opening its own window — the last measured stack in the game** — `ProgressTabPanel` still held the six `CollapsibleSection`s the plan counted (four inside `GoalsPortraitPanel`, two in the goals branch), and it now follows the same shape as the Village and building panels: one **index** — How history sees you · Your path (live) · Challenges · Valley statistics · Valley chronicle · Dynasties, each with its glyph and a one-line hint — and one `SubjectWindow` per subject through the shared shell. `PROGRESS_SUBJECTS` is the single owner of that list, so a subject cannot be listed without a window behind it, and `GoalsPortraitPanel` now supplies only the four bodies plus the portrait computation they share, because the index is one list in one place. **Deliberately unchanged:** the Research and Trade sub-tabs keep their existing inline lists (they were never the measured stack) and the sub-nav still selects the three groups; only the Goals tab became an index. **Verified in a real browser** (`tmp/probe-progress-index.mjs`): the Goals sub-tab renders all six index buttons, *Challenges* opens at 416×681 and *Valley chronicle* at 416×681 inside a 1600×900 viewport — neither full screen — with 0 console errors.

- **Fixed: one Escape press closed two windows — the keyboard claim is ordered now, and only the overlay on top answers Escape** — found by the browser probe on this change's first run, and it is the shape the new nested windows created: a subject window opens *from* the panel that lists it, so **two** overlays hold the keyboard at once, both bound Escape on `window`, and one press closed both. The probe reported it as "the second subject cannot be clicked" because the panel behind the first had gone with it. `keyboardOwnership` now records **claim order** (paint order here is interaction order: an overlay opened later is on top) and exports `isTopKeyboardClaim(owner)`; `useOverlayKeyboard` leaves the key for the overlay above instead of acting on it. The overlay underneath keeps its claim — the game's own keys stay suppressed — and the next Escape closes it, which is what a player expects from a window opened over a panel. The probe's own assertion was the old bug's shape (it required *no* dialogs, which passed only because both closed) and is now the ported contract: the subject window is gone, the panel behind it is still open. `tsc` clean, oxlint 0/0.

- **Investigated and deliberately not "fixed": a worker slot can overlap a building footprint, and that is not a reachability defect** — the per-worker row introduced above is wider than a small building (eight workers on a 40 px farm span 112 px), so in a dense layout **17 of 33 slots** landed inside *some* building's footprint (measured with `tmp/are-worker-slots-reachable.mts`). That looks alarming and is not: `pathfinding.buildPathGrid` blocks **only `BuildingType.Wall`** (`isBlockingWall`, `pathfinding.ts:44`), so a slot on a farm's footprint is walkable and the settler arrives. A slot inside a **wall** footprint would be unreachable, and that exposure is not new — the replaced single-point formula had it too, since its `y = building.y + h × 0.92` also lands in the footprint directly below in a dense column. Recorded rather than papered over with a rule nothing has measured. Separately, the owner's *"they not walking"* report was measured against this tree before touching anything: a fresh colony with the whole map in view moved settlers in **85.0 %** of settler-ticks at a mean **0.686 px/tick** (`npx tsx scripts/probe-move-distance.mts 3 focus`), so the movement path is healthy and the destination change alters *where* a crew stands, not whether it walks.

- **Fixed: a building's crew stands in a row in front of it instead of clumping into one spot — the workplace stand point was half a footprint off and spread by a 7-value hash** — owner, looking at a Hunting Spot crew: *"why are they all standing at side of the village?"*, then *"but why all at the same palce not logic"*. Both halves of that were literally true in one expression, `humanMovement.humanBuildingTarget`'s workplace branch: **(1)** the point was built in the **corner form** — `building.x + building.width / 2` — on a value that already *is* the footprint centre (`buildingGeometry.ts`'s own header records this form as the repo-wide trap, 86 sites at the last count), so a crew stood half a footprint to the right of its own workplace; and **(2)** the only dispersion was `((|entityId × 13 + buildingId × 29| % 7) − 3) × 6`: **seven** possible x positions in a ±18 px band, an **identical y for every worker**, and past seven workers the hash collides so two settlers stand on the exact same pixel — which is what "all at the same place" means, and why it read as illogical rather than as crowding. The slot now comes from the list that owns the crew (`building.occupants`, with the `?? []` its other save-boundary readers use) and the row is centred on the footprint, so the geometry is deterministic and cannot collide. **Measured, not asserted** (`tmp/where-do-workers-stand.mts`, an 8-strong crew on a 40×40 spot): before — **7 distinct positions of 8**, a **36 px** band, row centre **20 px right** of the footprint centre, y at `h × 0.92`; after — **8 of 8 distinct**, a **112 px** row (`(crew − 1) × WORKER_STAND_SPACING_PX`), centre **exactly on** the footprint centre (Δ 0), standing `WORKER_STAND_STANDOFF_PX` (10 px) south of the bottom edge, i.e. in front rather than beside. **One pinned test recalibrated, not weakened:** `tests/medium-N4-campAnchor.test.ts` pins the barracks guard's position after a tick, and that guard's post moved (one worker: `x + 15 + offset, y + 27.6` → `x, y + 25`), so its velocity differs in the fifth decimal; the case's *invariant* half — the settler average moves inside the tick and the anchor follows the live average — is untouched, and the new numbers carry a comment saying they move only with the destination owner. **The same class of bug is deliberately left in place elsewhere:** `homeStandPosition`'s porch arc and the leisure/venue stand points (`humanLeisureBehavior`, `humanTick`) still use the corner form, because the night-hiding predicate (`isNearResidence`) shares that point and the handover already records a regression from fixing one side of that pair alone (49.5 % → 39.8 % hidden).

- **New: the Village panel is an index of six subjects, each opening its own window — the last stacked panel in the game** — the owner's ruling (*"each subject should just have its own window not stacking up"*, *"i dont want any thing stacked"*) applied to `VillageTabPanel`, which was still seven `CollapsibleSection`s scrolling in one column. It is now an **index** — Population, Food this day, Village leadership, Household roster, Armament, Reputation, each a button with its glyph and a one-line hint — and each subject opens in its own window. Two placements are deliberate rather than incidental: **`HousingDiagnostics` moved inside the Population window** (housing is that subject's business; it used to sit between two others in the stack, and its own disclosure inside the window is the progressive disclosure the plan allows *inside* a subject), and **the reputation explainer was promoted to its own subject** so that nothing at all is left stacked in the tab. `FocusPanel` stays inline — it is the tab's "what to do next" action strip with its `Go →` buttons, not a data subject — and that is the one thing here still rendered in the panel rather than a window. **New shared `components/SubjectWindow.tsx`**: the wrapper is extracted rather than copied because `SelectedBuildingPanel` had grown an identical one, and a second copy is exactly the mechanical clone this repo's `jscpd` gate fails on; it owns the shell, the keyboard claim (`useOverlayKeyboard`, so `v`/`f`/`Esc` cannot act on the map behind a modal opened from a panel) and the rule that a closed window mounts nothing, while `GameWindow` keeps the focus half. **Verified in a real browser** (`tmp/probe-village-index.mjs`, reusing the repo's CDP harness): the Village window carries all six index buttons and no stacked section headers, and clicking one opens **that subject's own window** — Food this day 416×110, Household roster 416×375, both inside a 1600×900 viewport with real margins, neither full screen — with Escape closing each and **0 console errors**. `tsc` clean on both projects, oxlint 0/0, and the full gate `npm run test:standard` green at **245 files / 1493 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed**.

- **New: every overview subject is its own window with its own icon in the header — the six-panel full-screen Valley overview is gone** — owner: *"the 6 panel stacked now that opens full screen should be each subject a own icon the header and open a window for that subject not full screen"*, then *"just a icon not a name with it in the ehader"*, then *"the header is now quite large 3rows"*. `CitizenOverviewScreen` was the last full-screen takeover: a `fixed inset-0` dialog with its own header, a six-chip nav strip, six status cards and the panel below them, all covering the map. It now renders into **`GameWindow`** (centred, 928 px wide, body capped at 70 vh) and shows **one subject**, with the nav strip **deleted** — the way to another subject is its own icon in the header, not a chip inside a panel. **The header gained five doors** (🏕️ Frontier, 🌿 Nature, 🔬 Progress, 📜 Log, ❓ More); the sixth, **Village**, is the 👥 population chip that already opened that subject, so all six have a door without the bar carrying two glyphs for one thing. Each is **icon only** per the owner's second report, with the name in the `title` and the accessible name, and each is wired through the existing route owner (`sidebarTabForOverviewNav`) rather than a second list, so a subject cannot be reachable from `v`/`f`/`n`/`p`/`l`/`m` and missing from the header. **One owner for the subject's identity:** new `OVERVIEW_SUBJECTS` in `hooks/useGameShellState.ts` (icon, label, hint) is read by the header door *and* the window title, replacing the overlay's private `navTabs`/`titleByNav` pair — the same "a rule lives in its owner and a view renders it" rule the rest of this tree follows. The **six status cards now belong to the Village subject** (they count people, work, homes, life, food and mood) instead of sitting above every section, where one summary read as part of Frontier and Nature. The inspector is **no longer hidden** while an overview is open: that `invisible` was for a full-screen takeover, and a window with a backdrop does not need it — the map and the inspector stay visible around the window. **`SelectedBuildingPanel` de-stacked too**, on the owner's *"i dont want any panels over there i want each subject own window"*: its four `CollapsibleSection`s in the inspector column became an **index** (identity header plus one button per subject) with each subject in its own window — Overview, Workers (or Construction while it is being built), Actions and Advanced. The bodies are re-homed unchanged and still computed eagerly, exactly as `CollapsibleSection` received them, so the change moves *where* a subject renders, not when it is computed; `GameWindow`'s centred branch now owns the focus trap for both (`useModalFocus`), which the dashboard used to attach itself. **The header's height is measured, not eyeballed**, because the first version of the doors was the reported regression: five doors at chip size cost ~170 px and wrapped the bar onto a **third row** (owner: *"the header is now quite large 3rows"*). Compacted to one tight strip, with the bar's own gaps and padding reduced, it measures **52 px and one row** at 1600×900 where the first version measured **89 px and two rows** (and three on the owner's wider screen, whose dev-only auto-play status line is ~300 px of the difference). **Verified in a real browser, not by reading** — a purpose-built probe (`tmp/probe-subject-windows.mjs`, reusing the repo's CDP harness) boots the game along the real player path and asserts, per subject: the door is **inside `header.game-header`**, enabled and **carries no word**, clicking it opens `[role="dialog"]` named for that subject with `aria-modal="true"`, that box is **strictly smaller than the viewport with ≥8 px margins** (*"not full screen"* as a number: 928×227 to 928×681 inside 1600×900), the content is that subject's own, and Escape closes it; the 👥 chip opens the Village window with the roster in it. Result: **PASS, 0 console errors**, and the repo's own `scripts/autoplay-browser-probe.mjs` still passes on the live game after its capability was repointed (**9 bot acts, the family-tree window opens and closes through the new Village window, 0 console errors**). `tsc` clean on both projects and oxlint **0 warnings / 0 errors**. **Not done, and stated plainly:** the Village subject's window still contains `VillageTabPanel`'s seven collapsible sections — the tab-to-index sweep in `docs/plans/ux-one-window-per-subject-2026-09-29.md` is untouched for that panel — `ProgressTabPanel` (6) is untouched, and the building panel's index still lives in the right-hand column, which is the part of *"i dont want any panels over there"* that remains open.

- **Changed: the inspector column is a narrow rail until something is selected, and two audit contracts moved to the surface that replaced theirs** — owner, with a screenshot of the right column holding nothing but an open, empty `SIMULATION DIAGNOSTICS` drawer: *"now the right panel no need to be that big anymore"*. That drawer was the whole column when nothing was selected, so the column is now **`w-12` unless there is a selection** (the same narrowing the collapsed branch already did, Roadmap P6 / audit R40 — the map keeps the width) and `GameInspector` does not render the drawer without a selection, for the same reason it did not render it while collapsed: the drawer needs the column's width to lay out. **Two source-scanning guards failed on this change and both were ports, not weakenings:** `tests/uiRefinement.residuals.test.ts` R37 caught that deleting the overlay header took the mood **explanation** with the mood **label** (the audit removed the duplicate label, not the sentence), so `{overview.moodDetail}` now renders at the top of the Village window — its guard still fails if the sentence disappears; and `tests/keyboardGuards.contract.test.ts` A-6 (*"a selection strip states its selection instead of only painting it"*) pointed at the nav strip's `aria-current`, so it now asserts the header doors state the open subject with `aria-current={open}` — the same contract on the control that replaced the strip, since nothing about "state it, do not just paint it" changed. **Also fixed, because it caused a real failure in this session:** `scripts/autoplay-browser-probe.mjs` never deleted Chrome's per-run profile, measured at **27 leaked profile dirs / 2.5 GB**, which is what ended a later probe run with `ENOSPC`; it now removes its own scratch in its `finally`.

- **New: the Village overview is its own window with its own door in the header, instead of a full-screen takeover behind an unlabelled 📊** — owner: *"there is a villageoverview but its not wired … i want a new icon in the header to open it and not full screen"*. Two things were true at once and both were reported as one problem. **(1) The data was never the problem; the shell was.** `GameDashboard` was a `fixed inset-0` dialog that covered the entire game — its own title bar, its own `✕ Esc` control, its own `useModalFocus` trap — so "the village overview" was a screen you entered and left rather than a window you read beside the map. It now renders into **`GameWindow`**, the repository's one window shell (title bar, one close control, one scrollable body), centred over the shell's existing backdrop: measured on the running build it is **928×681 at (336,110) in a 1600×900 viewport** (margins 336/336/110/110), with the game's own header still on screen behind it and the map visible either side. **(2) The door was real but unreadable.** A control *did* exist and worked — `GameHeader.tsx`'s button has been wired to `setShowDashboard(true)` since 2026-09-21, and the repo's own browser checklist recorded `ph-dashboard-opens … pass` — but it was a bare **📊** with no label, sitting between the 👥 population chip and the resource badges, which reads as one more stat rather than a way in. It is now a **🏘️ door in the header bar**, icon only like every other control there (the owner's later ruling: *"just a icon not a name with it in the ehader"*), with the name in its `title` and its accessible name; it shows the same glyph the window's title bar shows, so the door and the window it opens are visibly one subject. **`GameWindow` gained an optional `widthClassName`**, and that is the load-bearing part rather than a convenience: the shell offered exactly two hardcoded widths — a 26 rem centred window and an 18 rem anchored one — so a subject carrying a five-column resource row, two SVG charts and a settler table had **no way to fit it**, and hand-rolling a full-screen dialog was the only route left open. Sizing belongs to the shell; the prop removes the reason to bypass it, and it replaces the window's `w-*` **and** its `max-w-*` together (a later `w-*` in the class attribute does not reliably win — Tailwind orders by stylesheet, not by markup), which the prop documents because getting that wrong is silent. **The focus contract moved with the chrome:** `useModalFocus` is now attached inside `GameWindow` for its centred (modal) variant rather than by the dashboard, because the element the trap must hold is the shell's own and is unreachable from outside its portal — **disclosed behaviour change:** `FamilyTreeWindow`, the other centred window, gains focus containment and focus restore it did not have before (it is a modal by construction, and the 2026-09-17 audit demanded exactly this for the overlays that predated it). The dashboard keeps the keyboard half itself (`useOverlayKeyboard('dashboard', onClose)`): Escape is *not* the shell's contract, since an anchored window beside a panel is not dismissed that way. **Verified in a real browser, not by reading** — a purpose-built probe (`tmp/probe-village-window.mjs`, reusing the repo's CDP harness) boots the game along the real player path and asserts: the control is **inside `header.game-header`**, enabled and labelled `"🏘️Village"`; clicking it opens `[role="dialog"][aria-label="Village overview"]` with `aria-modal="true"`; that box is **strictly smaller than the viewport on both axes with ≥8 px margins** (the assertion for "not full screen"); the concerns, daily council and food sections are really rendered; Escape closes it; the same door reopens it; the ✕ closes it; **0 console errors**. The sibling window was regression-checked too (`node scripts/autoplay-browser-probe.mjs`): the family tree still opens and closes, 9 bot acts, 0 console errors. `tsc` clean on the app project and oxlint **0 warnings, 0 errors** on the three files. **Two errors were then fixed on the owner's ruling** (*"doesnt matter if your fault fix t he error"*), because the first was already visible in this change's own verification: `npm run test:types` failed on a duplicated object key in `tests/familyTree.test.ts` (`TS1117`, the same `isJuvenile: true` written twice — a pre-existing defect from the previous session, first recorded as `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-30-familytree-fixture-duplicate-key.md` and left alone per AGENTS.md §6), and **the one real test failure** was `tests/foodLedger.acceptedCatch.test.ts`, whose fixture replaces `state.entities` wholesale and therefore left the Hunting Spot with nobody on its crew — the diagnosed cause already in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-30-hunting-yield-unbounded.md` (*"a FIXTURE problem, not an engine one"*). One duplicate line deleted, and one `rebuildEntityByIdMap(state)` added after the wholesale replacement (`entityIndex.ts` documents it for exactly this caller: *"Full rebuild from alive entities — load recovery, init, and tests only"*). **The production guard was not touched** — the no-hunter rule the previous session added is the owner's own rule and stands; the fixture is now genuinely staffed, as that report required. Verified: `tsc` exit 0 on both projects, and **`npm run test:standard` green at 245 files / 1493 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed**, with `npm run build` exit 0. One neighbouring fixture defect is recorded rather than fixed: the same file gives its deer `PREY_ID = 2`, colliding with the crew ids `1..8`, so the engine's invariants print `tick 144: duplicate entity id 2`; changing the id re-keys that fixture's seeded shot roll, so it is its own change.

- **Fixed: the Hunting Spot no longer shoots across the map — the assigned hunter does the hunting, from where the hunter actually is** — owner: *"the hunung spot cannot hunt its just a spot from where they hunt but they free to walk the people who work there they just should hunt its not a tower that can fire"*, then *"the range can be 350 where is looking but to kil it you have stand ne xt to it"*, after reporting *"they shoot all over the map they should be near a animal"*. The branch picked its target by distance to **`building.x + width/2`** with a hardcoded **320 px** radius and fired on a flat 85 % roll **with no distance term at all**, so the structure killed whatever wandered near it no matter where its hunter stood — and the old code only used the hunter to *draw* the arrow, hunting anyway when nobody was assigned. Targeting is now anchored on the **assigned hunter's position**, "looking" is the owner's **350 px**, and the shot needs the hunter within **120 px** of the animal. A distinct *"Too far to shoot"* message separates "nothing out there" from "the animal is over there and nobody walked to it". `findLiveAssignedWorker` also no longer throws when a building has no `occupants`. **Known limitation:** the kill reach is 120 rather than true adjacency, because prey wanders — measured directly, a fixture deer drifted from 30 px to **34.5 px** on the production tick against a 22 px adjacency reach, which stopped the spot producing food entirely. A strict "stand next to it" needs the hunter to **walk to the prey**, and no such pursuit exists yet; that is the remaining fix (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-30-hunting-yield-unbounded.md`).

- **Fixed: a settler must be 6 before school means anything — a newborn was eligible for a school shift** — owner: *"well stages its not logic if a zero year old go to school"*, proposing `0-2 baby / 2-6 toddler / 6-12 child / 12-18 juvenile`. School had **no minimum age**: `humanTick` gated the school run on `entity.isJuvenile` alone, and that flag is `age < HUMAN_CHILDHOOD_DAYS` (12) — true from birth — so a 0-year-old could be sent to school whenever one was in range. New `SCHOOL_MIN_AGE = 6` (the owner's own number for the start of the "child" band). Deliberately **not** a new global age ladder: the simulation's bands stay as they are, and this is one gate on one activity. The other three stages remain names rather than mechanics, pending a decision on what each should *do*.

- **Fixed: the family tree called a 16-year-old an "Adult child", and the parents had no age** — owner: *"16 years it not an adult? and why no age at the paretns"*. `childRelation` classified by `isJuvenile`, which flips at `HUMAN_CHILDHOOD_DAYS` (**12**, the graduation age) while the documented adult floor is `HUMAN_ADULT_MIN_AGE` (**18**) — so every 13–17-year-old was labelled an adult. It now compares the actual age against the adult floor, so the age ladder has one owner and the tree asks it rather than inferring from a flag with a different meaning. Separately, `buildFamilyTree` passed a `detail` string for **children only** (age, plus the other parent when it differs), so parents and the spouse rendered as bare names while the rows below them carried `16y`; every relative now shows their age. This is a **class** of bug, not one instance — any other code using `isJuvenile` as a stand-in for "adult" has the same 12-vs-18 mismatch and has not been audited.

- **Fixed: 16 building types no longer offer an upgrade that does nothing — a Road can no longer charge you 50w/25s/50g for a level nothing reads** — asked from play as *"what does updating a road do > so upgrading to level 2"*, then *"so why is it possible to upgrade?"* and *"well why do i see upgrade at an road then if does nothign"*. The answer was that `getBuildingUpgradeEligibility` gated upgrades on **state** (not finished / already level 3 / the Leader's House) and never asked whether the *type* gained anything from a level, so the button rendered for every completed building and the resources were really spent. Audited with `tmp/level-effect-audit.mts` against what actually reads `building.level`: **3 residences** gain capacity (`getResidenceCapacity`, +2 per level), **19 job buildings** gain output (`dailyBuildingEconomy` and `workshopEconomy` multiply by `building.level || 1`), and **16 types are read by nothing at all** — road, wall, wallGate, bridge, well, barn, silo, woodStorehouse, mill, tamingPost, watchtower, wildlifePreserve, garden, statue, lamp, fence. The new `buildingTypeHasLevelBenefit(type)` is *derived* from those two readers rather than hand-listed, so a new producing building becomes upgradeable automatically and the predicate cannot drift from the multipliers. Per the owner's ruling the dead types now behave exactly like a **maxed** building — `upgradePossible` is false, so `SelectedBuildingPanel` renders neither the button nor the warning and the building simply reads as complete. (`tsc` clean, oxlint 0, full suite 245 files / 1493 passed.)

- **Fixed: the settler home/workplace map rings were drawn half a footprint up and to the left of the building** — owner: *"why is there a offset the square"*, pointing at a blue dashed box sitting up-left of a church. `getBuildingScreenRect` returns **centre-x / bottom-y**, not a centre — it is the anchor the sprite is drawn from so the art rises off the footprint base, a convention both `buildPreview.ts:23` and `grid.ts:331` record. The map-ring code was the one caller that ignored it, subtracting `w/2, h/2` as if it were a centre. The ring is now built from the base: horizontally centred on `sx`, extending **upward** from `sy`. This was the "selecting a settler rings their home (sky-blue) and workplace (amber)" feature from the previous session's handover, which had never been looked at in a browser.

- **Fixed: "Civilization Builder" / "Species role" is gone from the citizen card — the block is deleted, not renamed again** — reported from play twice (*"why is she a civliazation builder then"*, then *"why stil civliazian builder"*). The first pass relabelled it: `Role` → `Species role`, moved under a `Food chain · humans` heading. That was a wording fix for a surface problem, so the second report was inevitable. The owner gave the decisive reason when confirming the deletion: *"work is already defined lower in the civ viewer and civilationd builder is nothign"* — the line was **redundant and empty at the same time**. Redundant, because this settler's work is already shown further down the same card as `💼 occupation` plus the job and skill lines. Empty, because `ecology.role` is the **species'** trophic role, a constant (`human: { role: 'Civilization Builder' }`), printed on an **individual settler**, so it read as that person's job while saying nothing about them — every citizen, a newborn included, was a Civilization Builder. The whole block (`Species role` / `Eats` / `Hunted`, and the `foodChainInfo` table that existed only to feed it) is now **removed**. `tsc` clean, oxlint 0 warnings, and a repo-wide search confirms the string survives only in the comment recording its removal.

- **Fixed: the family-tree button existed only for settlers who already had relatives, so for most of a young colony it was not there at all** — reported from play as *"it doesnt work when i cick on famliy three"*. The inspector rendered its `Family tree ↗` door behind `tree.parents.length + tree.siblings.length + tree.children.length > 0 || tree.partner != null` (`SelectedEntityPanel.tsx:569`), so a settler with no recorded parent, sibling, child or partner — which is most of a freshly-founded village — showed **no control**, and the feature read as broken rather than empty. Worse, `FamilyTreeWindow` already had an explicit empty state (*"No living relatives recorded"*) that the condition made **unreachable**. The door is now always rendered and labels the case on the right (`no relatives recorded`), so the window says it instead of the button silently vanishing. **Verified in a real browser** through the canonical bot, which now drives the whole chain: roster → `Locate <name> on the map` → inspector → the tree. Result: `opened: "Family tree of Jere" close="Close Family tree of Jere"`, `closed / gone: {"ok":true} / true`, 0 console errors. Two tooling fixes were needed to get there and are worth recording: every earlier selector attempt *guessed* at the DOM and failed, while the working one was read from the source (`PopulationPanel.tsx:57`); and matching the opener by its visible text hit the *collapsible section header* `🌳Family tree▾` instead of the button, so it now matches the button's `title`.

- **Fixed: the "Family tree" was not a family tree — it was an alphabetical surname list, and it was open by default** — reported from play as *"family three is not a family thee"*, and the screenshot confirms it: the Village tab rendered a `defaultOpen` `CollapsibleSection` titled **Family tree** whose contents were families as flat groups — `Musson` with 2 adults, `Mace` with 2 adults, `Wing`, `Andersen`, `Beeler`… one row per surname. It was both **mislabelled** (a list of surnames is not a tree) and the worst instance of the stacking the owner rejected in the same breath (*"i dont want any thing stacked"*), because being open by default it dominated the tab. It is **deleted**, with the reason recorded where it stood so nobody re-adds a list under that name. The real tree (`FamilyTreeWindow`, opened from a selected settler) now **draws** one: parents across the top, the settler with their `⚭` spouse as the anchor row, siblings hanging off the same parents, children below each on its own stem with a connector — so descent is visible rather than inferred from section headings. That is the difference between the earlier "grouped by generation" pass and this one: grouping is still a list of groups, and a tree needs the line.

- **New: `GameWindow`, the one window shell every subject renders into, and the work & venue hours editors move into their own window** — owner ruling: *"each subject should just have its own window not stacking up"*. `components/GameWindow.tsx` is the primitive — title bar, one close control, one scrollable body — and owns **no** subject logic, so adding a window is a small change rather than a new layout. `FamilyTreeWindow` was refactored onto it (it had hand-rolled its own chrome, so the shell did not exist yet), and the three schedule editors that were buried inside `CitizenOverviewScreen` now live in `components/WorkHoursWindow.tsx`, opened from the Village section by an **Edit hours ↗** button. The editors themselves are unchanged owner-owned panels, so the Apply button behaves exactly as before; routing them into the shell is the whole change. **Deliberately not draggable yet**, and stated in the component: the plan lists draggable/resizable as part of the full shell, there is no drag or resize helper anywhere in this repository, and that is new interaction work rather than a refactor — every window is anchored top-right until it lands.

- **Changed: the work and venue schedules have no length restriction — the owner sets how long the colony works and how long the Tavern and Hotel stay open, and a long window is staffed rather than refused** — reported from play as *"i cannot change work times"* and *"i choose how long the tavern should be open"*. The panel was not broken: a 04:00–23:00 (19 h) Tavern was being **refused** by `MAX_VENUE_SERVICE_HOURS = 18`, and the ordinary work window carried a 2–16 h band. Measured on the owner's own screenshot, the status line read *"Blocked: Venue service cannot exceed 18 hours"* with the Apply button correctly disabled — a bound, not a bug. `MAX_VENUE_SERVICE_HOURS` and `MIN_VENUE_SERVICE_HOURS` are deleted, and so are `MIN_STANDARD_WORK_HOURS` (2) and `MAX_STANDARD_WORK_HOURS` (16); the only width limit left is the long-standing **no-wrapping-past-midnight** rule, so 1 hour is the narrowest window and 23 hours (00:00–23:00) the widest. Verified in a real browser, not by reading: the ordinary work panel accepts **00:00–23:00 (23 h)**, enables Apply, commits it, and the current line reads `00:00–23:00 (23h)`; the Tavern accepts **04:00–23:00** and commits it. **The staffing rule is unchanged and is the point of removing the cap** (owner: *"if its goes over 8 hrrs a extra person should go work at the place"*, clarified *"well 9 hrs also fin"*): `getVenueAutoStaffingTarget` keeps `ceil(hours / 9)` bounded by the building's own crew cap, so 1–9 h is one settler, 10–18 h two and 19 h+ three. A venue open all day is covered by more staff, never refused.

- **Fixed: the normal work day is 9 hours, not 8 — a colony working its own standard day was charged an hour of overtime every single day** — `scheduleFatigue.NEUTRAL_WORK_HOURS` was **8** while `workSchedule.STANDARD_PRODUCTION_WORK_HOURS` was **9**, so the game's own two halves disagreed about what a day is: a settler doing a standard 9-hour shift accrued `FATIGUE_PER_EXCESS_HOUR` (10) of fatigue for it and recovered nothing. `getScheduleTargetHours` caps its target at that constant, which is why the whole system shifted at once when it was corrected. The owner's rule is explicit (*"normal work day is 9 hrs"*), and the correction is self-evidencing: with `NEUTRAL_WORK_HOURS = 9` the existing case *"emits one settlement summary for multiple meaningful work-fatigue increases"* — two settlers on a 12-hour shift — now produces **36** (`30 excess − 4 base`), which is exactly the value the test already asserted and which the 8-hour constant was silently defeating. Tests updated with the arithmetic recorded: a neutral nine-hour day recovers `30 − 4 = 26`, one hour of overtime lands on `0 + 10 − 4 = 6`, and a zero-hour day recovers `80 − (4 + 9×3) = 49`.

- **Fixed: production is paid for by settlers who actually turn up — output scaled with the *configured* work window while a settler asleep in bed counted as working** — reported from play as *"is work hours related to production at this moment and i mean the person shoud eb really at the work palce"*, and the report was right on both halves. **(1)** `dailyBuildingEconomy` and `workshopEconomy` both derived their multiplier from `getWorkScheduleHours(getWorkSchedule(state))` — the *configured window*, not work done — so setting a 23-hour day multiplied every building's output by **2.56×** without anyone being at work. **(2)** The only presence test anywhere in the production path was `staffed = workers > 0`, and `workers` came from `buildingWorkerStats`, keyed on `worker.homeBuildingId` — an **assignment**. Nothing read an entity's position, so a farm produced its full `12 + workers × 5` while its worker slept at home. Attendance is now measured and shared: `resolveDailyScheduleFatigue` snapshots the hours each settler actually worked into `scheduleLastWorkedHours` **before** zeroing the tick accumulator (it runs at `tickLayerDaily:42`, ahead of the economy at `:81`, so without the snapshot the only record of attendance was destroyed before production could read it), and one exported rule — `getWorkplacePresenceShare(workedHours, assigned, scheduleHours, hasAttendance)` — is applied by real production **and** by the workshop estimate, so a preview cannot drift from output. A crew that works its whole window scores **1** and keeps exactly the output the window alone used to give; a crew that never turns up produces **0**; an unmeasured world (a fresh load, before the first day boundary) reports 1 rather than 0, because "not yet measured" must not read as "nobody ever works".

- **Changed: a settler leaves early enough to be AT work when the shift starts, instead of starting to walk then** — owner: *"they should be AT work when working tim start not begining with walking"*. The allowance was a fixed `WORK_COMMUTE_LEAD_HOURS = 1` for everyone, which cannot be met by a long leg, and `COMMUTE_SNAP_DISTANCE` only teleports commutes under 200 units — so anyone further out simply arrived late. `simulation/humanMovement.commuteLeadHoursFor(distance, walkSpeed)` now derives each settler's own lead from the movement owner's numbers (base `speed`, `distRush`, `PATH_STEER_SPEED_RATIO`), so it cannot drift from how the walk behaves, and it is clamped to **1–6 hours**: the floor makes a short commute bit-identical to before, and the ceiling stops a settler being scheduled to leave the night before. **It is an estimate with a floor and a ceiling, not an arrival guarantee** — past `MAX_COMMUTE_LEAD_HOURS` a very long leg cannot be walked in the time available, and the existing shift-start snap is what still puts that settler at their post. Both facts are recorded in the function's doc because the first version of this conflated them.

- **Fixed: people get into and out of prison visibly — an imprisonment is its own event type with its own Chronicle filter** — reported from play as *"i cant seee if people get in prison its not happneing or its not logged and not shown"*. `arrestForScandal` logged `'event'`, so imprisonments landed in the generic Events bucket (1 531 of the owner's 2 000 Chronicle entries) with nothing to group them. There is now a real `'prison'` type on `GameEventLog`, a **Imprisonments (jailed for scandal)** filter, a ⛓️ icon and colour, and — the part that made it invisible even when logged — a `Jails` count in the daily report, which previously showed neither scandals-adjacent number. **The tempting one-line fix (`'event'` → `'scandal'`) was deliberately not taken:** `rumourLedger.SOURCE_KINDS` reads `type === 'scandal'`, so it would have pulled imprisonments into the rumour ledger and inflated the scandal count. The compiler found two more sites by itself — `EventLogPanel`'s `Record<GameEventLog['type'], string>` maps for icons and colours errored immediately, naming the missing member. **A stale test was the reason this class of defect could hide:** `tests/eventLogPanel.filters.test.ts` hand-typed its list of event types and had already fallen behind (`conception` and `divorce` were absent), so the test guarding "every event type has a filter" could not have caught a missing one. It is now keyed as a `Record<GameEventLog['type'], true>`, which turns a new event type into a **compile error** there rather than a silent gap.

- **Fixed: the dev server no longer full-reloads the page from Rust build output, so the game can actually be opened and driven in a browser** — `vite.config.ts` watched everything under the project root, including `src-tauri/target/doc/**`, which `cargo doc` fills with **thousands** of generated HTML files; Vite issued a full-page reload per file, so the page never settled. This is why the first browser run of the session could not reach the game at all. `server.watch.ignored` now excludes `**/src-tauri/target/**`. Nothing under `target/` is ever imported by the app.

- **New: a separate testing bot that raises the whole building catalogue — and it proves a settler is physically at the church, not merely assigned to it** — owner: *"give the bot just options to build all things so cheating but fo rtesting"* and *"keep virtual-player.ts apart thats for in game and use own for testing"*. `scripts/test-bot-build-all.mts` drives the real command boundary and **closes a recorded coverage gap** (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-29-automated-runs-never-build-a-church.md`): no automated run in this repository had ever reached `churchStrength > 0`. Result: **21 of 34 buildable types raised, the world still simulates, and the church is live at strength 1.** Two traps were found by running it, both recorded in the script: **(1)** `buildDebug` places a *construction site*, and `getChurchStrength` is gated on `b.completed` — so the first run reported "church built: true, churchStrength: 0"; **(2)** `countWorkersAtBuilding` counts `homeBuildingId`, i.e. an **assignment, not an arrival** (the same assignment-vs-attendance distinction as the production fix above), so the bot now ticks until the keeper is **physically at the church** before it claims success — owner: *"no it need people workin there"*. Two opt-in primitives support it and are unreachable from any shipping path: a `buildDebug` worker command and `startBuilding(..., free)`, which skip **research and cost only** — terrain, overlap and one-per-village placement are still enforced by their owner, because two buildings in one footprint would corrupt the spatial indices rather than merely cheat. `scripts/autoplay-browser-probe.mjs` also gains a reusable `CAPABILITIES` registry, so the UI-driving bot gains named handles onto the game's screens instead of each test re-deriving selectors.

- **Investigated and deliberately reverted: the in-game bot does not build a Church, and the reason is its own pinned ordering** — an attempt to extend the shipping auto-play ladder (owner: *"do extend its building options so let it build a church and other things"*) failed **twice**, at the civic step (**36 of 68** cases in `tests/virtualPlayer.test.ts`) and at the end of the ladder (**15 of 68**), and pre-placing a church in the shared fixture made it *worse* (**18**) because the Church is itself a manual-staff workplace. The cause is not a bug: those 68 cases pin the exact decision sequence and treat `null` ("the colony needs nothing") as a reachable healthy state, and any step that fires in a small, well-stocked fixture contradicts 15 terminal expectations. Extending the in-game repertoire is therefore a redesign of the bot's identity — **does a healthy colony keep building?** — and that is an owner decision, not an edit. The ladder is left exactly as it was and `virtualPlayer.test.ts` is green at 68/68; the church coverage gap is closed by the separate testing bot above instead.

**Verification for this entry:** `npm run test:all` green at **245 files / 1493 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, with **0 lint warnings and 0 errors** over 350 files, and `tsc` clean on both the app and test projects. The two schedule panels and the new window were driven in a real browser (the repo has no DOM test tier): ordinary work accepted and committed `00:00–23:00`, the Tavern accepted `04:00–23:00`, the Hotel `06:00–21:00`, the work-hours window opens and closes from its button, and the console was quiet throughout (`0 console errors`). The canonical browser bot still passes on the live game after the UI change (9 acts, farm before research, status line visible). **Not verified, stated plainly:** the walk-speed and `TICKS_PER_HOUR` rebase discussed in this session was **not** implemented — measured, the map is one settlement site (~102 m across at 11.76 px/m), a crossing currently costs **23 ticks / 7.7 in-game hours**, and the owner's chosen target (one day to cross at `TICKS_PER_HOUR = 5`) requires walk speed ≈ **14 px/tick with `distRush` deleted**, which rebases every duration in the game and is left as its own reviewed change. Footpaths, the night-hiding residual (~38 % still drawn), and the browser pass over the family-tree window and settler map rings also remain open.

## <u>[0.6.5]</u> — 2026-09-24

- **Fixed: the People screen reports the year's affairs instead of an instant that averages zero — 93 affairs a year were reading as "0"** — reported from play as *"in game there are zero affairs with 400 citizins"*. The engine gate says otherwise: `npm run test:full-year` (seed 12345, 360 days, ~70 settlers) establishes **93** affairs, catches **62** in the act and jails **9** for it, with **12** gossip exposures. What was wrong is the number the player reads. The Life card's `affairs` slot counted settlers holding `affairPartnerId` **at that instant**, and an affair is deliberately short-lived: measured on the same gate, the instantaneous count averages **0.2 per day** and is non-zero on only **35 of 360** days of a year with 93 establishments — so the card read zero on ~90 % of days, including every day the owner happened to look, and the feature looked absent while working. The card now reads `${married} married · ${N} sweethearts · ${N} affairs this year · ${N} jailed`, where the year count comes from the event log the Chronicle already reads, filtered to the current colony year. The establishment phrase is now **one exported constant on its owner** (`AFFAIR_ESTABLISHED_LOG_PHRASE`, `humanRelationships.ts`) used by both the writer and this reader — a second copy of the string would have made the count silently read 0 forever if the two ever drifted. **A correction to my own earlier report, recorded because it reversed the conclusion:** I first measured this with a hand-built 400-citizen probe that pushed settlers straight into `world.entities`, bypassing the registration the immigration path performs. That fixture reported "0 live affairs, 0 exposures, every pair cleared as NOT MUTUAL" and I reported the drama chain as still dead — none of it was the game. The same measurement on a world built only by `prepareColonyWorld` + `gameTick` shows 38 establishments, 72 exposures, 33 catches in a year, and **zero** non-mutual clears. The engine gate is the authority, not an improvised probe. Verification: `tests/citizenOverview.affairsThisYear.test.ts` (2) pins the two ways the count can lie — another year's affairs, and a scandal line that is not an establishment (a rumour or a catch) — beside the existing youth-love case; `tsc` app+test clean, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, full standard suite green (243 files / 1,479 passed, 2 skipped).

- **Fixed: an affair can be established again — exposure fired at 45 % while establishment needs 100, and a rumour *reset* the progress it interrupted, so in a village with a church no affair ever completed and the entire drama chain downstream of establishment was dead** — reported from play as *"things are not balanced and some are totally missing like affairs"*, and the owner's own save is the proof rather than a model of it: New Frontier (v0.6.5.0, Y0 D198–D279) logged **145 "Whispers spread" and 0 "began a secret affair"**, and with it **0 "was caught with"**, **0 "imprisoned for scandal"** and **0 feuds**. **The cause is an ordering inversion, not a tuning value.** `tryDailyAffairEncounter` establishes a pair only when *both* partners reach `AFFAIR_PROGRESS_MAX` (100) — the bump is 16–27 per tryst at a 0.07/day roll — and `tryDailyAffairGossip` exposed a pair once both passed **45** (church) or **85** (no church). Because `exposeAffair` calls `clearAffairPair`, which sets *both* partners' `affairProgress` back to **0**, a rumour did not merely embarrass a pair mid-climb: it undid the climb. Exposure almost always won, and every gate below it keys off `hasAffairPartner` — which only establishment sets. So `tryExposeCaughtAffairForPair` (both call sites), `arrestForScandal`, the "imprisoned for scandal" line, `tryDivorceOnCaughtCheater(…, caughtInAct = true)` and the **only `startFeud` caller in the game** were all unreachable, and had been since the 2026-09 rebalance that halved the tryst chance (0.14 → 0.07, 0.20 → 0.10). **The fix restores the rule the code already claimed to follow.** `gameConstants.ts` says *"only establishment can produce a scandal"* and `tests/affair.cadence.test.ts` says *"exposure requires an established affair"* — both were false. `tryDailyAffairGossip` now requires `hasAffairPartner` before it rolls at all, and `findAffairLover` resolves only an established mutual lover (its 45-progress scan is deleted with the floors it served). The church branch keeps its own roll and salt (`602`, 0.12/0.22; no-church `601`, 0.06), so seeding a run does not move which days already gossiped; the church still suppresses affairs through `AFFAIR_CHURCH_FLOOR_FACTOR`, which scales the daily tryst chance — what it no longer does is make the drama impossible. **Measured by A/B on the real engine** — the same seeded colony, the same 360 days of shipped `gameTick`, with a completed staffed church added as the only difference between the two columns, and the whole run repeated against the pre-fix source:

| | before, no church | before, **church** | after, no church | after, **church** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| population | 103 | 144 | 130 | 104 |
| `affairsEstablished` | 50 | **17** | 130 | **81** |
| `"Whispers spread"` | 291 | **414** | 10 | **21** |
| `"was caught with"` | 38 | 8 | 105 | 49 |
| `"imprisoned for scandal"` | 29 | 6 | 65 | 35 |
| `"A feud is brewing"` | 36 | 8 | 97 | 44 |
| forced-divorce lines | 71 | 16 | 190 | 82 |
| **rumours per establishment** | 5.82 | **24.35** | 0.08 | **0.26** |

**Why this run is housed and the first one was not.** The first A/B used the shipped `prepareColonyWorld`, which puts 8 houses under 21–70 people, so most settlers were homeless — and a homeless settler passes the encounter's gates *for the wrong reason*, because `isAtMaritalHome` is false when you have no home. **Housing is the variable that decides this bug**, so the numbers above place **60 completed houses** first: the same seed, the same 360 days, measured on `gameTick` with the whole drama chain counted. The church column is the owner's configuration and the difference is 24.35 → **0.26** rumours per establishment, with establishments **17 → 81**, caught **8 → 49**, imprisoned **6 → 35** and feuds **8 → 44**.

**Three measurement errors in my own probes on the way here, all recorded because each produced a false reading:** (1) the first harvested only `type === 'scandal'`, while `"X was imprisoned for scandal"` is logged as `'event'` (`arrestForScandal`); (2) its "staff the church" step took the first adult founder, who is exactly the starter **Prison Guard** `prepareColonyWorld` assigns, so the church arm had no guard and `arrestForScandal` could never fire; (3) it was under-housed, which is the whole regime. And the conclusion the owner's own question forced out: **the church never blocked imprisonment — nothing was ever caught, because nothing ever established.**

**The second half, and the one that actually reaches a housed village — the encounter was evaluated at midnight for the whole settlement at once.** With the gossip gate fixed the owner would still have seen nothing, and asking *"why not just run it more often"* is what surfaced it. `tryDailyAffairEncounter` is the **only** writer of `affairPartnerId` (`tests/affair.cadence.test.ts`: *"the daily owner is the sole establisher"*), and it ran on the global `isNewCalendarDayTick` gate — `getTickOfDay(tick) === 0`, i.e. **00:00 for everyone**. At midnight it cannot pass its own gates: `canPursueSecretAffair` returns false on `isSpouseNearby` (22 px, `humanRelationships.ts`), which is true for a housed couple asleep in the same room, and `isAtMaritalHome` is true besides. So in a well-housed village the one daily sample was taken at the one hour it was guaranteed to fail — which is exactly the owner's objection, *"all affairs at 00:00 is not logical"* / *"people sleep between 23:00 and 06:00"*. **The fix: stagger it.** Each settler's encounter is now evaluated at their own hour across the **waking day** (`affairEncounterHourOfDay(entityId)`, 06:00–19:00 derived from the owner's existing `NIGHT_END`/`NIGHT_START`), once per settler per day. **It costs nothing in probability, which is why staggering is the whole fix:** `personDayRoll` (`humanSchedule.ts`) hashes `(entity, colony day, salt)` and **not** the tick-of-day, so a settler evaluated once a day at their own hour gets *exactly the same single roll at exactly the same chance* — only the hour the conditions are sampled at changes. No latch, no new save field, and `decisionRegistry` already declares this cadence as **"staggered/daily"**: the implementation was the part that was not staggered. (The alternative the owner offered — have cheaters skip sleep and sneak out at night — would work too, but it means changing the sleep/idle schedule, a far larger surface than a call site.)

The church column is the owner's situation, and it is the inversion in one line: before, a housed church village produced **24.35 rumours for every affair that managed to complete**; after, **0.26**. **Verification:** the A/B above is the substantive evidence — it is the shipped `gameTick`, and the "before" arm is produced by restoring `humanRelationships.ts` from HEAD, so the difference is the change and nothing else. `tsc` app+test projects clean; `npm run lint` **0 warnings / 0 errors**; **full standard suite 243 files / 1479 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**; `tests/fullYear.integration.test.ts` green (360 days / 25,920 ticks, no invariant violations). **Not verified, and deliberately not faked:** there is no automated regression test for this. Two attempts were made and both were deleted rather than shipped — a `tryDailyAffairGossip` fixture test (it hand-set `affairProgress = 95`, i.e. exactly the manufactured precondition this entry criticises) and a bot-played integration test (it hand-placed the world and took **214 s** for one assertion, far past the standard tier, and it failed because the bot never builds a church). **Also removed, at the owner's instruction:** `tests/affairCaught.reachability.test.ts`, whose header claimed it was *"the reachability regression guard"* for this exact chain while its fixtures **hand-set `affairPartnerId` and `affairProgress = 60`** — it assumed the establishment that was unreachable, so it stayed green for as long as no affair could establish. It was **untracked** (`tests/**` is gitignored), so a verbatim copy is preserved at `tmp/deleted-tests/affairCaught.reachability.test.ts` for recovery. **A Church was briefly added to the virtual player's civic build ladder and reverted:** because step 6 runs before roads, militia, diplomacy and refugee screening, a church the colony can afford (45 wood / 35 stone / 20 gold) shadowed every later decision and broke **36 of the 68** cases in `tests/virtualPlayer.test.ts`. The coverage gap it was meant to close is real and stays recorded instead — see below. **The blindness that let this ship is recorded separately** (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-29-automated-runs-never-build-a-church.md`): the only year-long gate, `scripts/run-full-year.mts`, never builds anything — **18 buildings at day 30 and still 18 at day 360**, because `prepareColonyWorld` places a fixed completed skeleton (8 Houses + Tavern + Prison + 8 storage) and the harness never issues a construction command. It therefore runs at `churchStrength` **0** forever, and the in-repo bot never builds one either (`virtualPlayer.ts`: *"(Church, Prison, Barracks, School, Town Hall) — those stay the player's call"*; `CIVIC_BUILD_ORDER` is TownHall + Blacksmith). A church is precisely what selects the branch that killed establishment, so no automated run in this repository could have seen it.

- **Fixed: the Watchtower never draws rotated, and is no longer offered a rotation — a 90° turn laid the tower on its side** — reported from play as *"the watchtower cannot be rotated"*, and the accurate version is that it *was* rotated, wrongly. `BuildingRotation` is `0 | 90` and a 90° turn is applied by **rotating the sprite** (`spriteDrawing.drawSpriteFrame` → `ctx.rotate(Math.PI / 2)`), which is the correct meaning for a flat strip — the run axis, horizontal versus vertical — and the wrong one for a building drawn with height: a Watchtower at 90 was drawn tipped into the ground. One type was doing two unrelated jobs. Three changes, one rule. **(1)** `Watchtower` leaves `ROTATABLE`, so the rotate key does nothing for it. **(2)** A new owner function, `buildingRotation.effectiveBuildingRotation(type, rotation)`, returns the stored rotation only for types the player may rotate and **0 otherwise** — which is why this is not just a menu change: selecting a build type resets `buildRotation` to 0 (`App.tsx`), so the only remaining source of a rotated tower is an **older save**, and that save must not render one into the ground either. **(3)** Both draw sites (the completed pass and the construction pass) now read that one function, so they cannot disagree, and the `flipX` argument that used to mirror the already-sideways tower is **deleted** with the rotation it compensated for. `Bridge` is still in the rotatable set and is not a strip type either, so it has the same defect; left untouched because it was not part of this request. **A pre-existing test asserted the opposite** — `tests/watchtowerDetection.test.ts` had *"Watchtower is rotatable with the R key"* expecting `true` — so that case was **rewritten, not deleted**, to pin the reversed ruling with the reason, rather than leaving two definitions of the same rule. Verification: `tests/watchtowerDetection.test.ts` (4) passes, and the rotation-adjacent files `visualsAudit.renderWiring` / `buildingFootprint.convention` / `footprintBounds.owner` pass (35 across the four). `tsc` app+test projects clean, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, full standard suite green (243 files / 1,486 passed, 2 skipped).

- **Fixed: a wall run is a straight wall again — the diagonal isometric face was being tiled along an axis it cannot fill** — reported from play as *"you dont build walls like this"*, with a wall rendering as a sawtooth of offset slabs. The cause is projection, and it is provable rather than a matter of taste: the world→screen transform is a plain scale+translate with **no isometric skew** (`viewState.worldToScreen`: `screenX = (x − cam.x) × zoom + cw/2`, same for y), so a horizontal run is a horizontal screen line; `wall_isometric.png` is a **45° face**; and `drawProceduralWall` drew that one asset **once per piece** at rotation 0 for every piece in the run (`drawIsometricDefenseAsset`, which also forced `imageSmoothingEnabled = false` and a square `max(w,h) × 1.18` box with no footprint anchor). Every piece therefore drew the same diagonal band at the same rotation, offset by exactly the strip pitch — a periodic sawtooth by construction, not by tuning. Walls now draw through the palisade path that was already written as the asset's fallback, and which was always axis-correct: `beginRotatedStripFrame` rotates the **frame**, not the art, and its width is `max(w,h)` = 60 = the strip pitch, so consecutive pieces abut exactly and the posts rise off the footprint (height preserved — a flat top-down wall would be indistinguishable from `road.png`). **No corner pieces are involved:** a corner is an H run and a V run overlapping, and the wall tool never had a corner type — `drawProceduralWallCorner` / `drawProceduralWallJunction` are exported but have **no caller** (left untouched as pre-existing dead code and reported here). The two isometric PNGs stay as the build-menu icons in `BUILDING_CONFIGS`; only the map drawing changed. Verification: `tsc` app+test projects clean, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, full standard suite green (243 files / 1,485 passed, 2 skipped). **Not verified by me:** the rendered pixels — the test runner has no canvas, so this is a geometry proof plus a green suite, and the visual result needs a human look.

- **New: sweethearts are finally visible — youth love gets a panel line and a People-screen counter, and an affair is no longer invisible while it is still building** — reported from play as *"i played now one year there are zero affairs and zero youth love — is youth love registered?"*. It is registered, and that was the problem: `advanceYouthLove` (`simulation/humanRelationships.ts`, daily from `tickLayerDaily`) writes `youthLovePartnerId` / `youthLoveProgress`, carries them through the save, `simDelta`'s catalog keys and the invariant collector, and logs *"X and Y became sweethearts"* — but **no `.tsx` file in the tree referenced `youthLove` at all**. No panel line, no counter, no map badge: the only in-game trace was one generic 🌝 log entry, while affairs get a notification banner. So the People screen's `0 sweethearts` was not a reading, it was the absence of one. **Now:** `computeCitizenOverview` gains a `youthLove` count (one half only, the `id < partnerId` convention `affairs` and `activeYouthLovePairs` use), the Life card reads `N married · N sweethearts · N affairs · N jailed`, and clicking a youth shows `💗 Sweethearts with <name> (N%)`. **And the same invisibility had a second case, reported as *"what about affair"*:** `affairProgress` accumulates over several trysts (16–27 each) *before* `affairPartnerId` is set at 100, and the panel only read the established id — so a settler at 90 % of an affair displayed nothing. It now reads `💋 Secret affair brewing (N%)` until the pair is established, then the existing named line takes over. **Why the counts read zero is a separate question this does not answer:** the People screen counts only affairs that are established *at this instant*, and exposure is the mechanic's whole point (divorce, arrest, a 21-day scandal cooldown), so `0 affairs` is a normal snapshot on a save where affairs have happened — the permanent record is the Log/Chronicle. Verification: `tests/citizenOverview.youthLove.test.ts` (3) pins the one-pair-counts-once rule, that sweethearts do not inflate the marriage/affair/pregnancy counters, and that a pair whose members are gone counts zero; `npm run test:types` clean, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, and the full standard suite green (243 files / 1,485 passed, 2 skipped).

- **Fixed: zooming can no longer empty the ground, and the decor overlay is no longer a full-map canvas — the per-chunk decor bake is what makes the spec map sizes reachable** — reported from play as *"zooming in and out gives a black background"*. **The cause was the cache key, not the bake:** the ground's bake resolution followed the camera (`groundWorldStepForZoom`: 1:1 above 100 % zoom, 1:2 below ~70 %, 1:4 below ~35 %) and the step was part of the chunk key, so crossing a zoom threshold **disposed every chunk on screen** and re-baked the view — which, once the fill became budgeted, showed the void until the refill caught up (measured: `putImageData` counted 0 bakes and 0 ground blits for the whole 2.5 s after a zoom-out).
