Below 721px the same media query in that block already widens the drawer across the whole map, so there is no room beside it and the mini-map stays put. **Verified in the built artifact rather than asserted:** in `dist/assets/index-*.css` the rule is emitted **unlayered** (brace depth 0 immediately before it) while Tailwind's competing `left-4` sits at depth 1 inside `@layer utilities`, so an unlayered declaration wins the cascade without `!important`; the literal is moved into the custom property verbatim and `.game-view-drawer--build` reads it, so the drawer width has one definition and the two readers cannot drift. `:has()` is unsupported in no shipping target, and where it is dropped the rule is simply ignored — the previous behaviour, not a new regression. **Recorded, not repaired:** the right-anchored info drawer (`--info`, 46rem above `z-index: 30`) covers the bottom-right zoom controls by exactly the same mechanism; the owner did not report it and it is out of this fix's scope. No TypeScript, simulation, save or protocol change. Gates: `npm run build` exit 0, `npm run test:types` exit 0, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npm run test:standard` **269 files / 1476 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, `npm run dup` 22 clones. Honest limit: this was verified by cascade inspection and layout arithmetic, **not** by a browser pass — `npm run test:browser` was not run.

- **Fixed: the renderer hot-path batch — one world-units-per-tile constant, and the build grid no longer scans every building per cell** — the deferred renderer findings are worked to a recorded state, each either fixed or left with the reason. **One terrain-tile size, not three:** `frontierCombat` carried `PIXELS_PER_TILE = 10` and `placementUtils` carried `PLACEMENT_TILE_SIZE = 10`, while the owner `TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE = 10` already existed in `gameTypes` — the same "world units per tile" stated three times, so raid-distance and footprint-tile math would have silently disagreed with the terrain grid if the owner ever moved; both copies are gone and a source guard now fails on their return. **Two visible correctness fixes:** quadruped sprites are anchored at `y = 0.88`, but the viewport cull pad was `0.75 × spriteH`, so an animal whose body was still on-screen by up to ~0.13 of its height popped out at the bottom edge instead of sliding in — the cull now uses the anchor's actual per-axis extents; and the worker/SoA path's grass fallback was a hard-coded empty array, so with the spatial grid switched off it drew no grass at all while the main-thread path still did — it now draws from the bucket's own grass shims. **Allocation and hot-path fixes, each output-identical:** the grid overlay called `worldToScreen` per line and discarded half of the `[x, y]` tuple it allocated — the owner now exposes single-axis `worldToScreenX`/`worldToScreenY` accessors and the overlay calls them; `drawBuildings` re-filtered and re-sorted the completed-building list every repaint — it is now cached on the buildings array identity plus the completion count (identity alone misses an in-place `completed` flip, the count catches it); `buildRenderSnapshot` copied the whole alive list and every by-type bucket on every frame in the worker path where nothing read the buckets — the SoA path now reuses the world's own alive array and a shared frozen empty table; `drawHuntChaseLines` rebuilt a full id map and spread two bucket arrays every repaint — it now reuses the id map the bucket builder already made per tick and iterates the two buckets directly; and `drawGroundAO` created a radial gradient per tree and per building per repaint — it now composites a single cached, pre-rasterized blob (the viewport washes and the per-particle smoke gradients are left: one per frame is not the cost the per-entity ones were). **The one algorithmic win:** in build mode the validity markers called `canPlaceBuildingSnapshot` per candidate cell, and each call scanned every building for an overlap — O(cells × buildings) per repaint while panning; a renderer-only spatial occupancy index (buildings inserted into every cell their footprint touches, queries visit the cells theirs touches, and the same strict-inequality test runs on the shortlist) now answers the overlap check in O(nearby), with `tests/placementOverlapIndex.equivalence.test.ts` proving it byte-for-byte agrees with the linear scan across a lattice, edge-touching placements and rotated footprints. The authoritative placement path keeps its plain linear scan — it is a single call, not a lattice. Gates: `tsc` clean on all three projects, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npx vitest run` **269 files / 1475 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, `npm run build` exit 0, `npm run audit:deps` 339 modules / 0 runtime cycles, `npm run dup` 22 clones.

- **Fixed: the audit's remaining findings — pressing Save no longer moves your village, two saves can no longer race, and the keyboard contracts hold** — the deep audit's first pass left twelve findings recorded with evidence rather than repaired; eight are closed here. **The one a player would feel: saving used to replace the world the UI was showing.** `exportAuthoritativeWorld` first called `syncAfterWorkerMutation()` — which *adopts* the worker's authoritative world into the display — and then assigned the exported clone to `GameLoop.this.world`. Since `applySimTickDelta` never writes `paused`/`speed`/the dismissed-id sets, and `mutateWorld` forwards a control to the worker only when it changes, a pause or a speed change made just before a save could be **reverted on screen and could not be re-sent**. The export is now a read: it returns the authoritative world for the caller to persist and leaves the display alone (it needs no nudge — `whenIdle()` already waits for every tick and command to settle). **Two overlapping saves could also silently store the wrong world:** the 30 s autosave and a Ctrl+S resume from the same idle wake, the second `exportSave` is refused by the host, and the loop caught that and fell back to the *display shadow* — recording a world up to four ticks behind **while toasting "Game saved successfully"**. `persistGame` now holds the in-flight save and makes a second caller await it, coalescing rather than queueing, because both callers want the same snapshot written to the same slot. **And loading a save is finally a read of its input:** `pickWorldStateFromSave` copies top-level keys by reference, so the returned world's arrays were the caller's, and `migrateTickTimeline` scales tick-valued fields in place — loading the same parsed object twice scaled it twice (`createdAtTick` 700 → 2100 → 7200), retroactively moving the first session's raid deadline; the load now clones once at the entry point. **Three more repairs:** `beautyGrid` is rebuilt on load instead of waiting for the next daily tick (so a loaded colony no longer runs up to a game day with free-time settlers ignoring decor and no beauty happiness nudge — and `villageHappiness` is deliberately *not* recomputed with it, because it is saved; the existing persistence test rejected a first cut that did); the logistics overlay is memoised on world identity plus the tick, so panning no longer re-projects the whole colony every frame (**measured: 0.072 ms/call** on a 903-entity / 120-building world, so the earlier "stutter" framing was too strong — it was ~4.3 ms/s and ~12 000 avoidable objects/s); and `gameWorker.node.ts` now belongs to `tsconfig.node.json`, so `npm run build` finally type-checks the Node/Tauri worker entry that no project checked (measured at 0 errors first, so the gate arrived green). **Keyboard and accessibility:** the cinematic moment card covers the screen for ~4.8 s and never claimed the keyboard, so hotkeys acted on the game behind it and Escape *cleared your map selection* instead of skipping the card — it now uses the shared overlay-keyboard contract; the story card and the diplomacy card both pinned themselves to `top-4 z-10`, so a pending diplomacy card painted over an authored story choice (both are flow children of the overlay slot now, story above diplomacy); the intro's "press any key" had no target check while its click handler had one, so Tab to the mute button and pressing Enter or Space skipped the intro instead of toggling mute; three icon-only build-rail buttons gained accessible names, the dashboard's settler rows became keyboard-reachable (they were the only route to the "Why this settler…" panel), and the Big News dismiss button lost the `role="status"` that was overriding its button role. **Still open, deliberately:** the twelve renderer hot-path allocations (they span ten modules and need a browser-measured before/after each) and the manual-save floating text that the worker's next delta erases (routing it through the worker is a protocol addition, and the alternative is losing the in-world confirmation — an owner call). `tests/audit2026-09-21.regression.test.ts` (30), `tests/gameLoop.exportAuthority.test.ts` (4), `tests/logisticsOverlay.cache.test.ts` (5) and `tests/ui-a11y.contract.test.ts` (9) pin every repair, and `tests/useGamePersistence.test.ts` gained three cases for the save coalescing. Gates: `tsc` clean on **all three** projects, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npx vitest run` **268 files / 1471 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, `npm run build` exit 0, `npm run audit:deps` 339 modules / 0 runtime cycles. No test was deleted, skipped or weakened; two source guards were widened and are disclosed in the report. `test:full-year` and `test:browser` were **not** run.

- **New: caravans buy and sell stone** — stone was the one `ResourceKey` with no visitor-trade action at all: the catalogue offered food, wood and iron in both directions while stone — the input for every building, repair, upgrade, forge order and research node — could only be imported through a trade route. Two actions close it, priced against their neighbours: **Buy stone · 20💰 → 25🪨** (a smaller pile than wood for the same gold, as iron is dearer still) and **Sell stone · 35🪨 → 20💰** (a slightly better rate per unit than wood, which the caravan has no cheaper way to obtain). Both are gated by the same owner rule as every other action — price first, then room in the store for what arrives — so the buttons carry the reason they are refused instead of failing after the click, and the auto-play bot now tops stone up below its industry shelf alongside wood and iron (the Quarry step's inline `60`, now the named `LOW_STONE_STOCK` its second reader needs). No simulation rule, save field or protocol version changed. Gates: `tsc` clean on both projects, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npx vitest run` **265 files / 1447 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, `npm run build` exit 0.

- **Fixed: a deep audit of an already-green tree — twelve latent defects the suite could not see** — the pass ran five independent area audits (worker boundary, renderer and hot paths, simulation domain, save/load and persistence, React UI/hooks) plus a semantic-duplication sweep against a baseline of **264 files / 1420 tests passing**, so none of these was a failing test. **Two could take the server down or corrupt a colony.** A command's render refresh could exhaust the worker's render-buffer pool — `MAX_PIPELINE_DEPTH` reserved a slot for the display buffer but none for the command's own, and `canPipelineTick()` did not count a command in flight — and the worker answers pool exhaustion with a *fatal* fault, so issuing a command at high pipeline depth **tore the simulation worker down mid-session** and the loop fell back to main-thread ticks; `canPipelineTick()` now also requires no command outstanding, and the command is never dropped (`applyCommand` queues it and posts the moment the slot frees). And the load path accepted a same-version payload with `tick: "not-a-number"`, loading a world whose calendar was `NaN` — `tick % TICKS_PER_DAY` is then never 0, so **the daily layer never ran again**: no days, seasons, aging or births, and a clock reading "Year NaN"; an incomplete `resources` object did the same class of damage more quietly, since every affordability rule is `>=` against `undefined` and therefore permanently false, so the colony could never build again and the next save wrote the broken state back. `findUnrestorableField` now refuses both **by name** through the existing `unrestorable` refusal. **Reloading also no longer refunds a day of winter:** `villageCanHeat` — computed once per day and read by the *simulation* for the rest of it to decide whether every settler takes the 1.5× unheated-winter energy penalty — was absent from the save allow-list, so a reload after a failed morning wood burn read "heated" and skipped the cold. **Three statistics and messages now tell the truth.** The yearly **Marriages** total could never rise: it counted `relationshipStatus === 'married'` while conception sets **both** partners to `'expecting'`, so `floor((married − lastYear) / 2)` had the two departing partners cancel the two arriving ones exactly — marry a couple and conceive them the same year and the year closed at zero while the People screen showed them married; a new leaf owner `civilStatus.ts` (`isMarriedOrExpecting`) replaces the count and collapses the two private `isActivelyMarried` copies in `familyTree`/`socialLife`. The leader-honored Big News banner was gated on a reputation **delta**, which the 0–100 clamp makes zero once reputation is capped — retiring the announcement for every later victory for the rest of the game; `addReputation` now returns the amount actually applied and the banner fires on `granted > 0`, which is true for a victory that *reaches* the cap and false only when the clamp genuinely delivered nothing. A multi-resource trade float announced only the last resource (`receivedLabel` was assigned per loop pass), so a deal carrying gold **and** iron showed only the iron; it now reads `resourceTypes.formatResourceAmounts` over what actually landed. **Drawn truth and stored truth diverged in two more places.** The renderer kept a private copy of the village anchor whose no-hall/no-house branch returned the *first completed player building*, while the simulation falls through to the settlers' mean — so on a colony whose only finished structure was a Barracks, the drawn raid march line and the simulated raid ended at different points; the rule now lives once in the new leaf `villageAnchor.ts`. And `EntitySpatialGrid.insert` early-returned when an entity had not changed cell, but the worker's render path builds a **fresh shim object** per slot per tick with the same id and cell — so the grass render grid kept the first tick's objects forever and any field that changed without a cell change (`flash`, `size`) never reached the renderer, while N shims per tick were allocated as pure garbage. **Three smaller correctness repairs:** `extractSimPrep` spread `floatingTexts`/`deathParticles` shallowly while the realtime layer decays those elements in place, so a failed tick's rollback restored post-tick values and aged every popup twice; `beautyGrid` was missing from the runtime-cache strip list, letting a live `Int16Array` ride an export clone; and a nameless settler read **"Settler"** in a workforce log, **"Unknown"** in an election notice and **"A settler"** from the owner, because both copies were `.ts` files that the existing guard — which walks only `.tsx` — structurally could not see. **Two action gates failed open:** the village-request card's `acceptBlockedReason` prop was declared, rendered and never passed, so an unaffordable Accept button stayed enabled carrying the *positive* detail and refused only after the click; and the raid card re-derived the raid owner's gate inline, testing `hasIronSpears || hasStoneSpears` where the owner tests spears **or swords**, with refusal copy that had already drifted from the rule it described. Both now ask their owner and render its reason. `tests/audit2026-09-21.regression.test.ts` adds **27 cases**, one per repair plus the owner guards the old suite could not express — including an **all-`.ts`** scan for nameless-settler fallbacks and a save-refusal case for each malformed-payload shape. **The audit's highest-priority suspicion was disproved rather than fixed:** a field-by-field probe comparing a real `gameTick` against `applySimPrep(extractSimPrep(world))` over five tick phases including a year boundary found **zero** rollback gaps, so the payload is closed. Twelve further confirmed findings are recorded with file:line evidence in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-21-deep-audit.md` and deliberately left unfixed — twelve renderer hot-path items (per-frame gradient creation, per-frame tuple allocation in the grid overlay, build-mode repaint cost, per-repaint `Map`/spread/closure garbage, `drawBuildings` re-sorting every repaint, snapshot lists the SoA path never reads, per-tick `JSON.stringify`/`structuredClone` over all buildings), the 30 s autosave's display-world/authority split, two overlapping saves silently storing the shadow, `loadGameFromParsed` mutating its payload, the manual-save float written into the tick-owned array, a one-day `beautyGrid` gap after a load, and six UI/a11y defects. Gates: `tsc` clean on both projects, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npx vitest run` **265 files / 1447 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, `npm run build` exit 0, `npm run audit:deps` 339 modules / 0 runtime cycles, `npm run check:source` OK. No test was deleted, skipped or weakened. `test:full-year` and `test:browser` were **not** run.

## <u>[0.6.4.1]</u> — 2026-08-28

- **Fixed: the working tree did not build, and five guard tests were red against APIs the last session had already replaced** — the tree was left mid-edit: `src/components/tabPanels/VillageTabPanel.tsx` still rendered `<FoodLedger state={state} />` inside its "Food this day" section while the component and its `summarizeFoodLedger`/`ECONOMY_SOURCE_LABELS` import had been deleted, so `npm run build` failed at `TS2304: Cannot find name 'FoodLedger'` and the Village tab would have thrown on render. The component's own doc comment had been left orphaned directly above `export interface VillageTabPanelProps`, which is how the deletion was identified as an accident rather than a decision. `FoodLedger` is restored (the ledger owner sums, the view renders), and the reputation block again names the visitor-trade bands through `VISITOR_TRADE_FRIENDLY_REP`/`VISITOR_TRADE_HARSH_REP` instead of restating `80`/`30`, which is the rule `tests/uiSingleOwner.test.ts` guards. **The four remaining red tests asserted the pre-refactor API and are updated to the owners they were written to protect, not weakened:** `housingDiagnostics`' `unassignedPlayerHumans` → `homelessPlayerHumans` (`residencyOccupancy.isHomelessSettler`, prisoners excluded — the count the old field got wrong); `singleOwner.predicates` now accepts `playerHumansFrom` beside `isPlayerHuman`, both of which are `playerHuman.ts`'s own accessors, so the "asks the owner, never re-derives" half is unchanged; `uiRefinement.owners` R29 pins `villageStats.beds` from `computeVillageStats` (the panel stopped printing an open-bed figure of its own — that is the People screen's stat card); and `huntingSpot.cleanup` expects the fourth `clearHuntersTargetingPrey` argument, the tick's `byType` buckets that keep the reverse-hunt sweep off every living entity (audit H-1). Gates on the settled tree: `npm run build` exit 0, `npm run test:types` exit 0, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npm run test:standard` exit 0 — **262 files / 1414 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, `npm run audit` exit 0 (335 modules, 0 runtime cycles), `npm run test:full-year` exit 0 (360 days, 0 invariant violations), `npm run test:browser` **verdict: pass**, 0 console and 0 page errors. No test was deleted or skipped.

- **Fixed: the UI/UX owner sweep — fifteen rules that were still computed inside a view, and the last three `.tsx` clones (2026-09-20 audit, `O-1`…`O-8`, `W-1`…`W-3`, clones 2/3)** — the pass asked one question of every component: is this a rule the simulation already owns? **The worst finding was an affordance that lied.** `App.tsx` counted "idle workers" with its own filter (`!isJuvenile && homeBuildingId == null && !faction`) while the staffing owner's gates also refuse the **imprisoned** and the **already-assigned** (`buildingStaffingActions.staffCandidateRefusal`), and `removeWorkerTransition` clears `homeBuildingId` on imprisonment — so jailed settlers were counted, and the inspector printed "Fill workers (5)" in exactly the branch where the owner's assignable list is empty. The count and its prop are gone; the button says "Fill workers" and the list is the owner's. **The header invented a second housing threshold:** it flagged the colony near-cap at `population / cap >= 0.9` while `housingDiagnostics` bands the same question at 0.95/0.80, so at 91 % the chip was red and the Village panel said "tight". The band and the cap now live in `populationGrowth` (`POPULATION_NEAR_CAP_RATIO`, `resolvePopulationCap`) and the chip reads `isPopulationNearCap`. Two lines below, `absoluteDay` was `Math.floor(tick / TICKS_PER_DAY)` written out by hand next to calls to two other owners; it now calls `getAbsoluteCalendarDay`. **Four countdowns and fifteen name joins moved into their owners:** `dayCycleClock.daysUntilTick` replaces `Math.ceil((untilTick - tick) / TICKS_PER_DAY)` in the prison, diplomacy, festival-cooldown and header paths (two had no lower clamp, so an elapsed deadline could read as a negative day); `citizenId.citizenFullName` replaces ten hand-rolled surname joins in the panels — `moonHowler.ts` even carried a private `humanDisplayName` *with the same name as the owner's*, and three sim log lines re-typed `'A settler'`. `formatCitizenName`'s own `'Settler'` became the owner's `SETTLER_LABEL_FALLBACK`, so a nameless settler reads "#12 Settler" in a list and "A settler" in a sentence, both owned. **The Church card stopped printing odds that cannot happen:** it clamped an empty priest count up to one before asking the rite weights, so "0 priests on duty → ~25 % cure" could render while the rite itself returns `no_priest`; it now asks `countStaffedPriests` and states the zero case. Also: the fill formula's three remaining copies read `dashboardData.resourceFillPercent`; the build catalogue asks `isUniqueBuildingAlreadyBuilt` and reads `PLACEMENT_FAILURE_LABELS.unique` instead of re-testing the unique flag and re-wording the refusal, and its lock test now asks `isBuildingTechUnlocked` (it had ignored `node.researched`); `NatureTabPanel`'s health and pollution label and bar are one percentage each; `WorkSchedulePanel` reads `readVillageFatigue` (mean **and** bands — the 60/25 thresholds were view-side); and the population panel's "Village mood" reads the `citizenOverview` label instead of a raw happiness field with a view-local `?? 50`. **Deduplication:** one `hooks/useOverlayKeyboard(owner, onEscape, active)` now owns the "an overlay claims the keyboard *and* handles Escape" contract for the four overlays that each hand-wrote both effects — the pair that shipped broken as A-1 — and `GatedChoiceButton` replaces the story/diplomacy choice block, the other remaining `.tsx` clone. `npm run dup`: **25 → 22 clones, 0 in `.tsx`**. The two `App.tsx` load entry points share one `loadFromSlot`, so the menu and the new-settlement screen can no longer grow two refusal wordings. Guards: 11 new source guards in `tests/uiSingleOwner.test.ts` (scanning **every** `.tsx`, so a new view cannot opt out by not being listed), the keyboard-claim guards re-pointed at the shared hook, and four over-specified guards narrowed with the reasoning in the test rather than deleted. Gates: `tsc` clean on both projects, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npm run audit:deps` clean at 334 modules, `npm run test:standard` green except `tests/gameLoop.pacingContract.test.ts`, which fails on another lane's deleted `scripts/perf-all.ts` (all three surviving rate copies read `1`).

- **Fixed: the last open code item from the persistence/worker audit — a stale entity-type cache is now detected, one type-guaranteed test case is replaced with a behavioural one, and the harnesses' own type errors are 19 fewer (audit `OPEN-16`, bug 73)** — `entityTypeCache.getCachedEntityByType` returned whatever bucket set the per-world `WeakMap` held, without comparing it to the caller's entity list. The entry is cleared only by `invalidateCachedEntityByType` (`worldRuntimeCaches.ts`) or by the tick's own composition heuristic, and that heuristic diffs the tick's **opening** list (`deathsThisTick = aliveEntities.length - (allAlive.length - newEntities.length)`, `gameTick.ts:271`), so an entity added *between* ticks left the stale entry in place: the next tick read buckets that omitted it and then republished them as `state.entityByType`, which `ctx.byType` (predator selection, `playerHumans`, `workingSettlers`/`idleSettlers`) and every `byType` renderer read for that tick. The one detector was `assertSimInvariants`' bucket-count check — dev-only, once per colony day, and a `console.warn`. The accessor now fingerprints the cached set (total entities) against the caller's list, rebuilds on disagreement and drops the stale entry; the **reuse** half is deliberately kept, because `gameTick` compares `state.entityByType` identity to decide whether the render catalog must rebuild. `tests/entityTypeCache.validation.test.ts` (1 case) pins both halves, and the rebuild half was proved red-before: reverting the guard fails it with `expected { grass: [ …(178) ], …(8) } not to be { grass: [ …(178) ], …(8) }`. **Test tier:** `tests/simulation.decisionRegistry.test.ts` asserted `DECISION_CADENCES` contains a value the type system derives *from* that array (`cadence: DecisionCadence`, table `satisfies Record<DecisionKey, DecisionOwner>`), so it could only fail with a compile error; it is replaced by a behavioural partition through the registry's own accessor — each decision key returned by exactly one cadence bucket, by its own declared cadence, and the accessor's union equal to `DECISION_KEYS` — which also gives the previously zero-caller `getDecisionsByCadence` a real consumer. **Harness type errors (bug 73), quantified and 19/23 repaired:** with the node project's own options, the 38 `scripts/**/*.ts|.mts` plus `src/game/simWorker/gameWorker.node.ts` carry 23 type errors in 8 files. Fixed: three `MapSize.Small` (no such member — `initGame` was silently resolving a **Medium** map every time, including the 3-year `scripts/sim-invariants.ts` run), two `BuildingType.WallCorner` preset entries (no such member; wall corners are drawn procedurally, `stripRender.ts:301`), `probe-construction`'s `BuildingType.Warehouse ?? BuildingType.Store` (the fallback was already the only reachable branch), `autoplay-food-probe`'s `activeResearch?.name` (it is a **string id**, so the probe printed "(none)" while a research was actually running), `probe-day-budget`'s `Map<number, number>` whose keys are `` `${id}|${dayStart}` `` strings, `perf-all`'s `Set` literal, `repro-worker-stall`'s `unknown` command slot, and five unused imports/locals. **Four remain and are recorded rather than guessed**, because each would change a *published measurement*: `balance-militia.ts:27,75` (completing the fixture sets `age`/`maxAge`, which changes the militia count that harness exists to report) and `perf-all.ts:308,326` (`gameTick(state, FULL_SIM ? undefined : state)` passes a **WorldState where a `SimulationFocus` is expected**, so the "focus throttle" mode throttles everything rather than the viewport). The `scripts/` project entry is therefore still **not** added — a gate that would be red on arrival verifies nothing. Gates: `npm run test:standard` exit 0 — 259 files / **1367 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**; `npm run test:types` exit 0; `npm run lint` **0 warnings / 0 errors** on 336 files.

- **Fixed: the last open row of the 2026-09-20 building pass — the hotel guest index is memoised with its equivalence proved, and the Hotel stops promising gold it never charges (N-8 + U-5 = OPEN-5)** — OPEN-5 was deferred rather than shipped, on the recorded ground that *"shipping unverified memoisation into the tick would be worse than a recorded OPEN"*. `tickHotelLodging` rebuilt an alive-visitor `Set` and rescanned every entity **per hotel per tick**, and `hotelHasVacancy` did it again **per visitor × hotel** inside `pickHotelForVisitor` — O(visitors × hotels × entities) every tick for a handful of guests. It now builds `entityIndex.ensureEntityByIdMap` **once per call** (the same owner the campaign had already used for `defenseStructures.getBarracksGuardCount`) and repairs every hotel's stay pointers in **one** pass over `state.entities`; `guestCount` / `pruneHotelGuests` / `hotelHasVacancy` / `pickHotelForVisitor` take the index as an optional argument and **fall back to the array scan on a map miss**, so a spawn that reached `state.entities` without being indexed still counts as a guest. `checkInVisitor` keeps the old prune-plus-repair for its own hotel alone. **The equivalence argument is the deliverable, not the speed-up:** an entity carries exactly one `hotelStayBuildingId`, so only the per-hotel pass for *that* hotel could ever clear it, and such a pass read only its own already-pruned guest list plus the entity's own liveness and faction — which is why every prune now runs before the union repair, and why the old interleaving (prune A, repair A, prune B, repair B, …) and the new one (prune A, prune B, repair all) reach the same state. `tests/hotelGuestIndex.equivalence.test.ts` (6) diffs two worlds against the pre-fix loop **kept verbatim in the test file**, pins the expected guest lists and pointers literally, covers a dead guest, a guest that stopped being a visitor, a guest id backed by no entity, a pointer to a hotel outside the tick's list, a completed-but-unstaffed hotel, the map-miss fallback, and the exported `hotelHasVacancy`'s own drop-and-repair contract (kept: the repair fires whenever its prune actually dropped a guest, which inside the tick is never), and proves its own differential is non-vacuous (a prune-only implementation changes the snapshot). **U-5:** the Hotel promised "sleep overnight **for gold**" in three places — the module doc, the catalogue description and `checkInVisitor`'s doc — while the implementation charges nothing and the inspector and `describeHotelStatus` already said "free lodging"; all three now say free, so no copy in the game contradicts the code. No gameplay, save, protocol or cadence change. Gates: `npm run test:types` exit 0 · `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors on 336 files · `npx knip` exit 0 · `npm run audit:deps` exit 0 (335 modules, 0 runtime cycles) · new file 6/6 · six adjacent files 30/30.

- **Fixed: the residual open rows from the 2026-09-20 UI/UX audit — the last two a11y gaps, the unconfirmed save-wipe, and a boot-time asset sweep (audit `A-2`, `OPEN-14`, bug 66)** — three rows the campaign had left open or part-fixed are closed, each verified in the code rather than from the register. **The mini-map was unusable without a pointer:** it was a bare `<canvas onClick>` with no role, no accessible name and no tab stop, so the map's only navigation control could not be reached by keyboard at all. It is now `role="button"` + `tabIndex={0}` + a name, with Enter/Space centring the camera on the village (leader first, otherwise the settlers' centroid — the target the `H` hotkey already uses) and a visible focus ring; the shortcuts sheet's entry now reads "Click, or focus it and press Enter, to jump the camera". **Starting a settlement destroyed the browser save from one keystroke:** `beginNewGameSession` calls `deleteSave()`, and Enter in the village-name field called `handleStart` directly, so typing a name and pressing Enter wiped a saved colony with no warning. The start now arms a confirmation first — the two-step shape `SelectedBuildingPanel`'s demolish already uses — whenever a save exists, and Enter routes through it instead of past it. **The 16 legacy `human_male_v0…v7` / `human_female_v0…v7` walk sheets were fetched on every boot** although nothing had referenced them since the class-ladder art landed; the paths leave `spriteLoader.HUMAN_SPRITE_PATHS` (the PNGs stay in `public/sprites/` for rollback, and the coupling to the ladder is documented where the set is defined, because that set decides both preloading and the feet-down anchor). Also: `useGameShellState` returned six members no consumer read (`openTabs`, `toggleTab`, `closeSidebarTabs`, `setCitizenOverviewOpen`, `setOverviewSection`, `setOverviewWorldFocus`) and the three callbacks behind them are gone. `SidebarTab`'s `'schedule'` member is **deliberately kept**: `tests/useGameShellState.test.ts:46` pins its route to the People section, so deleting it would have meant weakening a test. Guards: two new tests in `tests/keyboardGuards.contract.test.ts` (the mini-map is named, Tab-reachable and keyboard-activatable; the destructive start is confirmed and Enter cannot bypass it). Gates: `npm run test:types` exit 0, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npm run test:standard` exit 0.

- **Fixed: the 2026-09-20 audit campaign — 75 findings worked to a recorded status, with the ones that changed play listed here (full record: `docs/private/audits/2026-09-20/BUG-REGISTER.md`, report by subject: `AUDIT-REPORT.md`)** — five audit passes (simulation, building logic, UI/UX + dead code, persistence/worker, test-tier survey) produced 73 numbered bugs plus residuals; **61 fixed, 4 confirmed not-a-defect, 3 part-fixed, 7 open with the reason beside each**, plus 20 claims investigated and cleared. **The three with the most player-visible teeth:** **(1) every story-card ecology effect did nothing.** `ecosystemHealth` and `pollutionLevel` are derived — `tickEcosystemMetrics` recomputed and assigned both outright, *later in the same daily tick* than the story writers ran — so all 13 sites (deer parliament, story events, a rival's "+0.5 pollution") were overwritten before anything read them while the cards promised "Ecology +6 now". The metrics owner now accepts a queued delta, applied on top of its own recompute; the deltas persist in the save, the tick delta and the rollback payload. **(2) spawns in the daily layer were being discarded.** World events, autumn herds and emergency grass patches used bare `allAlive.push` instead of `pushNewEntity`, and the daily layer runs *after* the spatial-grid invariant check but *before* `state.entities` is committed — so a `nature_boom` spawned trees onto an array about to be thrown away, and prey were replenished onto pasture that did not exist, because `spawnGrassPatch` was the one spawner missing the hook that counts patches. **(3) day 1 enforced the wrong storage caps.** `storageMax` had three disagreeing definitions (world-gen, the load fallback, the owner), and since `updateStorageCaps` runs only in the daily layer while a world starts at tick 24, a fresh colony's gold ceiling was **2000 instead of 20000** and its wood cap 1000 instead of 800; all three writers now call the owner's `computeStorageMax`. Also fixed: a worker save wiped the live predator scent trail (auto-save included), visitor trade moved food outside the day ledger, the workshop consumed full inputs when gold accepted only part of the output, refunds bypassed the storage cap so one refund could make it permanently non-authoritative, a Wall→Gate replacement inflated "Buildings" forever, five worker-assignment sites sized venues from the default tavern window, and a paired dialogue could strand a settler as dialogue-busy after a save/load — or for a night, when the Moon Howler transform took the speaking half out of the ticked population. **UI:** the Farm hint promised 3 workers where the config allows 2, the Barracks "+14" and preserve "+4" were typed as prose against exported owners, the Mill's bonus rendered twice, the Dutch word "Handmatig" sat in the English UI, a **"Spawn Moon Howler" cheat shipped to every player** in the Guide, the Valley overview claimed the keyboard and ignored Escape, the first-run tutorial let every gameplay hotkey fire behind it, and four selection strips signalled state by colour alone. **Developer:** `npm run audit` exited 1, so its `&&` never reached the import-cycle gate (now exit 0, with the cycle gate running inside it), and `tsconfig.vitest.json` included a glob matching **zero** files — so `npm run test:types` passed while checking no test file and **132 real errors** were invisible, including six fixtures whose `MapSize.Small` did not exist and silently built Medium maps **while all six tests stayed green**; the tier now checks **252 test files, up from 0**. Every fix carries either a red-before proof or an explicit statement that it does not. **The valley map can now be driven from the keyboard** (closing the last accessibility gap in the game): **arrow keys** move a cursor in 40 wu steps, clamped to both the map and the visible viewport so it cannot leave the screen; **Enter** activates at the cursor by dispatching the *same* canvas click a mouse produces, so placement and selection travel the identical `WorkerCommand` path (`Enter` with no cursor only reveals the cursor — never a blind placement); **Escape** clears the cursor, leaving cancellation of an armed build type to the existing global chain rather than duplicating a rule that already has an owner. The cursor is an amber DOM marker paired with the canvas's own placement ghost and hovered-building highlight, and its state is announced. `Space` is deliberately **left unbound**: `useKeyboardControls` binds it to pause on `window` capture and a `role="img"` canvas is not an activatable target, so binding it here would pause the game on every placement (`OPEN-6`, bug 55's canvas half).

- **Recovered: the reverted generation is back, and the defects it was hiding are fixed** — on 2026-09-20 an older copy of the project was laid over the working tree, reverting every file it contained to its 2026-09-17 state (`docs/HANDOVER-tree-revert-recovery-2026-09-20.md`). The newer generation was reconstructed from the DSH session transcripts and re-applied, then the failures it left were worked to zero. Gates: `npm run test:all` **exit 0** — 241 files / **1283 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**; `tsc` clean on both projects; `npm run build` exit 0; the import-cycle gate clean at 334 modules / 0 runtime cycles. **Real defects fixed, not just restored files:** orphans were adopted into the Leader's House and then force-moved into the manor the housing rule had just refused (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-20-orphans-adopted-into-the-leaders-house.md`); **contextual tips never fired at all**, because detection compared a world object against *itself* — the loop mutates one `WorldState` in place, so every "since the previous tick" test was false (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-contextual-tips-never-fire.md`); a jailed settler was mustered into raids and could be a casualty, because `canBeMustered` existed but no combat pool called it; 30+ raw reputation/resource writes bypassed their owners, so a completed visitor quest could clamp an over-cap purse *down* and a reputation reward could take 99 → 101; `simHelpers.resolveResearchEffect` had two implementations; and the least-crowded-home rule had lost the discount that stops a mover being counted twice. Also: one owner per rule where the guards demanded it — `faceVelocity` (8 inlined sites), `gridCellAxis`, `PREDATOR_TYPES`, `isWallBuildingType`, `isPlayerHuman`, `getRaidExpiresAtTick` — and the dead `formatRaidDeadlineSafe` wrapper module deleted. **Two storm-announcement tests are now `skipIf(!HOSTILE_WEATHER_ENABLED)`** rather than failing: that switch is off by owner ruling and gates the announcement *inside* `applyDailyWeatherEffects`, so those assertions cannot pass while it is off and must not be deleted — they are ready for the day it is flipped. **The browser tier has still not been run since the revert** and remains the open item.

- **New: the performance harness reports real path calls instead of a substituted proxy, and the browser console gate is strict again (roadmap O4, P2)** — the roadmap's "path calls" had no counter behind it: `scripts/perf-seeded-year.mts` published the `spatialQueryMetrics` counts under the name `pathCalls` and carried a `source` string telling a reader not to quote the number as pathfinding. **One counter owner, not two:** `src/game/pathfindingMetrics.ts` records `findPath` invocations, nodes expanded, found/failed/node-cap outcomes, the cheap `lineCrossesBlocked` gate, passability-grid rebuilds and waypoint-cache hit/miss — following the established `spatialQueryMetrics` contract (opt-in by env flag, per-tick and session buckets, **zero overhead when disabled**) — and `gameTick` resets and flushes it beside the spatial bucket. `src/game/pathfinding.ts` is its only writer. The harness now publishes a sibling `pathfinder` block while `pathCalls` keeps its historical name with an honest `source`. **Measured over a full 360-day Huge run (seed 12345, `--scenario=producers`, 25 920 ticks):** **11 656 A\* calls (0.53/tick), 854 247 nodes expanded (73.3/call), found 11 656 · failed 0 · node-cap 0**, 108 546 line checks (4.9/tick), **1 grid rebuild** (one passability grid per map, as designed), **71.2 % waypoint-cache hit rate**; tick p50 3.21 / p95 16.76 / max 285.09 ms, 0 stalled ticks, 0 invariant violations over 360 checks, worker 72/72 with 0 fallbacks. The result is worth more than the counter: A\* runs **0.53 times per tick** against **104.5 spatial queries per tick**, so pathfinding is two orders of magnitude rarer than the spatial scans, and the standing assumption that pathfinding is a hot path is not supported by measurement. `tests/pathfindingMetrics.test.ts` (5) pins the contract, including that a disabled harness counts nothing and that a pre-A\* reject is not laundered into a search failure. **And the browser tier's one exception is gone:** `scripts/browser-smoke.mjs`'s `KNOWN_CONSOLE_ERRORS` excused `Failed to load sprite: /sprites/mountains/…` for a defect `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-10-mountain-sprites-missing.md` had already **resolved on 2026-09-13** — the four paths left `preloadAllSprites` and the ridge-peak stamp resolves through the cache-only `stampPropSprite`, so nothing requests the art and the pattern matched nothing (that record's own verification reported `knownConsoleErrors: 0`). The entry contradicted the block's own rule that every exception must name a live bug record, so it is removed and the console gate fails on any error again. Shipping the four directional PNGs remains an **art task, not a defect** — `mountainSpritesReady()` already gates the decor rebuild. No simulation rule, save field, cadence or protocol version changed.

- **New: the logistics overlay shows blocked paths, and the projection can no longer disturb the simulation's path cache (roadmap F4 residual)** — the overlay's row text promised "supply routes, commute pressure, **blocked paths**, and poorly connected buildings", and blocked paths were the one piece never built. **The rule:** a settler's home↔work leg is now `severity: 'blocked'` when its direct line crosses water or mountains, with precedence `blocked → extreme → over_budget`. It is reported **even when the leg is inside the walk budget**, which is the point — such a leg cannot be walked at all, so the settler must detour (`pathfinding.steerWithPath`), and a short-but-blocked commute was previously invisible here because it never exceeded the budget. `totals.blockedCommuteCount` joins the other counters, the renderer gained `BLOCKED_COMMUTE_STYLE` (violet, deliberately outside the red/amber family) and a legend entry, and the severity→colour mapping now lives in one `commuteStrokeStyle` helper instead of a nested ternary. **The hazard this exposed, fixed rather than dodged:** the obvious implementation calls `pathfinding.getPathGrid`, but that grid is a **single module-level cache shared with the simulation**, and `setCurrentPathMap` treats a change of its identity as a new map and calls `pathCache.clear()`. The overlay is recomputed on **every render snapshot** (`renderSnapshot.ts`) and passes its own `buildings` array, so a projection using it would flip that identity back and forth and clear the simulation's A\* cache from the render path — a simulation side-effect produced by a read-only projection, which the architecture forbids ("the renderer decides nothing"). `pathfinding.ts` therefore now has **one builder and two caches**: `buildPathGrid` is the single blocked-terrain/wall rule, `getPathGrid` owns the simulation's grid, and the new `getReadOnlyPathGrid` owns a second cache that touches neither the sim grid nor the waypoint cache and skips `recordGridRebuild`, so the pathfinder metric keeps counting *simulation* builds only. The classification uses the cheap `lineCrossesBlocked` (4–128 samples, scaling with span), **not** A\*, because it runs per snapshot. `tests/logisticsOverlay.projection.test.ts` is now **16** cases: the new ones are a controlled pair (identical buildings, entity and 40 wu distance — only the river column differs, so only terrain can explain the severity change) and a guard that the projection's grid is a different object **and** that asking for it does not swap the simulation's grid identity. No save or simulation impact; the overlay remains opt-in and off by default.

- **New: the long-run harness now measures the colony it gates, and the browser tier measures the build it ships (roadmap P1, P2, P6)** — three roadmap rows closed by measurement rather than assertion, each with the numbers recorded. **P1 (seeded colony health):** `scripts/run-full-year.mts` gained a named scenario switch and both long runs now share one health projection (`scripts/colonyHealth.ts`), so the historical gate and the new one cannot drift. `--scenario=producers --days=100` closes the coverage gap the audit measured — producers are staffed from day 1 and **nothing is restocked after day 0** — while `--scenario=restocked` (the default, `npm run test:full-year`) keeps the old behaviour, and every run header and checkpoint states which is which. Verified over two consecutive seed-12345 runs: checkpoints at days 30/60/90/100, food carried by production instead of being pinned to the cap (650 → 1796 → 1973 → **1437** → 1490, including a **−42 net day**), housing pressure moving comfortable → tight → **critical**, workforce 13 working / 35 idle, lifecycle reported as **8 conceptions ≠ 2 active pregnancies ≠ 9 births ≠ 0 deaths**, 19 marriages with 25 transitions, and **0 invariant violations**. Stalled ticks, serialization spikes and invariant violations are counted separately. Honest limit: every state-derived field is identical across the two runs, but three `deltas` fields (`jsonHash`, `v8Bytes`, `serializeMs`) are not — the health report reproduces, the serialized delta does not, and the 728-byte size difference is recorded rather than explained away. **P2 (one-year performance):** `scripts/perf-seeded-year.mts` runs the Huge map for 360 days in two phases because the repository can only observe them separately. In-process (25 920 ticks): tick **p50 2.57 / p95 8.75 / p99 26.51 / max 255.6 ms**, 0 stalled ticks; delta size per day (JSON p50 1.35 MB, V8 p50 2.57 MB); serialization p99 35.9 ms with **3 spikes** past 50 ms; memory trend heap 64.4 → 201.2 MB (peak RSS 475.9 MB); **console volume 0 calls**; 0 invariant violations over 360 checks. Worker transport: 72/72 ticks, **0 fallbacks**, 0 errors, pipeline depth 4, result gap p95 28.0 ms. "Path calls" has no per-call pathfinder counter in this repo, so the record publishes the `spatialQueryMetrics` counts (2 709 793 queries, 104.5/tick) labelled with their source rather than inventing one. **P6 (normal-zoom presentation):** the browser tier gained a five-configuration viewport/DPI matrix (`Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` + `Page.captureScreenshot` per configuration, console-error gate re-applied per configuration) and a **41-item acceptance checklist** across the row's five areas plus the settler walk-through and the UI-audit rows, with human-judgement items marked `needs-human-review` and never auto-passed. Verified here: `npm run test:accept` **exit 0**, `verdict: pass`, `failures: []`, checklist 22 pass / **0 fail** / 12 human / 7 not-observed, void ratios 0.005–0.014 against a 0.25 limit, 0 console errors / 0 page exceptions / 0 4xx, and the four audit rows NOT REPRODUCED. The DPR check now compares the effective scale factor against the DPR with a 0.02 tolerance; the previous `cssSize × dpr` comparison produced a 1-px false failure at 150 % scaling. Residuals recorded rather than hidden: the building/road/wall/watchtower and child-sprite items and the walk-through's commute/work beat need a built-out settlement (the harness follows intro → settle → play and does not build for the player), and `scripts/perf-seeded-year.mts` is invoked by its documented path rather than an npm script. Gate on this tree: `test:all` exit 0, `test:accept` exit 0, lint 0 warnings / 0 errors.

- **Developer: the decision registry can no longer point at a test file that does not exist** — `src/game/simulation/decisionRegistry.ts` is the machine-checkable ownership contract, and its `testFile` column told a reader where each decision's coverage lives. One row pointed at `tests/phase678.regression.test.ts`, **which has never existed in this tree**, and four more still promised a test "to be added in Objective N" long after those objectives shipped (`construction`, `socialFeedback`, `conception`, `commands`); a reader following a 404 learns nothing about coverage, and a stale promise reads as missing work. The five rows now name the files that actually cover them (`humanLifecycle`/`conceptionEvent.labelling`, `demolish.roundtrip`, `humanChat.ambientPairing`, `youthConception.ageFloor`, `workerCommand.roundtrip`), and `tests/simulation.decisionRegistry.test.ts` gained a case that makes the column self-checking: every `tests/*.test.ts` path the registry names must exist on disk, the count must stay above a floor so a broken regex cannot pass it vacuously, and the registry may not carry "to be added" text again. Both assertions were proved non-vacuous by restoring each defect in turn (**`registry points at a missing file: tests/phase678.regression.test.ts: expected false to be true`** and **`a stale "to be added" note is back`**), then restoring the file byte-identically. Docs and test only — no runtime, save or protocol change.

- **Fixed: the four browser-measured UI-audit rows, and a pause that froze the sim without telling the player (roadmap 01 closed; P6 browser pass)** — the P6 browser tier supplied the evidence first, and it changed two of the four decisions. **R13, the one that mattered:** at 900×700 the header's content measured **1009 px in a 900 px bar**, `.game-shell` clips both axes with no scrollbar, and the ☰ Menu control sat at x 942–1009 — its centre (975, 27) outside the viewport with `elementFromPoint` unable to resolve it. The save/settings/new-game control was simply unreachable at a narrow window. The header now wraps its control groups (`flex-wrap` + `gap-y-1`) instead of clipping them; post-fix the bar measures 900/900 with 0 px overflow and the Menu hit-test lands on the control. Same fix closes the second browser finding: the bar also overflowed at 1024×640. **R40:** collapsing the inspector hid the panel body while the column kept its 18.5rem, so the map canvas measured **1304 px expanded and 1304 px collapsed** — the player who collapsed it to see more map gained exactly nothing. The collapsed state is now a compact control strip (expand + clear) in a 48 px column, and the canvas measures **1552 px**, a real **+248 px**. The diagnostics drawer is not rendered while collapsed, because a 3rem column cannot lay it out — expanding brings it straight back from its own remembered state. **R20:** the zoom select showed the snapped preset ("145%") directly above the live zoom ("160%") with no visible label, so after one `+` the player saw two bare numbers; they now read **Set** and **Live**, and the select's title no longer conflates the two. **R38:** the work window — the rule that decides whether production happens at all — was tooltip-only; the clock chip now carries a `🛠 06:00–16:00` badge whenever the window is non-default (measured: absent before, `06:00–16:00` after), hidden on weekends. All four now report **NOT REPRODUCED** in a re-run of the real build. **And a defect the browser pass found that no reading of the source would have:** with the simulation frozen, the pause control still read "Pause simulation" and the "⏸ Paused — Space to resume" banner never appeared. Root cause: `GameLoop.mutateWorld` ended in `notify(true)`, and `notify` **dedupes on the tick** (`tickChanged && tick === lastNotifiedTick → return`) — a pause never advances the tick, so the one notification that could repaint the header was swallowed and React kept rendering the pre-pause world; the world object is mutated in place, so nothing else would ever have repainted it. `mutateWorld` now forces its notification, the same shape the loop already uses for its other out-of-tick state changes, and `tests/gameLoop.speedControl.test.ts` gained the regression — red-before `pausing must notify subscribers: expected 0 to be greater than 0`, source restored byte-identically (sha256 compared) before the green run. Gate: `test:all` exit 0 — 239 files / 1 270 passed / 3 skipped, lint 0 warnings / 0 errors. **One item stays open and is not mine to close:** the browser harness itself still exits 1 on its own 1.5× matrix precondition (`expectedBackingStore 1476×1019` vs observed `1476×1018` — a 1-px DPR rounding mismatch), which is present in the harness's own earlier run too; it is reported to the P6 workstream rather than papered over.

- **Fixed: the C2 duplication register — one name, one idle rule, one reputation band, one headroom function (roadmap 01, audit §C2)** — the UI audit's semantic-duplicate list ("the same rule computed in a view instead of read from its owner") is now closed. **The settler-name fallback had three values on one screen:** `SelectedEntityPanel` read `entity.name || 'Unnamed'` for its title and `… || 'Settler'` twice for the spouse and pet lines while its own family list already used the `citizenId` owner — and the same `'Settler'` copy sat in six places in `SelectedBuildingPanel`, with `'Unnamed'` in the dashboard table and `'Unknown'` in `PopulationPanel`. All eleven sites now call `citizenGivenName`, so a nameless settler reads "A settler" everywhere. **The "many adults are idle" threshold** was typed in the overview screen as well as in `citizenOverview.ts`, so retuning it would have left the Work card green while the header mood said "Under pressure" on the same screen; the owner now exports `hasManyAdultsIdle` and both read it. **The reputation bands** that price visitor trade were hand-written in three places; `VISITOR_TRADE_FRIENDLY_REP` / `VISITOR_TRADE_HARSH_REP` and `getVisitorTradeReputationBand` now own them, `getVisitorTradePriceMult` is derived from the band, and the visitor banner and the Village tooltip name the bands instead of restating the numbers. **Storage headroom** in the visitor panel was a local `max(0, cap - current)` that dropped the owner's non-finite-cap branch; it now calls `getAvailableStorageHeadroom`. Verified already fixed in the same register, with the owner in place: building output hints (the drifting "+8 / max +72", "+15", "+25 % per worker", "+600", "+800" prose is gone, built from `getWallSegmentCap`/`SMITH_BONUS_PER_WORKER`/… at render time), skill→output %, resource fill %, the research gate, the ecosystem-health read, the dynasty rule, the food-low threshold, the refugee/rival/recruit gates, the focusable-element selector and the tab mapping. `tests/uiSingleOwner.test.ts` grew from 23 to 27 cases and its name-fallback guard was strengthened: the old one checked two named expressions, which is precisely how three sites in one file kept drifting — the new one asserts that no view file re-types a nameless-settler fallback at all. Red-before proven against a full mirrored pre-fix `src/` tree (**4 failed, 23 passed** — the four new guards, nothing else), with the mirror deleted afterwards. **One regression I introduced and fixed in the same pass, disclosed:** my first version had the visitor panel call `getVisitorTradePriceMult`, which the F18 guard in `tests/visitorTradePanel.eligibility.test.ts` correctly forbids — the panel must render, not price (`expected … not to match /getVisitorTradePriceMult|getVisitorTradeRewardMult/`). The owner now exposes the *band*, the panel asks for that, and both guard files pass together. This time the gate caught it: `test:all` failed 1/1272 before the correction and is green after.

- **Developer: ownership coverage is 100 % again, and the two claims that had drifted are corrected** — a regenerated `npm run graph:calls` showed **2 504 of 2 536 functions** owned (98.7 %), not the 100 % roadmap row **N31** still advertised: eight modules had grown since the 2026-09-16 sweep without an ownership row (`logisticsOverlayData` and `renderer/logistics` from F4, `legacyGoals` from F5, `simulation/movementSteering` from A14, `keyboardOwnership`, `buildingPlacementLabels`, `components/WorkforcePolicyPanel` from F3, and `src/test/factories` from G4), and the two helpers added in this session's O2/T4 work were unowned too. All ten now have rows in `OWNERSHIP_OVERVIEW.md` with their cadence and key entry functions, the graph was regenerated, and coverage measures **2 542 of 2 542 (100 %) with 196 decisions and 0 ambiguous calls**; N31's line records both the original and the re-measured figure instead of leaving the stale one standing. This is documentation only — no runtime, save or protocol change.

- **Fixed: four more UI-audit rows — the menu now takes focus, the intro stops eating Tab, the mood stops repeating itself, and a refused load on the new-settlement screen finally says why (roadmap 01, rows R11/R36/R37/R39)** — a second sweep of `docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/UI_REFINEMENT_AND_DUPLICATION_2026-09-17.md` re-verified the rows the campaign had not yet reached, and **four more turned out to be already fixed** (R8, whose challenge-progress default branch now covers the year/building goals; R10, the population chart gained a legend from its own `SERIES` plus an accessible name; R18, the pause chip moves to the bottom when a decision card occupies the top centre; R28, the cinematic title card is a focusable `role="button"` with Enter/Space/Escape) — twenty-seven of the forty rows are now closed by earlier work. The four fixed here: **R11** the game menu declared `role="dialog"` and ran a Tab trap, but nothing ever moved focus into the panel, so both wrap branches were unreachable and Tab walked every control of the game behind the click-catching backdrop (a screen reader heard no dialog); focus now enters on open, in the layout effect that already positioned the panel. **R36** the intro screen treated **any** key but Escape as "continue", so Tab could not reach the 🔊/🔇 control beside the CTA and modifier-only chords advanced the intro; Tab and Ctrl/Cmd/Alt are now ignored. **R37** the Valley overview printed the village mood twice on one screen — as a sentence in the header and as the "Village mood" stat card — competing for the same attention; the card keeps the label and the header keeps the explanation. **R39** is the one with teeth: "Load saved game" lives on the new-settlement screen, and that screen never rendered the save banner, so a refused load set a message that was never drawn and expired before the player reached the game — the F19 fix had made the *reason* exist, but this screen could not show it. The banner is now one shared component rendered by both session states, raised above the setup screen's `fixed inset-0 z-50` overlay. `tests/uiRefinement.residuals.test.ts` grew to 9 cases, all four new guards proven non-vacuous against a mirrored pre-fix tree (**8 failed pre-fix**). The four rows still open (R13 header overflow at narrow widths, R20 the preset-vs-live zoom pair, R38 the tooltip-only work window, R40 the collapsed inspector freeing no width) are all visual or measurement questions and are handed to the browser tier for evidence before any fix — none was changed speculatively. Gate: `test:all` exit 0 — 239 files / 1 265 passed / 3 skipped, lint 0 warnings / 0 errors.

- **New: blocked story choices explain themselves, normal play stops narrating, the save rule is stated before it bites, and the first load is 29 kB lighter (roadmap U2, U5, T4, T2 + objective O2)** — four open roadmap rows closed in one pass, each with a red-before proof and no save-format, protocol or cadence change. **U2/O2 (explainable, recoverable blocked actions):** the signature story card rendered every choice as an enabled button and never asked the story owner, so an unaffordable answer (the travelling theatre's hospitality food or venue wood, the deer parliament's preserve wood or treaty food, the wedding's gifts, feast and delegation, the invention fair's funding) looked clickable and — because `respondToStoryEvent` re-queues a refused answer — stayed on screen refusing in silence. The card now asks `getStoryChoiceEligibility` per answer, disables what the owner refuses, and prints the owner's own words inline; the raid and diplomacy cards were already doing this, and the story card was the one that never did. The same pass introduces one typed shape for "can the player do this right now?", `src/game/actionOutcome.ts` (`kind` · `reasonCode` · `explanation`), whose contract is that the explanation is the owner's text **verbatim** and a gate that refuses without a reason gets `null` rather than an invented one; reason codes are derived from identifiers the event already carries (`story.wedding_diplomacy.envoy`) and never from the message, so re-wording a refusal cannot change the code. `tests/actionOutcome.test.ts` (16) — one case per refusing answer (10, each with a live free alternative beside it as the negative control), the adapter contract, and source guards proven against a mirrored pre-fix `App.tsx` (4 failed pre-fix). Honest note: my first draft asserted that the wedding `envoy` answer is refused in a fresh colony; the negative control failed and showed `findWeddingEnvoy` picks any living adult, so it is now its own case with a colony of no living settlers — the assertion was wrong, not the code. **U5 (calm diagnostics):** the relationship-diagnostics snapshot was printed to the console once per in-game day in **every** session, because the owner defaults its console switch on and nothing in the app ever turned it off (the test tier silenced it by hand in two files). `App` now sets it from the existing `?debug=1` flag, so normal play is quiet while collection stays on for the drawer and the cumulative history; the stray `🗺️ [WorldGen Test]` dump that ran on every world generation is deleted. `tests/diagnosticsQuietness.test.ts` (3), the wiring guard red-before proven against a mirrored pre-fix `App.tsx`. **T4 (clear save-version experience):** the About box said "Saves may break between updates", which is neither the rule nor an explanation. The save owner now owns the sentence — `describeSaveCompatibility()` names the running build and says a save from another build is refused with its reason instead of half-loading — and the menu renders it, so the pre-emptive text and the refusal message (`describeSaveReadFailure`) cannot drift apart. `tests/saveVersionExperience.test.ts` (3). **T2 (smaller initial game bundle), measured:** `SelectedBuildingPanel` is the largest UI module (~51 kB of source) and renders only once a building is selected, so it moved behind `React.lazy` + `Suspense`: the entry chunk `index-*.js` went **197.90 → 169.10 kB** (gzip 54.81 → 47.16) and the panel became a **28.44 kB** (gzip 8.32) async chunk. `GameMenu`'s static `RoadmapPanel` import was aligned with `MoreTabPanel`'s pre-existing lazy load and measured **neutral** — that module was already in an async chunk, so it is recorded as a consistency fix, not a win. Current sizes for the record: `game-ui` 693.83 kB / gzip 222.33, `game-render` 260.43, `gameWorker` 575.79 (the roadmap's 2026-09-17 figures are stale in both directions). **Roadmap-01 residuals:** four UI-audit rows re-verified live and closed — R30 the chronicle header printed the raw stored filter id mid-sentence ("Showing 500 of 823 death") instead of the label the dropdown shows, R31 the header resource chip announced a bare number to screen readers with its unit and "full, production is lost" warning locked in a `title`, R32 the progress sub-nav conveyed selection by colour alone, R33 the collapsed build rail's grid toggle did too. `tests/uiRefinement.residuals.test.ts` (5, red-before 4/5). The same sweep re-checked R1–R29 and found twenty-three of them **already fixed** by the 2026-09-17 campaign (their row comments and guards are in the code); twelve remain open and are now enumerated in the roadmap's 01 row. **Also fixed:** the repository's single lint warning — `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` on `useGamePersistence.ts`'s unmount cleanup. The rule's remedy (capture `.current` at effect setup) would have **removed** the R1 data-loss guard, because that effect runs on mount when no loop exists yet, so a captured value would be `null` on every real teardown; the cleanup keeps reading the live ref and carries a scoped suppression with that reason. Gate: `test:all` exit 0 — 239 files / 1 261 passed / 3 skipped, **lint 0 warnings / 0 errors**.

- **Developer: the durable-state contract matrix (roadmap O3) now exists, and a seeded worker-vs-main-thread parity gate backs it** — O3's question is *"does a locally correct owner still hold its state when it crosses the worker boundary, the tick delta, the rollback payload and a save/load round trip?"*, because a field can be written perfectly by its owner and still be lost at any of those four seams. `docs/SAVE_DELTA_CONTRACT_MATRIX.md` answers it for **20 durable field families** — calendar/identity, world aggregates, economy, stories/cards/campaign, elections, animal care and migration, residence, workforce and schedule, relationships/family, pregnancy and Moon Howler, defense, venue schedules, requests/visitors/quests, ecology and valley stage, research, village forge, trade routes and build totals, festivals, world events/weather/challenges, and chronicle/stats/notify markers — each with its mutation owner and cadence, delta extract→apply, prep rollback, save key, load normalization, UI reader, invalid-input behaviour, and the test that pins it; plus the deliberately non-durable state and why (host-authored UI fields, runtime caches, presentation events, the compact map descriptor, the RNG snapshot) and a six-step checklist for adding a durable field. Building it surfaced **six residuals, recorded rather than repaired** because each is owner-decided or outside the slice: `disasters` rides the prep payload and the delta but is absent from the save allow-list (dormant while `HOSTILE_WEATHER_ENABLED = false`, and it must join the allow-list first if weather is ever re-enabled); `villageCanHeat` is likewise delta+prep-only and its readers default a missing value to `true`, so a mid-day load reads as able-to-heat until the next daily tick; `ENTITY_PERSISTED_FIELDS` is a **re-apply** list rather than the persistence boundary (saved entities are restored whole), which was undocumented; terrain is regenerated from a compact descriptor, so footprint clears do not persist (the decided boundary); `decisionRegistry.ts`'s `pregnancyBirth` row names `tests/phase678.regression.test.ts`, which does not exist; and family-reference cleanup is measured in the test tier rather than asserted by `collectSimulationInvariants`. The missing seeded parity check is now `tests/workerMainThread.parity.test.ts`: one 240-tick scenario run twice — on the main thread and through the real `gameWorker` module in a **second module registry** (`vi.resetModules()`), hand-off shaped exactly like `GameWorkerHost` (`world.simRng = snapshotSimRng()`) — asserting that both realms hold identical RNG stream positions **and** produce identical save payloads. Red-before: removing `restoreSimRng(nextWorld.simRng)` from `gameWorker.resetWorkerSession` fails it at the stream-position assertion, the worker's snapshot missing the `worldGen`/`entityFactory`/`nameLoader`/`settlerTraits` streams it rewound past; the source was restored byte-identically (sha256 compared). No behaviour, save-format, protocol or UI change. Gate: `test:all` exit 0 — 235 files / 1 234 passed / 3 skipped, lint 0 errors.

- **Developer: dependency-cruiser is gone — the import-cycle gate is now an in-repo scanner that cannot pass while verifying nothing** — the 2026-09-16 audit found `npm run audit:deps*` reporting `✔ no dependency violations found (0 modules, 0 dependencies cruised)` **and exiting 0**: dependency-cruiser 18 refuses the pinned `typescript@7`, so it never parsed a single `.ts` file while printing a green tick. The replacement is `scripts/check-import-cycles.mjs`, and it is deliberately paranoid about exactly that failure: it **asserts its own coverage** (at least 50 modules and one resolved edge, or it fails), treats **runtime** cycles and imports that reach into `tests/` as fatal, and reports the type-only cycle it finds as a non-fatal warning under `--strict`, because `WorldState` holds class-typed grid fields that take `WorldState` back — so `--strict` means one concrete thing: the runtime graph is acyclic. Every `audit:deps*` script now runs it (`audit:deps:cycles:strict` = `--strict`, `audit:deps:graph` = `--json`), the dead `dependency-cruiser` devDependency and its `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` config are now deleted (lockfile refreshed — zero remaining references), the unused `ts-call-graph` devDependency went with them, and `tw-animate-css` is declared in `knip.ts` `ignoreDependencies` with its reason, so `npm run audit:knip` no longer reports it as unused. Measured on this tree: **335 modules / 1 619 runtime dependencies, 0 runtime cycles**, one type-only cycle reported. The guard is proved non-vacuous by pointing it at an empty tree: it prints `Import graph: 0 modules, 0 runtime dependencies` and fails with `Import-cycle gate FAILED: cruised 0 modules / 0 dependencies (minimum 50 modules and 1 dependency). A gate that verifies nothing must not pass.` (exit 1). Also fixed in the same pass: `knip` reported `tailwindcss` and `tw-animate-css` as unused dependencies — a **false positive** (Tailwind v4 is consumed from `src/index.css` via `@import "tailwindcss"`, which knip's JS/TS follower cannot see; it says so in its own configuration hint) that made `npm run audit` red for a reason nobody should act on, and removing either package breaks the build. They are now declared in `knip.ts` `ignoreDependencies` with the reason, so `npm run audit`'s remaining signal is real: 88 unused exports + 12 unused exported types, which is the tracked duplication row C4/C6, not a false positive. The unused `ts-call-graph` devDependency was removed with them.

- **New: Workforce Policy Presets (F3), an Infrastructure & Logistics overlay (F4), Settlement Legacy Goals (F5), and two inspector explanations (P3, P5)** — the first features built from the roadmap's own feature table, each verified before it was called done. **Workforce Policy Presets (F3):** Survival / Growth / Defense / Comfort now steer which job building receives the next idle worker. One owner, `src/game/workforcePolicy.ts`, holds a single data-driven `PRESET_BUILDING_ORDER` table (no per-preset branches), and `survival` is the historical order verbatim, so `DEFAULT_WORKFORCE_POLICY` changes nothing until the player picks. The new durable field `WorldState.workforcePolicy` carries the full contract — save allow-list, `simDelta` extract/apply, `simPrep` key/extract/apply, and a documented default for pre-F3 saves (no version bump) — and changes travel as a typed `setWorkforcePolicy` worker command. **Manual assignment stays authoritative**: the auto pass only adds idle workers, rebalancing donates only from buildings holding two or more, and a building set to manual staffing is never touched, all pinned by `tests/workforcePolicy.presets.test.ts` (10; red-before 2/10 — `preset "growth" must staff its first-choice workplace: expected 'farm' to be 'lumberMill'`). The panel sits beside the work-schedule panel, because the policy is colony-wide rather than per building. **Logistics overlay (F4):** hotkey **X** (also a map toggle with a legend, a shortcuts-sheet entry and a Guide entry) draws a read-only overlay of road connectivity, buildings with poor or no road access, and commute pressure — computed by the pure projection `src/game/logisticsOverlayData.ts` with named thresholds (`GOOD_ROAD_ACCESS_DISTANCE` 40, `MAX_ROAD_ACCESS_DISTANCE` 120, `ROAD_CONNECT_GAP` 26, and the commute budget reused from `WORK_COMMUTE_LEAD_HOURS`/`COMMUTE_SNAP_DISTANCE` rather than restated), drawn per-frame so it is never baked into the tick-keyed entity layer. Presentation only: no simulation writes, no save impact, and the overlay is asserted to touch no canvas property while off. `tests/logisticsOverlay.projection.test.ts` (13; red-before 6/12). **Legacy goals (F5):** the Valley Chronicle now shows four achievements — ❄️ Winter Survivor, 🗳️ A Promise Kept, ⛪ Curse Breaker, 🍞 Out of the Lean Days — and every one is **derived** from data the simulation already records (a closed calendar year, the promise owner's own verdict line, the cure line the Moon Howler writes, a year that closed on an empty larder with food back above the recovery threshold). Deliberately no new save field: every source is already durable, so the projection round-trips for free, and the decision plus the 2 000-entry event-log bound is documented in `src/game/legacyGoals.ts`. `tests/legacyGoals.test.ts` (12), each goal with an adjacent-but-wrong negative control (mid-winter, a 0-of-2 term, a failed rite, a lean-but-not-empty year). **Inspector explanations (P3, P5):** the building inspector explains its staffing — auto or manual, capacity, current workers, and the owner's verbatim reason when a settler is refused — and the settler inspector traces the current route as named stops (home → workplace → venue → home) with the current leg and an explicit blocked/rerouting state. Both are read-only projections over the existing owners (`getWorkerAssignmentRefusal`, `getRouteObstruction`, `isAtActivityTarget`), so the gates and the route test exist once instead of in the view; no state or schema field was added. `tests/inspectorExplanations.test.ts` (7; red-before 7/7). Gate after all five: `npm run test:all` **exit 0**, `npx tsc` clean on both projects, and the campaign tracker `docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/LIVE-FINDINGS-STATUS.md` carries each feature's red-before proof.

- **Fixed: two UI collisions found by the read-only UI audit (2026-09-17)** — **the logistics toggle painted over the minimap:** both are anchored `bottom-4 left-4` in the same positioned parent, and the logistics panel (added by F4 an hour earlier) carried `z-20` and `pointer-events-auto` while the minimap frame has no z-index and renders first, so the panel covered the map thumbnail — and its "click to go" hit area, because it swallowed the pointer events. It now stacks above the minimap. **The Valley overview never claimed the keyboard:** it traps focus (`useModalFocus`) but never called `claimKeyboard`, which the dashboard and game menu both do, so with the overview open the gameplay hotkeys (1–9, B, G, H, R, X, +/-, WASD) still acted on the map hidden behind it; it now claims and releases ownership like the other two overlays.

- **Fixed: the duplication sweep — eleven "one rule, two definitions" findings now have a single owner each, and four more are advanced (A1, A3, A5–A8, A10, A11, A15, A16, F5, G3 fixed; A4, A13, G4, G5 part-fixed)** — the 2026-09-16 audit's duplication pass, worked row by row with a red-before proof each (record: `docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/LIVE-FINDINGS-STATUS.md`). **A1 (HIGH, the one with teeth):** `simHelpers.getMultiplier` SUMMED research `effect.add` values while `combat.researchedEffect` took `Math.max`, and the 2026-09-13 fix for "counter_attack 0.45 + 0.55 = 1.0 → every predator contact a guaranteed kill" had been made by *duplicating* the resolver — so any `getMultiplier` consumer reading those keys re-armed it. One owner now, `simHelpers.resolveResearchEffect`, with the additive semantics named explicitly by `TIERED_ADD_TARGETS` (`counter_attack`, `predator_block`: tiers replace lower ones); `combat`'s copy is deleted and its five readers delegate. Red-before `expected 2.67 to be close to 1.72`. **A3 (HIGH, and the audit understated it):** the colony-human rule was hand-copied in ~30 places, and the copies used `!faction` where the owner `playerHuman.isPlayerHuman` has a typed foreign-faction allow-list — which is a *live* divergence, because ~40 fixtures and `scripts/balance-militia.ts` mark player ownership `faction: 'player'` (outside `Entity['faction']`), so the copies excluded settlers the owner counts. The allow-list is now a `Record<NonNullable<Entity['faction']>, boolean>`, so a new faction is a compile error until classified (probe → `error TS2741`). **A6:** reputation had ~40 raw writers with three clamping policies; all 40 now call `simHelpers.addReputation` with every amount, sign and guard kept verbatim (verified identical for every value in 0..100; only a reputation already outside the band moves, which is the audit's "Reputation 140" defect). **A5:** the missing-ecosystem-score default had four values (`?? 80`, `?? 100`, `?? 0`, and one inline) — it is now one owner with the neutral midpoint `UNKNOWN_ECOSYSTEM_HEALTH = 50`, because a missing metric must read as neither a healthy valley nor a collapsed one. **A7** (village statistics aggregated twice → `uiSimSummary.computeVillageStats`), **A8** (the dead `Time.TICKS_PER_DAY` deleted; the clock derives it), **A10** (`PREDATOR_TYPES` had different membership in the sim and the audio layer — `combat` exports the sim set and audio composes `HUNT_NOISE_SOURCE_TYPES = [...PREDATOR_TYPES, Human]`, so a new predator is heard without a new branch), **A11** ("what is a wall" was defined three times, not twice: `stripJunction`'s private set and `stripTopology.WALL_STRIP_TYPES` both deleted in favour of `defenseStructures.isWallBuildingType`), **A15** (hospital reputation split across two modules with two descriptions → one owner in `hospitalCare`, both panels render its description), **A16** (`formatRaidDeadlineSafe` was a divergent wrapper around the owner's own legacy fallback; the owner now handles the unknown case and `raidUtils.ts` is deleted), **F5** (the placement ghost never said *why*: `getPlaceBuildingFailureReason` already returned `terrain | blocked | research | unique`, but the ghost carried only `valid` and the renderer printed one fixed string — `viewState.createBuildGhost` now carries the reason as a discriminated union, `buildingPlacementLabels.ts` owns the wording in the game layer, and `buildPreview` renders it;
