Bridge/Fishing Spot name the whole-footprint water rule), **G3** (`TICKS_PER_DAY` was a bare literal in 21 test sites across 19 files, including two re-implemented production conversions — all now import the owner, guarded by `tests/ticksPerDay.literals.test.ts`). **Part-fixed, stated rather than smoothed:** **A4**'s two named world→screen copies delegate to `viewState.worldToScreen`, but the same one-line expression is still inline in ~11 other renderer modules; **A13**'s façade hop is removed but the middle re-export sits in a file another worker still owns; **G4** moved 39 fixture-factory declaration sites onto a new type-checked `src/test/factories.ts` (no `as Entity` casts — the field the copies silently omitted, `maxAge`/`reproductionCooldown`, is now set once from its owner) with ~25 sites left; **G5**'s calendar half is done and a `FIXTURE_SEED` collision (one name, two values) remains. Verification for the batch: `npm run test:all` **225 files / 1134 passed / 3 skipped, lint 0 warnings / 0 errors**, `npm run test:full-year` **exit 0** (360 days, invariants satisfied), `npx tsc` clean on both projects.

- **Fixed: four duplicated rules — two of them already lying in the test tier — now have one owner each (duplication A2, G1, G2, G3)** — the 2026-09-16 audit's duplication pass found the same rule written twice in several places; this closes three of its rows and advances the fourth. **A test that lied (G1, CRITICAL):** `tests/huntLogic.test.ts` re-implemented two **exported** production functions — its own comment called `freeHuntFoodGain` "module-private" — and its hand-copied yield table knew only Deer (52) and Rabbit (22), so every other prey type fell through to the 18 fallback: Wolf was asserted as 18 where production prices it 45, Fox as 18 where production prices it 28. Both copies are deleted; the file imports `freeHuntFoodGain` (`simulation/humanNeeds`) and `isValidHuntPrey` (`simulation/simulationEntities`), and the new case asserts an **ordering** against a prey type with no table entry — "prices every tabled prey type above the fallback for untabled ones" — so it restates no tuning value and cannot silently agree with a future copy: it failed against the pre-fix body (`expected 18 to be greater than 18`) and the file is 7/7. **A fixture detaching from the rule it drives (G2):** `conceptionEvent.labelling.test.ts`'s live-delivery helper searched for a stillbirth roll `>= 0.5` while the owner's threshold is `LIFECYCLE_CONFIG.STILLBORN_CHANCE` (0.001). That condition is a strict subset today, so it picked a genuine live birth and the test was not *yet* lying — which is exactly why the defect was invisible: retune the constant above 0.5 and the test would silently drive the **stillbirth** branch while asserting live-birth labels. It now compares against the owner, pinned twice: the returned tick must clear the threshold, and a source guard that the helper body names the owner and carries no `>= 0.x` literal. Reverting the helper left **1/6 failed** (`the helper no longer asks the owner for the threshold`) while the behavioural case still passed, and the source was restored byte-identical (sha256). **A selection rule written twice (A2):** `residencyReconciliation.pickSharedResidence` carried its own copy of "least-occupied residence, ties to the lowest id". `residencySelection.pickLeastCrowdedResidence` is now the exported owner and takes the movers as `movingTogether`, which expresses the shared-move discount (a home one mover already lives in counts them once) through the owner rather than beside it; the caller delegates. `tests/residenceBestFit.owner.test.ts` (4) pins it: three **equivalence** cases written from the old copy's semantics — they stayed green when the duplicate was put back, so they pin the generalization rather than the de-duplication — and the source guard, which is the one that went red (`the shared-residence caller no longer delegates to the owner`); source restored byte-identical. **Day length in the test tier (G3, part):** the two re-implemented production conversions are gone — `saveMigration.roundtrip.test.ts`'s `getAbsDay` (`Math.floor(state.tick / 72)`) calls `getAbsoluteCalendarDay(state.tick)`, and `migration.herds.test.ts`'s `setDay` is `day * TICKS_PER_DAY`; 14 value-identical literal sites remain and are enumerated in the tracker row. Scope, stated plainly: three of the four are test-tier truth and the fourth is an internal de-duplication whose only new behaviour is the movers-discount path the old copy already implemented line for line (the three equivalence cases demonstrate that), so nothing player-visible is intended to change here — the 360-day signature that moved during this pass belongs to the concurrent `building.x/y` coordinate repair, the change the handover predicted *would* move it. Per-finding record: `docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/LIVE-FINDINGS-STATUS.md`.

- **Fixed: the `building.x/y` footprint convention is settled — the stored point is the centre, and the three seams that read it as a corner are repaired** — the 2026-09-16 audits found two families disagreeing about what `building.x/y` means, and the answer decides real behaviour, so it was settled against the arbiter the player sees rather than by majority: the pad and sprite are drawn from `x - w/2`/`y - h/2` (`spriteDrawing.drawBuildingPad`/`drawBuildingSprite`, `drawSpriteFrame` with `anchorX = 0.5`), every strip is filled from `translate(sx, sy)` into `[-rw/2, +rw/2]` (`stripRender.beginRotatedStripFrame`, so roads, walls and fences are centred too), the placement ghost's footprint rectangle is stroked around the ghost point (`renderer/buildPreview.ts`), the bounds/terrain/overlap checks all validate `x ± w/2`, and the snapped point the player clicks is stored unchanged and then drawn with the ghost's own geometry. Six independent player-visible seams agree, and the ghost-under-the-cursor argument is decisive on its own — the corner readers are the wrong ones. Repaired here: **(1)** `clearTreesUnderFootprint` cleared `x … x + width`/`y … y + height`, a rectangle half a footprint south-east of the pad, so a tree standing inside the pad survived (inside the new building) while a tree *beside* the building was felled for no reason; it now reads the new single owner `placementUtils.getBuildingFootprintRect`. **(2)** `simulation/humanRelationships` exported a **second `getBuildingCenter` with the same name and the opposite answer** (`x + w/2`), so every proximity test in that file — `isNearBuilding`, the affair tryst radius, the tryst rendezvous point — measured from a spot half a building to the south-east of the building on screen; the duplicate is deleted and the owner is imported. **(3)** the building renderer sampled terrain relief at the south-east corner while drawing its rect from the centre, so on a slope a building was drawn at a different height from the ghost that previewed it (the preview's own comment already claimed they agreed); it now samples the south-centre. Tests: `tests/buildingFootprint.convention.test.ts` (2) places a real building through `startBuilding` and asserts the cleared ground is exactly the drawn pad, with probe trees chosen so the two candidate rectangles disagree about them — **both failed** with the rect source reverted to the corner expression and the source came back byte-identical (sha256); `tests/affairCaught.reachability.test.ts`'s fixture no longer computes its own corner centre (its "35 px from the home" was really 55 px from the home that is drawn) and passes 8/8. **Two things this deliberately does not do, recorded rather than glossed:** the ~75 remaining centre computations across 32 files (villager steering targets, spawn points, float positions, tile indices) are enumerated in `LIVE-FINDINGS-STATUS.md` and left for their own pass — a half-migrated family is worse than a started one — and `buildingRotation.isEntityOnBuilding` (with its delegate `terrainSystems.isInsideCompletedBuilding`, which decides spawn validity, the Moon Howler cure sweep and road detection) plus `spatialGrid`'s road-avoidance buckets must move as **one unit**, because those two are internally consistent on the corner rect today and flipping only the predicate would put the road speed bonus on the wrong half of every road. **Harness, with its caveat:** with the placement fix alone the 360-day run is identical to the pre-change baseline (`docs/log/full-year-2026-09-17T18-06-40-285Z.jsonl` — day 300 = 65 settlers/16 married; final 67 · 25 births · 84 marriages · 68 divorces · 23 pregnancies); the `getBuildingCenter` delegation moves it, and a controlled A/B on the current tree (external work held constant) gives 72 · 29 · 67 · 53 · 26 without the delegation and 63 · 24 · 103 · 87 · 24 with it. **An external session was editing residency/housing in this tree at the same time**, so the drift from that recorded baseline is not this change alone, and one seed cannot size a rate change (the courtship-cooldown entry above says the same) — the direction is attributable, the magnitude is not. If the owner wants the romance-rate consequence measured, compare several seeds with and without the delegation; if the intended radii (55 px near-building, 22 px spouse-block) were tuned against the old corner geometry, re-tuning them is a balance call rather than a code defect.

- **Fixed: the production build was broken by a Tailwind version mismatch** — `vite build` failed inside `@tailwindcss/vite` with `Can't resolve 'tailwindcss' in 'C:/Wilderfolk/src'`. The top-level `tailwindcss` dependency had been repointed to `0.0.0-oxide-insiders.ff2c25f`, a build that ships **no `index.css` and no exports map**, while the plugin is `@tailwindcss/vite@4.3.3` — Tailwind v4 requires those two to be the same version, and the lockfile still carried the matching `tailwindcss@4.3.3` nested under `@tailwindcss/node`. Restoring `tailwindcss@^4.3.3` (which also removed the 61 packages the insiders build pulled in) makes `npm run build` green again — `✓ built in 1.11s`, exit 0 — and the test gate is untouched (`test:all` 212 files / 1058 passed / 3 skipped, lint 0/0). **Rule for whoever reads this next:** `tailwindcss` and `@tailwindcss/vite` must stay version-matched, and knip's "unused devDependency `tailwindcss`" is a **false positive** (it cannot follow the `@import "tailwindcss"` in `src/index.css`) — do not act on it by removing or replacing the package.

- **Fixed: toast notifications now actually leave the screen (F22)** — the audit filed this as *suspected* because it seemed to need a browser to confirm; tracing the restore path makes it a confirmed defect. Two mechanisms expired a toast: a 2-second sweep (`expireNotifications`, 12 s lifetime) and a per-toast 12-second timer. Only the timer's path (`dismissNotification`) wrote the dismissal ledger, and the display world is rebuilt from the worker every tick with `simDelta.preserveNotificationDismissals` filtering on **that ledger alone** — so the sweep's removal was undone by the very next delta, the reconciliation effect then cancelled the restored toast's timer, a fresh 12 s timer was armed, and the sweep removed it again two seconds later. The player-visible result was a toast card that blinked out and back every ~2 s and never left on its own, which is exactly what `NOTIFICATION_DISPLAY_MS` was introduced to prevent. The sweep now records the ids it drops, with the same ordering and cap as `dismissNotification`, so it is a second route into the one ledger rather than a path around it (which is what the `MAX_DISMISSED_NOTIFICATION_IDS` comment already claimed every removal did). `tests/toastExpiry.ledger.test.ts` (3) asserts the consequence rather than the bookkeeping — after the sweep, `preserveNotificationDismissals` filters the same toast out of a delta, so it cannot come back — plus that a fresh toast is untouched and a no-op sweep leaves the ledger alone: **2/3 failed** with the pre-fix sweep restored, source restored byte-identical. This closes the 2026-09-16 `ui-logic` report at zero live rows.

- **Fixed: purchase trade routes finally state what they actually pay (F13)** — the reward line rendered gold **or** stone and nothing else (`route.resourcesReceived.gold > 0 ? gold + 'g' : stone + 's'`), so every route paid in wood, food or iron advertised **"+0s per round-trip"**: `trade_8` receives wood 60, `trade_10` food 60, `trade_3` iron 15. Those are exactly the rows a wood-starved colony needs — and the same three the coin→materials fix had just re-enabled — so the panel was telling the player that the route they wanted paid nothing. `resourceTypes.formatResourceAmounts` now renders every non-zero key through the existing per-resource owner (`formatResourceAmount` + `RESOURCE_METAS`), which is the fix the audit suggested, and the panel line is a single call. `tests/tradeRouteRewards.copy.test.ts` (3) pins the wood/food/iron and mixed cases, walks **every shipped route** asserting none renders an empty or zero reward (so a balance change cannot reintroduce it silently), and fails if the gold-or-stone ternary comes back — **1/3 failed** with the ternary restored, source restored byte-identical.

- **Fixed: the council's "Notices" row can appear at all (F24), and the two "Buildings" totals are confirmed to be one number (F21)** — F24 is a real fix, F21 a verified close. **The dead notices row (F24):** `collectDashboard`'s council loop read `(e as { title?: string }).title`, a field `GameEventLog` has never had — the event log writes `message` (`eventLog.ts` is its only writer) — so the type cast silenced the compiler, the "last three things that happened" list stayed empty, and the row that renders it was unreachable code. It now reads `e.message`, so the council panel lists the previous day's most recent events by their real text. `tests/councilNotices.row.test.ts` (2) drives the previous day's event window and asserts the row carries the logged messages, that it stays absent on a quiet day, and that the `title` cast cannot return: **2/2 failed** with the cast restored — both the behaviour and the guard — and the source was restored byte-identical. **Two "Buildings" totals (F21):** the audit's disagreement is already gone — both panels now read the single field `state.totalBuildingsCompleted` instead of `state.buildings.length` and an inline `completed && faction !== 'rival'` filter. Rather than close it on sight, I checked the denormalized counter's writers, since that is what such a field lives or dies by: completion increments only for non-rival buildings, demolition decrements under the same guard, load recomputes with the same filter, and the field rides the sim delta and the worker prep snapshot. No code change, so it is recorded as closed-by-other-work rather than fixed.

- **Fixed: "center map on this building" now centres on the building (F23)** — **five** call sites passed `x + width/2, y + height/2` for the "Open Blacksmith" camera focus: the forge-paused and forge-queue alerts in the alert strip, the same pair in the Focus panel's hints (`focusHints.ts:244-245` and `:265-266`), and the Goals panel's button — so the focus landed half a building off the building the button names. (My first pass fixed three of the five, and its guard listed only two files, so it could not have caught the other pair; the completion is recorded in the tracker.) `building.x/y` **is** the footprint centre — the pad and sprite are drawn from `x - w/2`/`y - h/2`, `overlapsAnyBuilding` compares `b.x ± b.width/2`, and the map-bounds and terrain checks take `x ± w/2` — so adding half a footprint asked the camera to look at a spot half a building off the thing the button names, and the offset only looks harmless because it lands on a building-shaped area rather than empty ground. `placementUtils.getBuildingCenter` now states the convention in one place (and lists the consumers that agree, with `isEntityOnBuilding` named as the one remaining reader on the old corner assumption — its own tracked row, since it also drives spawn validity, the Moon Howler sweep and road detection), and all three sites call it. `tests/buildingFocus.centre.test.ts` (3): the accessor returns the stored point, the bounds rule demonstrates that the shifted point is a *different* place (a footprint flush with the right edge is legal at its centre and out of bounds half a footprint further right), and a guard fails if either file adds `.x + *.width / 2` again. **2/3 failed** with the offsets restored, both sources restored byte-identical; the full-year harness is unmoved (day 300 = 65/16), so the change is view-only. **One honest caveat, recorded rather than smoothed over:** the convention this fix assumes is **contested**, not settled — a full grep shows ~90 simulation sites computing a building's centre as `x + w/2` while the player-visible family (pad, sprite, ghost plates, bounds, overlap) reads `x/y` as the centre, so one of those two families is wrong everywhere. The tracker's "Other" row carries the evidence and the decisive test; nothing else was mass-edited.

- **Fixed: the visitor trade buttons can no longer be clicked into nothing, and "food is low" has one definition again (F18, F20)** — two `ui-logic` findings, both "the view re-derived a rule the game layer already owns". **Dead trade buttons (F18):** `VisitorCampPanel` recomputed the price multipliers itself and gated each button on that arithmetic, while the owning eligibility (`groupEvents.getVisitorTradeEligibility`) also rejects a **caravan that is leaving**, a **group with no gold**, and — the reachable case the panel missed — **not enough storage for what the trade receives**. With gold at its cap, "Sell food · 30🍖 → 25💰" stayed enabled; clicking it consumed nothing and pushed its message at the camp, far from the panel the player was reading. Prices were a second implementation of `VISITOR_TRADE_COSTS × getVisitorTradeTerms`, so a balance change would have desynchronised the quote from the charge. `getVisitorTradeTerms` is now exported, both prices and gates come from the owners, each disabled button carries the owner's own reason (`Need 25💰`, `They are out of gold`, `The caravan is leaving`, `Cannot store more gold`), and the 30/40/25/20 quantities come from the deal table instead of the markup. **Five "food is low" rules (F20):** the dashboard used `humans * 3`, the focus hints and the citizen mood `max(20, pop × 2)`, the alert strip and the low-food tip `max(15, pop × 1.5)`, plus the owner's own helpers — so at 2 settlers with 18 food the header badge warned and the Focus panel said "Feed the village" while the strip said nothing. `resourceUtils` now owns the numbers (`getFoodCriticalThreshold(population)`, with amount-level predicates for call sites that hold only numbers) and all five sites call it. **Two bands survive on purpose and are now stated per site:** "low" (absolute 20) drives the badge/hint/mood, "critical" (the per-settler rule with its 15 floor) drives the "react now" strip, the dashboard concern and the tip — so the strip's silence in the 15–20 band at low population is a UX decision rather than an accident of arithmetic. Tests: `tests/visitorTradePanel.eligibility.test.ts` (4, **1/4 failed** with the panel's own price arithmetic restored) and `tests/foodThresholds.owner.test.ts` (3, **3/3 failed** with the owner's rule swapped for `max(20, pop × 2)` and two sites re-deriving theirs); every reverted source was restored byte-identical.

- **Fixed: the taming panel offered settlers the command would refuse, and measured the Taming Post's reach its own way (F16, F17)** — two `ui-logic` findings that are one panel and one owner. **The tamer list (F16):** the candidate list was every adult human in the world — `faction: 'visitor'` and `'rival'` included — while `tameEntity` refuses a non-player settler with an early `return state` *before* its floating-text path, so picking a visitor's name did nothing at all, ever; the four-slot cap also let foreign adults crowd out every eligible settler. The list now comes from a new owner, `settlerInteractionActions.listTamingCandidates(state)` (living adult player humans), so what the panel offers and what the command accepts cannot drift. **The reach test (F17):** the panel measured `Math.hypot(b.x - entity.x, …) < 140` and the command's owner `hasNearbyPlayerTamingPost` measured from `b.x + width/2`, and only the panel counted a **rival-owned** post — so the panel offered taming the sim refused ("Need a Taming Post nearby" floating on the map) and hid taming the sim allowed. **The owner was the wrong one of the pair, and the drawing is the arbiter:** `spriteDrawing.drawBuildingPad`/`drawBuildingSprite` draw every footprint from `x - w/2`/`y - h/2`, `placementUtils.overlapsAnyBuilding` compares `b.x ± b.width/2`, and `isWithinMapBounds`, `isFootprintOnBuildableTerrain` and `snapBuildingCenter` all put that same point at the **centre** — so the panel's plain `b.x` was geometrically right and the command's circle reached a post-shaped area half a post away from the post on screen. The owner now measures from the centre and the panel calls it (exported), which is the agreement the finding asked for — not its literal "reuse the existing helper", because that name existed carrying the wrong geometry. (My first pass recorded the finding's direction as inverted on the strength of `renderer/buildings.ts:30` reading `sx = b.x`; that line computes the centre and `drawBuildingPad` subtracts half, so the variable name misled me and the row is corrected.) The one reader that still assumes a corner is `buildingRotation.isEntityOnBuilding`, behind `terrainSystems.isInsideCompletedBuilding` — spawn validity, the L16 howler sweep and road detection all run through it, so it is recorded as its own live row with its consumers rather than patched here. `tests/taming.candidatesAndPost.test.ts` (4) pins the candidate list, the foreign-tamer refusal, the reach geometry (a creature 130 px from the footprint centre is in reach for the command and was out of reach for the panel's corner rule) and the rival-post exclusion; it also carries both pre-fix expressions verbatim so the guards cannot pass vacuously. **1/4 failed** with the panel reverted to its own filter and reach test, and the source was restored byte-identical (sha256). Full-year harness reproduced the recorded signature (day 300 = 65/16, final 67 · 25 · 84 · 68), confirming the change is presentation-only.

- **Fixed: the intro's CTA no longer appears 11.6 s before the intro ends, and a jobless-homeless settler stops losing a suggestion row (F9, F25)** — two more 2026-09-16 audit findings, landed and tested together. **The intro finished twice (F9):** `ready: 6000` sat inside `INTRO_TIMELINE_MS`, a map in which every other entry is an offset from mount, so "Choose your land →", the "press any key" prompt and the bottom progress bar all completed at **6 seconds** — while the subtitle (9.8 s), hook (14.2 s) and food-chain (17.6 s) beats were still coming. The button appeared over a half-built title screen, jumped down the screen as the remaining beats opened, the bar claimed completion two-thirds of the way through, and any keypress after 6 s silently skipped the rest of the reveal (the listener attaches at `phase === 'ready'`). The timeline now lives in `src/game/introTimeline.ts`: the beats hold offsets only, `INTRO_READY_DELAY_MS` is the hold, and `INTRO_DURATION_MS` — the last beat plus the hold, **23 600 ms** — is what both the CTA and the progress bar read, so the arithmetic is testable instead of implied. **A dropped suggestion row (F25):** `explainSettler` pushed `label: 'Suggested'` from two independent branches (no workplace, no residence) and the dashboard keys those rows by label, so a settler who was jobless **and** homeless produced a duplicate React key and one of the two suggestions could vanish on re-render. The labels are distinct at the source (**Suggested workplace** / **Suggested home**) and the view no longer depends on label uniqueness — both label-keyed lists (the settler card and the daily-council rows) key on label + position. Tests: `tests/introTimeline.cta.test.ts` (3, **2/3 failed** with the duration back at 6 000 and a `ready` key back in the map) and `tests/dashboardData.suggestionLabels.test.ts` (2, **1/2 failed** with the shared label restored); the prisoner guard in `tests/dashboardData.prisonAndCouncil.test.ts` was re-pointed at the new label so it cannot pass vacuously, and every reverted source was restored byte-identical.

- **Fixed: a full store no longer deletes production in silence, the readiness card names the armament it counts, and "Load saved game" says why it refuses (F6, F15, F19)** — three defects from the 2026-09-16 audits, landed and tested as one group (per-finding record: `docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/LIVE-FINDINGS-STATUS.md`). **Silent storage loss (F6):** every gain goes through the clamp `addResource`, which caps at `storageMax` and returns **what it actually accepted** — and every producer in `dailyBuildingEconomy` threw that return away, so a capped Lumber Mill was indistinguishable from an idle one (measured: wood sat at exactly 800, the cap, from ~day 60 to day 290 while a staffed mill kept running) and the one helper that answers "is this store full?", `isResourceCapped`, had no consumer at all. Production now routes through a single `addProductionOutput`, which announces a refused gain and returns the accepted amount for the ledger and skill gate — including a **partial** clamp (5 of 30 accepted was silent even where a total one was announced) — and the Workshop, which on a full gold store consumed nothing and said nothing, now says why. The Fishing Spot and Workshop fold the refusal into their own float rather than stacking a second one at the same point. `isResourceCapped` gained its two consumers: the production helper and the HUD, where all five `ResourceBadge`s now ring amber and title the resource "full, production is lost until you spend it". **Wrong armament (F15):** `FrontierPanel` restated the armament tiers by hand and knew two of five, so a village with iron swords — or with shields but no spear tech, which is reachable because `iron_shields` carries no forge prerequisite — read **"Militia 132 · 🏹 Unarmed adults"** while the strength beside it was computed *from* those same multipliers. It now asks the owner `getMilitiaArmamentLabel`, which `frontierCombat` already used. **A button that did nothing, forever (F19):** the new-settlement screen's "Load saved game" called `loadGame()`, which collapsed every cause — clear slot, blocked storage, another build, parsed-but-unrestorable — into `null` and then only refreshed a boolean, so the click produced no toast, no navigation and no visible reason on any retry while the button stayed enabled. The menu path already named its cause, so that is now the owner both paths read: `saveLoad.loadGameOutcome()` returns the session **or** the reason (with a new `unrestorable` case) and `describeSaveLoadOutcome` is the single home of the messages; the reason-discarding `loadGame` and its re-export are deleted. The guard for it caught a **third** copy of the "parsed but could not be restored" string, in the load-from-**file** path, which the audit had not named. Tests: `tests/storageCapped.productionAnnounced.test.ts` (3, **3/3 failed** with the lumber site reverted — `the mill deleted its output without a word`), `tests/militiaArmament.label.test.ts` (3), `tests/saveLoad.outcomeReasons.test.ts` (4, **1/4 failed** with the setup path reverted); every reverted source was restored byte-identical.

- **Balance — the baseline pace is now 1 tick/s: an in-game day is 72 real seconds at 1×** — the owner's pacing call (*"3 ticks 72 sec per day"*, *"24 × 3"*), and it leaves the calendar alone: the grid stays `HOURS_PER_DAY (24) × TICKS_PER_HOUR (3) = 72 ticks/day`, while `gameLoop.BASE_TICKS_PER_SECOND` goes `1.5 → 1`, so an in-game hour is 3 real seconds and a day is 72 (0.5× ≈ 144 s, 2× ≈ 36 s, 3× ≈ 24 s, 10× ≈ 7.2 s; a 360-day year ≈ 7.2 h at 1×). This **supersedes** the "slower baseline pacing" value of 48 s/day, the same decision whose silent reversion to 24 s/day was repaired as a defect (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-baseline-pacing-reverted-to-24s-day.md`, now annotated as superseded — its record of *how* the revert survived a green gate still stands). Scheduler-only: ticks, save format, worker protocol and every simulation cadence are untouched, so no save migrates. Every source that reasoned from 48 s moved with the constant (`gameLoop`'s own doc comment, `villageLeadership`'s election-term arithmetic — a year is ~7.2 h at 1×, ~2.4 h at a nominal 3× — `buildingProgressDisplay`, `renderer/buildings` and the ramp's test comment), and the three harness copies of the rate (`scripts/test.ts`, `scripts/perf-all.ts` and `scripts/probe-day-budget.mts`, whose printed real-time table was 2× too long) are now **guarded**: `tests/gameLoop.pacingContract.test.ts` (5) asserts `1` and 72 000 ms/day and fails if any copy drifts. Reverting the constant to `1.5` fails all five; the source was then restored byte-identical (sha256).
- **Fixed: a colony whose beds ran out said nothing (F10)** — the priority strip spoke about housing only when **no** house was standing, so the ordinary case was silent: immigration outruns housing by design, and a settler with no `residenceBuildingId` never gets the home-rest saving in `humanNeeds` while nothing on screen says so. `getPriorityAlerts` now raises `housing-short` whenever a player settler has no bed *and* a residence stands, naming the count and offering the House build action; it escalates `info → warning` at `HOMELESS_WARNING_THRESHOLD = 3` so a one-settler gap cannot crowd the tier the raid and diplomacy cards use. **Prisoners are excluded on purpose** — imprisonment clears `residenceBuildingId`, which is exactly why `collectHousingDiagnostics`' `unassignedPlayerHumans` still counts jailed settlers as homeless (the audit's route to this finding; noted in the code comment). `tests/priorityAlerts.housing.test.ts` (4) fails 2/4 when the alert can never fire, and the source was restored byte-identical.

## <u>[0.6.4.1]</u>

- **Fixed: eleven presentation defects from the 2026-09-16 visuals audit (D16–D27), one audit finding withdrawn, and one left to the weather rewrite** — all presentation; no simulation, save or cadence change. **A child's painted figure was not scale-normalised (D16):** the four `child_*` PNGs carry 0 %–12 % transparent top padding, so one shared `spriteH` drew their figures at up to 14 % different apparent height — `spriteH` is the *canvas* height and the figure inside it is only `fraction` of that. `humanSprites.ts` now carries each PNG's measured figure fraction (from its alpha bounding box) and the draw site grows the sprite box by `1 / fraction`, so all four figures come out at the shared height. `spriteH` itself stays figure-sized, which keeps `headY` — and with it the speech bubble, status badge, contact shadow and selection ring — measured against the figure instead of the padding; adults are untouched. **The decor build preview showed a straight rectangle (D17):** Garden/Statue/Lamp declare `sprite:` paths whose art does not exist on disk — they are drawn procedurally once placed — so `drawBuildPreview` fell through to its validity-coloured `fillRect`. It now calls the same `drawProceduralDecor` the placed building uses, at the same footprint offset. **The entity-layer cache key omitted inputs its painters read (D18):** `state.selectedEntityIds` (`humans.ts`/`animals.ts` draw a ring per id) and `state.juiceEffectsEnabled` (contact-shadow strength) are keyed now, so a shift-click that changes only a non-primary id — or toggling the preference — no longer leaves a stale bitmap for a whole tick, or indefinitely while paused. `state.season` was deliberately **not** added: `gameTick` advances `state.tick` and assigns `state.season` in the same call and `tick` is already keyed, so the audit's "the grass palette lags one tick" cannot happen. **The leader's aura froze between rebakes (D19):** its pulse runs on `renderTime`, which advances every frame, but it was painted into the tick-keyed entity bitmap (one repaint per sim tick, none while paused); it is now drawn by `drawLeaderAuraOverlay` from the per-frame overlay pass, re-deriving the same leader the cached pass drew for (zoom floor, asleep-at-home, and the zoomed-out marker that draws its own crown). **Ghost and strip previews ignored terrain relief (D23) and the build-grid plate sat on the wrong cell (D27):** the ghost sprite, the ghost plate and wall/road previews sample `terrainRiseAt` on the same footprint point (centre-x / bottom-y) the placed building samples, and the coarse validity plates are drawn at the *snapped* centre their verdict is computed from instead of the raw 100 wu major-cell centre — a different cell, since placement snaps to `GRID_SIZE = 20`. **Two defects in the dormant Pixi path (D20, D22)** behind `USE_PIXI_GROUND = false`: the seasonal animation now takes `renderTime` rather than the page wall clock (it survived new games, save loads and tab-outs), and a boundary tile feathers *every* differing neighbour with the alpha split across them instead of blending whichever of N/E/S/W came first and hard-edging the rest. **Three measured fixes:** `huntingspot` and `storehouse_wood` gained the `spriteAnchorY` their art implies (0.847 / 0.882 — the same `paintedBase − 0.08` rule LeaderHouse already carried), so they stop standing ~4–6 % of sprite height proud of the ground line [D24]; the progress-smoothing sample map drops finished buildings and is capped, instead of holding one entry per building id ever displayed for the life of the session [D25]; and the floating-text fade and shrink ramps share one named `FLOATING_TEXT_FADE_TICKS` instead of the drifted 7 and 6 [D26]. **Withdrawn: D21 is not a defect.** The audit read `stampPropSprite`'s two source-identical `drawImage` calls as a no-op flip, but the `flipX` branch runs *after* `ctx.scale(-1, 1)` and the destination rect is symmetric about the mirror axis, so the rect maps onto itself and the image **content** is mirrored — props already face both ways, and the audit's suggested fix (draw at `+drawW / 2`) would have displaced every flipped prop by a full sprite width. The code is unchanged and `tests/visualsAudit.renderWiring.test.ts` now pins the transform so the suggestion is not applied later. **D15 (weather particles are frame-rate driven) is reported but untouched:** the owner is replacing weather next version. Guards: `tests/visualsAudit.renderWiring.test.ts` now has 26 tests, gaining a describe per finding (D16–D27) plus two new non-vacuous cases, including behavioural checks for the child-figure invariant, the layer key — with the pre-fix field list reimplemented to prove it could not tell those states apart — and the D21 transform.

- **Fixed: four live defects from the 2026-09-16 economy audit (H1, H3, M1, M3)** — all four are game code, and the per-finding record (evidence, line numbers, red-before counts) is `docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/LIVE-FINDINGS-STATUS.md`, which is the tracker the owner asked for in place of per-finding reports. **Ironport never paid its iron (H1):** `canStoreImports`/`applyImports` credited four of the five resources a trade route sends, so the route whose entire point is iron delivered none of it; the route's own `resourcesReceived.iron` was computed and then dropped. **A rival inflated the player's militia (H3):** the `chivalrous` bonus filtered on `isPlayerHuman` in the adult count but not in the trait count, so a *hostile raider's* trait raised the player's `militiaStrength` and fed straight into `resolveDefenseRatio` — while the label advertised `N × 8 %` for a bonus that is a single flat +8 %. **Prisoners were mustered (M1):** `isImprisoned` guards every staffing rule and both election paths, but not the militia — a jailed settler added a full adult's militia value, was drawn as a barricade fighter, could be selected as a raid casualty "defending the village", and was flashed on the map as fighting. Fixed by doing what the audit's own closing note recommended: `militiaBalance.canBeMustered` is now the single muster decision, used by the adult count, the fighter pool, the casualty pool, the chivalrous bonus **and** `frontierCombat.flashMilitia` — a fourth site the audit did not name, which receives the whole alive list and so decides who *appears* to fight. **Challenge rewards never paid their iron (M3):** `tickDailyChallenges` hand-wrote four `addResource` calls and skipped `iron`, so `century`'s "+500 all resources + 500 iron!" arrived as 0 iron — permanently, since the `completed` latch is saved — for the one resource that gates the forge chain. `tests/tradeImports.iron.test.ts`, `tests/militiaBalance.chivalrous.test.ts`, `tests/militiaBalance.prisoners.test.ts` and `tests/dailyChallenges.rewards.test.ts` (13 tests together) each fail with the fix reverted — the M3 suite leads with a probe reward carrying *every* `Resources` field so it pins the class and not the one resource — and every reverted source was restored byte-identically (sha256 compared). Presentation, cadence and save format unchanged.
- **Fixed: the food ledger recorded the nominal catch, not what storage accepted (M5) — and a chapter reward that entered storage unrecorded** — two sides of the same contract, `economyLedger`'s rule that a `produced` entry is "food that actually entered storage". **Over-recording (audit M5):** the Hunting and Fishing Spot branches called `addResource(state, 'food', amount)` for its return value and threw it away, then recorded `amount` — the audit's example, 795/800 food with a 30-food catch, credited 30 while the store took 5, so the "why is my food low?" panel and the 30-day `foodHistory` over-stated production. Both branches now record the accepted amount, and the float and chronicle lines (`+${added} meat`, `… hauled in ${added} fish`) carry it too: an explicit 2-line extension of the audit's fix, disclosed rather than silent, because they made the same false claim to the player. **Under-recording — found while fixing it, not in the audit:** `advanceValleyChronicle` credited every chapter reward through `addCappedResource` and never told the ledger, so three chapters' food (`first_harvest` +100, `the_hunt` +80, `the_river` +80) really did enter storage with nothing explaining it. The M5 fixture is what surfaced it: a naive "one below the cap" test passed **vacuously**, because the chapter reward filled the store before the hunters returned, so the fixed and unfixed builds both recorded `Stores full!` and nothing else. The test therefore calibrates that unrecorded gain by measurement and asserts differentially (`tight < roomy`), which is immune to spoilage and to the caps being recomputed mid-pass. `tests/foodLedger.acceptedCatch.test.ts` (2, one per site) fails 2/2 with only the two ledger arguments reverted — `expected 81 to be less than 81`, `expected 40 to be less than 40`, the pre-fix ledger recording the identical nominal in both runs — and `tests/valleyChronicle.foodLedger.test.ts` (1) fails 1/1 with the record call disabled. Both sources were restored byte-identically afterwards.
- **Fixed: the opening card could never appear (M4), and a mid-year succession made its promises inert (M7)** — two more live findings from the same audit, both in game code. **"The valley wakes" (M4):** `maybeOfferWelcome` required `year === 0 && dayInYear === 0`, which no reachable tick satisfies — `getCalendarDay` is `floor(tick / TICKS_PER_DAY) % DAYS_PER_YEAR`, so `dayInYear === 0` recurs only at ticks 0, 25 920, 51 840 …; the daily layer runs on exactly those ticks; a colony starts at tick 24, so tick 0 never happens; and on every such tick `yearRollover` increments `state.year` **before** the daily layer runs. The opening beat, its two choices and its `resolveWelcome` effects had therefore never fired in a real game, only in tests that called the function directly with `dayInYear = 0`. The gate now accepts day 1, where the first daily boundary of a new colony lands, and the existing `welcome` flag keeps it once-only. `tests/storyEvents.welcome.test.ts` (2) drives the real path — 48 `gameTick`s from tick 24 to tick 72 — and was written **before** the fix, failing 2/2 with `storyFlags.welcome` still `undefined`. **Promises (M7):** every promise flag is keyed by calendar year (`election_promises_<year>_0/1`, `…_eval_day`, `…_active_year`) except the "already judged" marker, and `recordElectionPromises` overwrote the first three while leaving the marker set. A leader dying after that year's term election therefore re-recorded promises under the same keys with a fresh 180-day window that both `tickElectionPromises` and `getActiveElectionPromises` early-returned on — inert promises, no reputation swing, no panel. The owner now clears the marker with the promises it replaces, so each election carries its own window (the codes and window are overwritten in the same call, so nothing is judged twice); keying promises by election instance instead is recorded in the code comment as the structural alternative. `tests/electionPromises.secondElection.test.ts` (1) drives term election → judged → same-year re-record through the public API and fails 1/1 with the clear reverted. Both sources restored byte-identically.
- **Fixed: campaign promises are now visible, tutorial tips no longer delete themselves, the request card says why it refuses, and the campaign's last step points somewhere real (F4, F7, F12, F13)** — four playability fixes, plus one audit finding **withdrawn** because the code disproves it. **Promises (F4):** `getActiveElectionPromises` existed with no consumer anywhere in `src/`, so the player learned their commitments from a single chronicle line at the swearing-in and were scored on them 180 game days later with no way to check. The Village-head panel now shows a **Campaign promises** block — each promise's label, `current / target` and a progress bar, plus "Judged in N days" — with the percentage computed by the owner (`getPromiseDetail` gained a `pct` field) rather than in the view. **Self-destructing tips (F7):** a tip was auto-acknowledged after 20 real seconds, which marked it permanently seen in `localStorage` *and* in the save's `tutorialSeen` — so a tip that appeared while you were reading another panel, alt-tabbed, or simply slower than 20 s never came back in any later colony in that browser, while the simulation ran on underneath (at 2× those 20 s are ~8 in-game days). The timer is gone; a tip is dismissed when you dismiss it, which the card already supported. **A blocked request that said nothing (F13):** `getVillageRequestEligibility` already returned `Need N gold` / `Food storage full`, but the card rendered Accept unconditionally, so clicking an unaffordable offer looked like a dead button and the explanation arrived as a 12-second toast elsewhere. Accept is now disabled and carries the owner's own reason, matching how the raid and diplomacy cards refuse. **A stale pointer (F12):** the campaign's final step sent the player to "focus hints (top-left)", a location that no longer exists — `FocusPanel` lives in the Village tab — so it now names Overview → Village. **Withdrawn (F11):** the audit claimed the "48 real seconds per day" figures were stale and the true rate is 24 s/day. It is the other way round: `gameLoop.BASE_TICKS_PER_SECOND = 1.5` is live code feeding `msPerTick = 1000 / (BASE_TICKS_PER_SECOND * speed)`, `tests/gameLoop.pacingContract.test.ts` asserts `1.5` and `msPerDay(1) === 48_000`, and a reversion to 3 was itself fixed as a defect (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-baseline-pacing-reverted-to-24s-day.md`). "Correcting" those five sources would have written the regression back in. Its one genuine half was fixed: the clock chip's tooltip hardcoded `(work 7–18)` although the default window is 07:00–16:00 and is player-editable — it now derives from `getWorkScheduleLabel(getWorkSchedule(world))`.
- **Fixed: the food panel can now name every way food leaves the colony — 25 spend sites that bypassed the ledger** — the follow-through on the owner's question *"if you spend something it should go down"*, and the real scope of the playability audit's F2. The ledger heard about **three** food sinks (meals, medicine, spoilage) while food was deducted directly at **28 sites**, so the store fell in real time but the dashboard's "Net food flow" and the village panel's food card stayed positive: you could pay tribute, feed a trade route, host a festival, equip a raid or hand out petition aid and the panel would not mention it. `economyLedger.spendFood(state, sink, amount)` is now the single call that **deducts and records** — it floors at zero through `deductResource` and records what was *actually* taken, so it also fixes the sites that could previously deduct more than the store held — and `ECONOMY_SOURCE_LABELS` gained fourteen named sinks (tribute, gifts, treaties, raids, refugees, recruitment, taming, shelter, festivals, petitions, hospitality, weddings, trade routes, disasters). All 25 converted: diplomacy and refugee events, the trade route's export, the Town Hall festival and petition aid, recruitment and taming, the whole raid family (including the loot-taken line, which now uses the helper's return value as the `min(food, target)` it computed by hand), the wedding family, theatre hospitality, the deer parliament's treaty gift, the children's shelter, the daily taming feed, and the plague's 15 % — which is now computed as the amount lost and handed to the owner rather than assigned as a new balance. Every conversion is behaviour-neutral, and the 360-day harness proves it: it reproduces its pre-sweep totals exactly (67 settlers · 25 births · 84 marriages · 68 divorces · 23 pregnancies). `tests/foodSpend.ledger.test.ts` (4) pins the helper's contract and guards all eleven converted files against a raw `resources.food -=` returning — that checklist caught my own premature entry when only one of the wedding file's three sites had been converted.
- **Fixed: three more "restated instead of called the owner" defects, plus a duplicated day index (L2, L7, L16)** — landed as one group, then tested as one group. **Stale barricade numbers in the build catalogue (L2):** the Wall read `+8 … (cap +72)` and the Watchtower `+15`, while the owners are `getWallSegmentCap` — a cap wall plates raise — and the ballista forge bonus that raises the tower's strength, both of which the strength break-down labels already compute dynamically (L47). The static copy was a third definition no upgrade could update; the numbers are gone rather than recomputed, because a catalogue entry has no per-village state to compute them from. **The day index was written out as `year * 360 + dayInYear` (L7):** `getColonyDay` is the owner, and the same expression also seeded `computeDailyTemperatureC`'s daily noise — both now call the owner, so `DAYS_PER_YEAR` has one definition again. The rival-profile field the audit found is written and never read; the write was kept (it is persisted save data, and a correct value costs nothing) rather than deleted as the audit's alternative suggested. **The Moon Howler's cure-window sweep re-implemented "inside a completed building" (L16):** a bare axis-aligned bounds test, while the owner pads the footprint by 10 px as every other consumer does, so a howler inside that margin counted as outdoors and was never forced away. The sweep now calls the owner helper. A fourth, the `NOTIFY_COOLDOWN_DAYS` comment (L15), was corrected to describe the rule that actually runs — same-stage re-notification is unreachable and a worsening always announces, so the cooldown only ever silences a recovery step; changing *that* would have broken a **tested** contract: `tests/low-5-combat-ecology.test.ts` already asserts that a recovery notice inside the cooldown is suppressed, in both the notification list and the event log, so the behaviour is unchanged and that test — not the audit's reading — is the authority. The same check confirmed the owner's recollection that this whole ladder is parked (`ValleyEcology.ENABLED = false`, 2026-09-08): although the park already guaranteed no notification could fire, nothing pinned that silence, so `tests/low-5-combat-ecology.test.ts` gained a case that feeds the same stress fixture which *does* notify when the ladder runs and asserts the shipped off-state emits zero notifications, zero big news, zero log lines and stays stable. `tests/copyAndDayIndex.owners.test.ts` (2) and `tests/moonHowler.indoorsPad.test.ts` (1) fail with the old code restored (`+8` back in the copy, the literal back in `rivalEvents.ts`, and `expected true to be false` for the howler left indoors), and all five restored sources are sha256-identical.
- **Fixed: two more audit defects where a writer restated a rule instead of calling its owner (L1, L5)** — the same shape as M1 and M5: the rule already had one definition, and the defect was a caller that re-implemented it. **The Invention Fair's "dismantle" refund (L1):** the recover-timber branch was a raw `state.resources.wood += 4`, the last write of its kind, so at a full store it pushed wood past `storageMax` (cap 1000 → 1004) and made the cap non-authoritative for the defects that assume stock is inside it — the `Math.min` sites behind M2 and `canStoreImports`'s over-cap refusal. It now goes through `addCappedResource` like the sibling gains. **Reputation could pass 100 (L5):** the owner `addReputation` clamps `0..100` as its documented contract, but three callers restated it with only the floor — `storyHelpers.bumpVillageReputation` (13 call sites across `deerParliament`, `inventionFair`, `rumourLedger`, `travelingTheatre`, `weddingDiplomacy`), `storyEvents`' private `bumpRep`, and the election-promise judgement in `tickElectionPromises`. All three now call the owner: the wide helper keeps its public name but delegates, `bumpRep` was deleted with its ten call sites repointed (it had no callers outside its own file), and the judgement writes through the owner. Reputation now stops at 100 whether a story reward or a promise evaluation moves it (`98 + 6` was 104; 99 + 2 was 101).
- **Fixed: the workshop's gold estimate now matches what the workshop actually produces (L6)** — the building panel prints `→ ~N gold / 2 days`, and that number was assembled differently from the output itself. Production computes `(1 + workers×0.5) × totalMult × goldMult × globalEff` (`dailyBuildingEconomy.ts`), where `totalMult` carries level · terrain · adjacency · festival · skill · **schedule fatigue** · **work-window scale** and `globalEff` carries global efficiency · **Town-Hall governance efficiency**. The estimate omitted the last three entirely and counted a workshop's workers by `occupants.length`, where production keys its worker stats on the settler's live `homeBuildingId` — so it could advertise output for a shop the game treats as unstaffed, and it ignored a tired crew and a short work day. It now uses the same owner helpers production uses for all of them. `tests/workshopEconomy.estimate.test.ts` (2) drives a tired crew (fatigue 100 → the owner's 0.65 floor) and a 5-hour work day against the standard 9, then an occupied-but-unassigned shop (production says "Needs worker", so the estimate must price it at 0 while still previewing with `{ previewUnstaffed: true }`); both failed with the changes reverted (`expected 15 to be less than 15`, `expected 15 to be +0`). The audit's preferred shape — one exported multiplier assembly shared by production and the estimate — was not taken, because `workshopEconomy` is imported by the UI that renders the estimate and the production module is 846 lines; the two assemblies are now identical in content and the code names the authority.
- **Fixed: spoilage now explains itself, and a Moon Howler save reproduces its own scatter (L8, L13)** — two live defects from the same audit, landed and tested as one group. **The food ledger could not account for spoilage (L8):** `applyFoodSpoilage` removed up to 2 %/day (×1.3 summer, ×0.6 winter) and floated `-N food spoiled`, but the ledger's `consumed` side only ever heard about `meals` and `medicine` — so the "why is my food low?" panel could never reconcile produced − consumed against the real delta. It now records the exact loss as a `spoilage` row (`ECONOMY_SOURCE_LABELS` gained `spoilage: 'Spoilage'`, without which the panel would print the raw key), on days that actually lost food. **The Moon Howler drew from the wrong stream (L13):** `forceMoonHowlerOutside` scattered a transforming settler with `getSimRng('moonHowler')()` and ignored the RNG the tick had been handed, as did the four flavour-line picks; both now use the injected stream (production passes exactly that stream, so live play is unchanged — the point is that the load path can be seeded). The half that actually bit was ordering: `saveLoad` ran the load-time form sync *before* `adoptSimSeedFromWorld`/`restoreSimRng`, so a save written at a full-moon `NIGHT_START` with a cursed settler scattered off the pre-load stream (seed 1) and could not reproduce the positions its own save implied. The snapshot restore now happens before anything on the load path draws. `tests/foodLedger.spoilage.test.ts` (1) and `tests/moonHowler.rngThreading.test.ts` (3) fail 4/4 with the three source changes reverted — including a real `buildSaveData` → `loadGameFromParsed` round-trip loaded from two different realms, which must agree.
- **Changed: "Auto-assign all workers" moved out of the building panel into the Village tab** — owner preference: *"it better i think to auto-assign at a building for that building and main button somewhere to auto assign all"*. The village-wide action was rendered inside `SelectedBuildingPanel`, so it was only reachable while a building happened to be selected — and it sat next to that building's own staffing controls, which is where the per-building actions belong. It now lives in **Village → Population**, under the working/idle/jailed/children row, with the per-building actions (`Fill workers`/`Fill builders`, and the `Auto-fill`/`Manual` mode toggle) left exactly where they were. The prop is threaded `App.tsx` → `CitizenOverviewScreen` → `VillageTabPanel`, and `SelectedBuildingPanel` no longer takes it at all, so there is one button rather than two that could drift. Its label carries **no count** — the staffing owner decides who is assignable, and a locally recomputed idle total contradicted it once already (F7, `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-fill-workers-count-disagrees-with-the-owner.md`).
- **Changed: a pregnant settler can be given work (L4)** — owner ruling, *"pregnancy can work"*. The game previously disagreed with itself about this: the automatic pass assigned a pregnant settler and the manual paths refused her, so the same settler was refused by hand on a building ("Auto-staff" on that building) and assigned by "Auto-staff all" in the same breath. The bar lived in **three** places in `buildingStaffingActions.ts` — the idle-pool predicate the roster and preview use, the construction-builder picker, and the assign-button gate for an unfinished site — while all the automatic filters already allowed pregnancy: there is one automatic owner, `workforce.assignMissingWorkers` (12 trigger sites), applying two filters (completed job buildings, and construction crews via `staffConstructionCrews` → `assignBuilderInPlace`). All three manual sites lost the bar. Note that the crew owner `addToConstructionCrew` never checked pregnancy either, so the picker was the only side saying no — which is why the M3 invariant test in `tests/medium-B1-staffing.test.ts` was **updated rather than removed**: it now asserts the picker accepts a pregnant settler *and* that the owner agrees, and re-pins "nobody is announced as a builder when the owner refuses" on a jailed settler. `tests/virtualPlayer.test.ts`'s regression fixture for a non-empty-but-not-empty-enough world used a pregnant settler as its "idle but unassignable" case and now uses a jailed one, preserving the shape. New coverage: `tests/staffing.pregnancy.test.ts` (4) drives every changed site through a public action and failed 4/4 before the change.
- **Fixed: two election-scheduling defects (L3, L11) — a mid-term handover announced as a term election, and a term election that could stall for two years** — both live in `villageLeadership`'s two ceremony starters. **Wrong label (L11):** `tryStartVacancyElectionCeremony` derived the ceremony's reason from the calendar year (`year % ELECTION_INTERVAL_YEARS === 0 ? 'term' : 'succession'`), although it only ever runs because a death or a deposition armed `pendingElectionYear` — so a handover that resolved in Year 2, 4 or 6 was announced and logged as "elected/re-elected village head", and because the reveal stamps `lastElectionYear` only for `'term'`/`'founding'`, it took that year's term slot as well. It now passes `'succession'`. **The stall (L3):** when a *term* election found no eligible candidate, `startElectionCeremony` logged "postponed … no eligible candidates" and returned `false` with nothing scheduled — and its gate only opens on `dayInYear === 0 && year % ELECTION_INTERVAL_YEARS === 0`, so the colony went headless until the next term year, two years later. It now arms the same `VACANCY_ELECTION_DELAY_YEARS` (0.25) schedule the death path uses, so the retry runs a quarter-year on; that is safe because the function has exactly two returns, so `false` can only mean the zero-candidate case. Reachability, stated plainly: L3 needs a term-year day 0 with no eligible leader at all (a colony of children, or every adult jailed), so it is a corner rather than an everyday path; L11 is the reachable one, and it is text plus a consumed term slot. `tests/villageLeadership.electionGaps.test.ts` (3) fails 2/3 with both fixes reverted (`expected 'term' to be 'succession'`, `expected null to be close to 2.25`) while its third case pins that an ordinary term election still reads `'term'` and arms no retry. Source restored byte-identically.
- **Fixed: a settler who no longer held office came back labelled "leader" (M8)** — a leader cursed into Moon Howler form hunts through the night as a werewolf, and `revertToHumanForm` restores the occupation saved at transformation. The office can meanwhile end — a transformed leader is not an eligible candidate (`isEligibleForLeadership` requires a Human-typed entity) while `isActingVillageHead` accepts a cursed werewolf — and the revert then handed the `village_leader` label back to someone with no office, which nothing repaired (`applyLeaderOccupation` only steps down the *current* `villageLeaderId`, and the invariant check covers only the opposite direction). The guard already handled "a different settler holds it now"; it treated `null` — exactly how the office is spelled when **vacant** (`villageLeaderId` is `number | null`, initialised `null`) — the same as an *omitted* option meaning "the caller cannot say". The two are now distinguished, so an explicitly vacant office returns the settler as a settler while an absent option keeps the previous behaviour. The audit flagged this as an owner call because `tests/moonHowler.staleLeaderOccupation.test.ts` documents the opposite; that test pins three cases and none of them is `null` (its "cannot say" case is `undefined`), so the tested contract is untouched and only its prose comment was corrected. A fourth case covers the vacant office and fails 1/4 with the guard reverted while the other three still pass.
- **Fixed: the council report called prisoners homeless and a 3.3-hour gap "yesterday"** — the last two open owner-ruled UI items. **Prisoners:** imprisonment deliberately clears `homeBuildingId` and `residenceBuildingId`, and `deriveConcerns` read that absence as a player-actionable gap, so the dashboard advised building homes or assigning workers for settlers it had itself jailed — while the same screen's settler table and `uiSimSummary` both reported zero. A jailed settler now counts only as imprisoned and is skipped by the homeless/idle tallies. **"vs yesterday":** the Settlers line compared the last two `populationHistory` samples, taken every 10 ticks (~3.3 in-game hours), so the same +1 could appear and vanish several times a day; "Food stored" and "Wildlife" shared that baseline. `deriveCouncil` now picks the most recent sample from an earlier `day` (falling back to `tick <= cur.tick - TICKS_PER_DAY`), so all three lines compare a real day. Presentation only — no simulation, save or cadence change. A third surface needed the same guard: `explainSettler` told a prisoner's detail panel "Build and finish a House so they have somewhere to sleep", so the residence suggestion now carries the guard its workplace suggestion already had. `tests/dashboardData.prisonAndCouncil.test.ts` (4) pins the ruling on all three surfaces and the day-based baseline; all four guards were reverted together to confirm the tests fail without them, and the source was restored byte-identically afterwards. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-prisoners-count-as-idle-and-homeless.md` and `…-vs-yesterday-is-a-ten-tick-sample.md`; both reports move from open to resolved and the register is back to **nothing open**.
- **Changed: a church no longer speeds up courtship; work-shift courting is far slower; a courtship carries a cooldown** — owner rulings. The per-tick advance was `(4 + churchStrength * 2) × … × PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE`, so a full church raised the base 4 → 6 per in-game hour (96 → 144 per day against the 100 needed to marry): a pious village courted *faster*, the opposite sign to every other church effect in the same subsystem (tryst chance 0.07 with vs 0.1 without, floor factor 0.72, caught chance 0.14 vs 0.08). The base is now the flat `COURTSHIP_BASE_RATE_PER_HOUR = 4` via `courtshipRatePerHour(socialTime)`, which takes no church argument. `COURTSHIP_WORK_RATE_FACTOR = 0.15` cuts it to 0.6/hour on shift, and a working settler never leaves the shift to close the distance. `COURTSHIP_COOLDOWN_DAYS = 1` is applied by the courtship system when a courtship dissolves or a marriage ends, counted down daily; `isEligibleToCourt` gained one gate with no tick parameter and no caller changes (a death's `griefUntilTick` is deliberately not reused — it drives the hospital path). New optional field `courtshipCooldownDays`, carried in `simDelta`. Measured on the 360-day gate, seed 12345 (marriages/divorces/caught scandals): cooldown 0 → 65/50/25, 1 day → 69/58/29, 3 days → 54/39/19 — one seed cannot attribute those (behaviour shifts every later draw; the 1-day row out-churning 0 is that drift), so 1 day was chosen **on structure**: the shortest cooldown that forces a clear day between divorce and remarriage, with the least bite out of the dating pool. `tests/courtship.churchRestraint.test.ts` (4); `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-church-accelerates-courtship.md`.
- **Fixed: contextual tutorial tips never appeared — and a load could fire the wrong ones** — reported through the 2026-09-16 UI-logic audit (F1). The hook held the live `WorldState` as "the previous world", but the game loop mutates **one** world in place (`gameTick(this.world, focus)` on the main thread; the worker host hands back its single `worldRef`), which `useVirtualPlayer.ts` already documents as "`world` keeps the same identity across ticks". `detectContextualTutorials(prev, curr)` therefore compared an object with itself and every transition check was false by construction — no "Building finished", "first worker assigned", "visitors arrived", "first winter", "research started/complete", "trade route ready/opened", "Moon Howler curse", "food is low", "ecosystem low", "valley strained" or "first child" tip could ever appear; only the day-one `shelter_night` tip survived, and because a load replaced the world object the inverse also happened (loading a winter save mid-summer produced "first winter"). Detection is now a function of the **current state alone**, reading the same "has this happened" derivation the load path already used (`seedTutorialSeenForExistingState`, which now also covers `valley_strained`) — so one definition serves both live play and save loading, and the ~110 duplicated per-tip transition comparisons are gone. A new world and a new game session seed that derivation too, because a fresh colony already has `mining_1` researched and possibly married founders, so the player is never told about history as if it just happened. `tests/contextualTutorial.detection.test.ts` (3) mutates a world in place — exactly as the loop does — and requires the tip; it also pins that after seeding, live detection returns nothing but the day-one nudge, which fails if a tip is ever added without a matching condition in the shared derivation. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-contextual-tips-never-fire.md`.

- **Fixed: the keyboard ignored open overlays, hijacked typing in the search boxes, and could leave the map drifting** — three related defects from the same audit (F3/F4/F5), all in `useKeyboardControls`. **Overlays did not own the keyboard:** the handler listens on `window` in the **capture** phase, so it ran before `GameMenu`'s document handler and `GameDashboard`'s — one Escape closed the menu *and* fell through to the catch-all that clears the map selection — while `showShortcutsRef` gated only the sidebar-tab block, so zoom, `1`–`9`, `b`, `g`, `r`, `h` and Space still acted on the game behind the shortcuts sheet. Overlays now take a shared claim (`src/game/keyboardOwnership.ts`: `claimKeyboard`/`releaseKeyboard`/`isKeyboardClaimed`), released in effect cleanup, and the game handler returns immediately while any claim is held; Escape and `?` stay above a second gate that stops the gameplay keys while the sheet is open. **Tab hotkeys stole typed characters:** the `v/f/n/p/l/m` block sat *above* the `if (inFormControl) return;` guard and even blurred the field it stole from, so the Guide's documented example `forge` stopped at `f` (Frontier), `village` broke at `v`, `militia` at `m`; the guard now comes first and the unreachable `blur()` is deleted, with Ctrl/Cmd+S left above it so saving works from a field. **A held movement key survived focus loss:** `handleKeyUp` skipped its delete whenever the event target was a text field (`isEditableTarget` falls back to `document.activeElement`), alt-tab delivers no keyup at all, and nothing reset the set — so the momentum loop re-added velocity every frame and the map slid by itself; the same gap left the camera drag refs set after an alt-tab mid-drag, because `GameMapStage` binds its mouse handlers on the canvas only. Keyup now deletes unconditionally (deleting an absent key is free), `window.blur` and `document.visibilitychange` clear the held keys and zero the momentum, and `useCanvasInteractions` runs its own exit reset on blur, `pointercancel` and `visibilitychange`. `tests/keyboardGuards.contract.test.ts` (6) guards the ordering and the claim wiring at the source, because this tier has no DOM to dispatch a key event into — that limitation is stated in the test file. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-keyboard-ignores-open-overlays.md`, `…-sidebar-tab-hotkeys-hijack-typing.md` and `…-held-key-survives-focus-loss.md`.

- **Fixed: five more UI defects the same audit confirmed** — none of them changed the simulation; all five are presentation. **An armed "Confirm demolish" survived a building switch (F8, the only one of the audit's findings that could destroy something):** the panel keeps its arm id in component state and compares it against the *current* building, so the arm was invisible — not cleared — while another building was selected, and `App.tsx` rendered the panel with no `key`, so React reused the instance and returning to the armed building showed the destructive confirm with no intervening click; one click destroyed it. The panel is now keyed per building, which also fixes `CollapsibleSection`'s per-building open state (its `storageKey` is read only at mount, so it had behaved as shared state). **"Fill workers (N)" promised settlers the assignment refuses (F7):** the number came from a local restatement of the eligibility rule, while the assignment uses `buildingStaffingActions.isEligibleIdleWorker` — which also excludes pregnant, imprisoned and construction-crew settlers — and the button renders only in the branch where the owner lists *no* assignable worker, so the count was always a promise the owner contradicted; the count is removed rather than re-derived, taking `idleWorkers`, its `App` memo and an import with it, leaving one definition of who may be assigned. **The minimap's viewport rectangle ignored the canvas (F6):** `world.width / zoom * scaleX * 0.5` collapses to `W / (2 * zoom)`, but the renderer's contract is that the visible span is the canvas divided by the zoom (`worldToScreen` centres on `cw / 2`), so the box was the wrong width and never followed a resize; `MiniMap` now measures the map canvas, which `GameMapStage` already held. **The leadership hint printed a raw fractional year (F11):** it interpolated `getYearsUntilElection`, which is `pendingElectionYear - (year + dayInYear / DAYS_PER_YEAR)` while a vacancy is pending, rendering "merit election in 0.08333333333333333 years"; the owner's `formatElectionDelay` is exported (it already rendered the leadership panel's "3 months") and the hint uses it. **Three strings promised elections every 5 years while the term is 2 (F12):** the Guide entry and the leadership tip, plus a third copy in the in-game roadmap that the audit missed and that contradicted a correct line 74 lines above it in the same panel; all three now interpolate `ELECTION_INTERVAL_YEARS`, so the copy cannot drift again. Two further audit findings were verified but **left open**, because each needs a product ruling rather than a code change: whether a prisoner should still count as homeless in the dashboard council (currently counted as both idle and homeless, since imprisonment clears both residence links) and whether "vs yesterday" should be recomputed to a real day or relabelled (it currently compares `populationHistory` samples 10 ticks — about 3.3 in-game hours — apart). Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-armed-demolish-survives-building-switch.md`, `…-fill-workers-count-disagrees-with-the-owner.md`, `…-minimap-viewport-ignores-canvas.md`, `…-election-countdown-prints-a-fraction.md`, `…-election-cadence-copy-says-five-years.md`, `…-prisoners-count-as-idle-and-homeless.md` and `…-vs-yesterday-is-a-ten-tick-sample.md`.

- **Changed: the Progress → Goals panel is scannable, and "Buildings" has one source again** — owner feedback: *"the ui should be easy to read not long lists scrolling"* and *"there can only be one truth one owner"*. **The scroll:** `Progress → Goals` stacked `ValleyChroniclePanel` + `DynastyPanel` + `GoalsPortraitPanel` (which itself held the portrait, "Your path (live)", Challenges and Statistics) into one five-block scroll behind a **non-default** sub-tab — which is why the "👶 Humans Born" tile had never been seen in play. Each block is now a `CollapsibleSection` — the component `VillageTabPanel` (9 sections) and `SelectedBuildingPanel` (4) already use, so this is reuse, not a new pattern. Chronicle, Dynasties, Your path, Challenges and Valley statistics all open **collapsed** with a one-line `subtitle` that stays visible when closed (the component's own design), and "How history sees you" opens expanded; "Your path" carries the strongest trait as a badge/subtitle (`Strongest: Nature`). Verified visually in the browser tier, not just by compile: the panel now renders five one-line rows. **The duplicate truth:** the UI showed **three** different numbers under the label "🏗️ Buildings" — `StatisticsPanel.tsx:24` used the raw `state.buildings.length` (counting rival settlements and half-built sites), `StatisticsPanel.tsx:45` showed `lifetimeStats.totalBuildings` under "Lifetime Records", and `VillageTabPanel.tsx:241` re-applied a hand-written filter. The owner already existed: `WorldState.totalBuildingsCompleted` is incremented on completion (`dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:180`), decremented on demolition (`buildingMaintenanceActions.ts:215`) and recomputed from `(b) => b.completed && b.faction !== 'rival'` on load (`saveLoad.ts:488-490`) — so all three sites now read that one field. The "Lifetime Records" tile is **removed** rather than relabelled: the field decrements on demolition, so it is a current count and was simply in the wrong section (a truthful lifetime "built all time" figure does not exist and would need its own counter). No new helper, no new constant, no save change. Gates: `test:types` 0 · `lint` 0/0 · `build` ok · `npm test` 167 files / 905 tests · `audit:deps:cycles:strict` ok · `test:browser` verdict pass.

- **Fixed: the entire ground layer was invisible — the map rendered as bare void** — reported from play as "rivers are constantly dry" with "on the minimap it looks great", and carried into the 2026-09-16 handover as a missing-atlas-art problem. That diagnosis was **wrong**: `public/sprites/tileset_grass.png` ships (192×336 RGBA, 210 of 252 tile slots non-empty) and all ten water-family tiles `ATLAS_TILES` uses are blue-dominant (all-water id 61 = `rgb(73,85,126)` against grass id 3 = `rgb(127,158,69)`), while the fill sprites Pixi samples are shipped and blue too (`water_deep_fill.png` = `rgb(63,127,166)`, loaded at runtime as 128×128). Instrumenting the three `drawGround` outcomes and reading back tile-centre pixels settled it: the canvas2D boot frame painted correct terrain (grassland `rgb(125,158,79)`, river `rgb(57,120,164)`, beach `rgb(193,191,147)`) while **every Pixi frame painted nothing at all** — grassland, forest, river, shallow water and beach all read exactly `rgb(22,40,61)`, the `VOID_WATER` surround `renderer.ts` lays down before the ground pass. The cause was one missing scene-graph edge: `createPixiState()` built the terrain container and added all six graphics children to it but never called `app.stage.addChild(root)`, and `Application.render()` renders the stage — so Pixi, the **first-choice** ground renderer, composited a fully transparent canvas every frame and the void fill underneath became the only visible ground. Water simply made the fault most obvious; every terrain type was affected, and the minimap stayed correct because it needs no art. The canvas2D fallback, which the 2026-09-16 `USE_PAINTED_ATLAS = false` change had been tuning, only ever ran for the single boot frame while Pixi was the live renderer — which is why that change altered nothing the player could see at the time. (That is no longer true of the tree: the ground now ships through the canvas2D path, so `USE_PAINTED_ATLAS` is load-bearing again — see the renderer entry below.) The browser tier **passed with this fault present** (the frame was neither blank nor dark enough to trip `imageLooksBlank`), so `scripts/browser-smoke.mjs` now measures the share of the void-surround colour family inside the map viewport and fails above 25 % — matched as a family, not by equality, because the closing screen vignette darkens the surround (`rgb(17,37,60)` dominates a real void frame while exact `#16283d` is 0.01 % of it); the first equality-based attempt silently missed the fault. Fail-before evidence: **60.7 % / verdict `fail` / exit 1** with only the stage attachment removed, against **0.96 % / verdict `pass`** with it restored. The same pass latched Pixi's failed-init path: a rejected `app.init()` previously nulled both `terrainState` and `initPromise`, so the not-ready guard never held and each frame built a fresh `Application` and re-attempted WebGL init forever with a parameterless `catch` logging nothing; a `pixiUnavailable` latch now attempts once per session, reports once via `console.warn`, and is cleared only by an explicit `resetPixiTerrain()`. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-pixi-terrain-container-never-attached-to-the-stage.md` and `…-pixi-webgl-init-retried-every-frame.md`; the superseded 2026-09-16 report is marked as such with its two false claims corrected.
- **Changed: the ground paints through the canvas2D layer, not Pixi/WebGL** — owner decision, after the stage fix above finally made the Pixi ground visible for review. It had **never rendered in a shipped build** (its container was never attached to `app.stage`), so its look had never been checked by anyone; once fixed it drew a bright vector river ribbon (`drawRiver(0x174a6d…0x47b7dc…0xb6eff4…)`) over pale, washed-out tile water with a visible square-tile checkerboard — two inconsistent water renderings on the same river, which the owner marked as wrong. `drawGround` now takes the canvas2D path (`buildTerrainCache` + `drawProceduralGround`, which bakes terrain into per-viewport chunks from the fill sprites) behind a documented `USE_PIXI_GROUND = false` constant, mirroring the existing `USE_PAINTED_ATLAS` switch so the decision is one reversible line with its reason attached; `pixiTerrain.ts` stays referenced through `resetPixiTerrain` and can be re-enabled once its water matches the tile water. Three measured effects: water and shoreline are now consistent with the minimap the owner treats as the source of truth; the 50 %-zoom surround changes colour rather than disappearing — measured `rgb(17,37,60)` (void blue) under Pixi against `rgb(7,16,11)` (near-black green) under canvas2D, so the `valley-far-zoom` void reading fell 0.5918 → 0.0034 because that colour left the guard's family test, not because the band went away; and the `game-render` chunk dropped from **633.99 kB to 272.56 kB** (gzip 188.28 → 83.47 kB) because the dead branch constant-folds away — `wilderfolk-terrain` no longer appears in `dist`. The browser tier's "this browser exposes no WebGL at all" precondition is **kept as a gate**: the canvas2D ground does not need WebGL, but `pixiTerrain.ts` remains in the tree behind a one-constant switch, so a WebGL-less browser is still not a representative environment for that tier. (An intermediate version of this change downgraded it to a recorded-but-ungated field; that weakened a check to suit the change and was reverted.) Measured in the built output: the render chunk fell 633.99 → 272.56 kB (gzip 188.28 → 83.47 kB) and **my** Pixi source was tree-shaken — `backgroundAlpha`, `wilderfolk-terrain`, `PixiError`, `biome-bases` and `soft-biome-transitions` each appear 0 times in `dist`, because eliminating `createPixiState` leaves `terrainState` provably null, so `resetPixiTerrain` folds to a no-op. **Correction:** `pixi.js` is *not* fully eliminated — library code still ships unused in `game-render` (`pixi.js/`, `ObservablePoint`, `Application`, `TextureSource` all match). An earlier version of this entry claimed Pixi was "gone from the bundle entirely"; that was wrong, and came from grepping only for my own source strings. Runtime confirms the path never executes: a temporary probe inside `ensurePixiReady` was never entered across a full harness run. What is genuinely dead is the `pixi.js` declaration in `package.json`; removing `pixiTerrain.ts` and its reset call would clear that, but would also remove the option to switch back.
