- **Fixed: the camera could not be panned to the map's left and right edges** — reported from play as "if it doesnt fit on screen i just should go to left or right". `clampCameraTarget` derives the allowed camera range from a viewport in pixels and falls back to `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight`, but the map viewport is the **canvas**, not the window: measured live as **1304×813** inside a **1600×900** window, the difference being the left rail and the right inspector (`w-[18.5rem]` = 296 px, fixed). The keyboard/WASD/arrow and momentum pan loop and the click-to-focus nudge both passed **no** viewport, so on the default Medium map (1200×900 world px) at the default 1.45× zoom the allowed camera x range was `[551.7, 648.3]` — 96.6 world px — instead of `[449.7, 750.3]`, 300.6 px: about a third of the map's width, short by exactly the panel chrome. Drag-to-pan already passed the canvas rect, which is why dragging worked while the keyboard did not. Both paths now pass the canvas rect, and `useKeyboardControls` requires a `canvasRef` in its options interface so the omission cannot recur silently. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-camera-clamp-uses-window-instead-of-canvas.md`.
- **Changed: day/night visual differences are gone** — requested by the owner ("i want day/night visuals gone they are really distracting each 48 second a night is annoying"). The atmosphere overlays were already no-ops (`drawNightAtmosphere`, `drawDayAtmosphere` and `drawNightBuildingGlow`), but two real differences remained and both are removed: the placement-grid major line switched from a dark green to a light slate colour at night (`grid.ts`), and lamps lit up after dark with a warm radial glow and amber head (`decorRender.ts`, reached through `buildings.ts`). No night-driven rendering remains wired — `getNightGlowIntensity` has no callers and `drawScreenVignette` is only ever called with `night = false`. The lamp now draws one constant appearance and its building description no longer promises a night glow. Settlers still go indoors and asleep at night: that is simulation behaviour, not a screen tint. Audio's night mode (`setNightMode`) is deliberately untouched, and is the remaining day/night difference if the owner wants that flattened too.

- **New: families and young settlers can now arrive** — daily immigration used to be adults only (18–55 singles, or a couple). An arriving party is now one of three shapes, rolled in this order: a **lone youth** (10 %, aged 12–17 — works, can be courted, cannot marry), a **married couple** (12 %, with a 40 % chance the wife is already expecting), or a **single adult**; a couple may bring **1–2 children** (60 % chance, each child needing a free slot), who take the family surname, are registered with both parents (`fatherId` / `motherId` / `childrenIds`) and enrol at a staffed school like any native child. A child never arrives alone — the simulation's own orphan path adopts or houses a minor without parents. All tuning lives in one new `Immigration` group in `gameConstants.ts` (adult / youth / child age bands, lone-youth chance, couple chance, pregnancy chance, family-child chance, max children) with the age ladder spelled out beside it, and the arrival notice now reads “A young settler arrived” / “A settler family arrived (N)” instead of always “+N Settlers arrived”. `tests/immigration.composition.test.ts` (7) sweeps 150 seeds per slot budget: all three shapes occur, a child always arrives with both parents in the same party, children share the family surname and are listed in both parents' `childrenIds`, party size never exceeds the free-slot budget, no children arrive when only two slots exist, and every lone youth is 12–17, single and below the adult floor.

- **Fixed: the adult floor was 16 while the documented age ladder says 18** — `HUMAN_ADULT_MIN_AGE` is the shared floor for courtship, leadership, adoption guardianship, recruit/immigrant age rolls and the legacy age clamp, and it read 16 while `docs/archive/FERTILITY_AGE14_F1.md` (“The adult marriage threshold remains **18**”), `YOUTH_LOVE_FEATURE.md` (“**18** = Adult transition”), this changelog (“affairs 18+”) and `HUMAN_MOVE_OUT_MIN_AGE` (18) all say 18. A 16–17 year old could therefore enter adult courtship (two years before marriage was possible), be elected village head, count as a “single adult” for solo housing, act as an **adoptive guardian** — the 2026-09-13 EK-E3 fix moved that gate onto this constant, so it stayed incomplete — and be cursed or illness-rolled as an adult. The constant is 18, every consumer now agrees with the documents, and no consumer code changed. `tests/adultFloor.age18.test.ts` (5) pins it against `HUMAN_MOVE_OUT_MIN_AGE` / `Relationship.AFFAIR_MIN_AGE`, the courtship and leadership gates (17 refused, 18 accepted), the affair-partner floor, and the adoption rule (an orphan is still housed when no adult is available, just never recorded as a minor's parent). Same-seed 360-day totals moved as expected for a deliberate gate change — settlers 77 → 69, marriages 53 → 48, births 35 → 27 — alongside the immigration composition change, which shares that RNG stream. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-adult-floor-was-16-against-the-documented-18.md`.

- **Developer: the runtime import graph is acyclic — the 9-module cycle is broken, not suppressed** — the repaired gate's first real cruise found a nine-module runtime component (`dayCycle → defenseStructures → forge → householdComposition → humanLifecycleCleanup → moonHowler → residencyReconciliation → residencySelection → workforce → dayCycle`), and it came from two real back-edges into the `dayCycle` facade rather than from the facade itself. **One:** `householdComposition.ts` took `HUMAN_MOVE_OUT_MIN_AGE` from `dayCycle`, which only re-exports it from `residencyOccupancy.ts` — the hub hop added `householdComposition → dayCycle` next to the existing `dayCycle → householdComposition`, closing every cycle through the residency branch (`residencySelection`, `residencyReconciliation`, `workforce`). It now imports the constant from the module that defines it, so that edge is one-way and four modules leave the component; no caller outside the cycle changed. **Two:** `humanLifecycleCleanup.ts` (the death primitive used by every death path) needed `finalizeMoonHowlerDeath` and `isSettlerRelationshipEntity` from `moonHowler.ts`, while `moonHowler.ts` kills the exorcising priest through `killHuman` — reached through the `dayCycle` facade — so policy and primitive genuinely imported each other. The Moon Howler **form** rules now live in a new leaf-side module, `src/game/moonHowlerForm.ts` (`HUMAN_FORM`, `RevertToHumanFormOptions`, `revertToHumanForm`, `finalizeMoonHowlerDeath`, `isSettlerRelationshipEntity`, and the two private prison helpers only the revert uses); `moonHowler.ts` keeps the policy and imports them, and `humanLifecycleCleanup.ts` / `humanTick.ts` / `simulationInvariants.ts` import the predicates from the form module instead of from the policy, so the dependency points one way. It is a pure relocation — the 360-day seed-12345 run ends with identical totals (settlers 69, births 27, marriages 48, divorces 33, scandal events 183) and the six Moon Howler test files pass with only the `revertToHumanForm` import path changed — and `npm run audit:deps:cycles:strict` now exits 0 on **322 modules / 1 470 runtime edges** with **no runtime cycle**. The 7-module **type-only** component is deliberately still reported and now never fatal: `WorldState` holds class-typed grid fields (`EntitySpatialGrid`, `RoadAvoidanceIndex`) while those classes take `WorldState` back, so cutting the spoke would mean duplicating each grid's public API into a leaf interface or widening the fields to `unknown` — both worse than a mutual type reference TypeScript erases. `--strict` therefore means exactly one thing: the runtime graph is acyclic. The Moon Howler registry row names `moonHowlerForm.ts`, so all 2 428 functions stay matched to a recorded owner. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-runtime-import-cycles-in-the-game-module-graph.md`.

- **Developer: a call-graph & ownership explorer — and the 72 ownership rows the code was missing** — `npm run graph:calls` writes `docs/tools/call-graph.html`, a self-contained interactive explorer (no server, no CDN) with three views: **Flow / decisions** (every ownership row with its true owner, cadence, `scheduledFrom`, permitted writes, tests and clickable entry functions), **Functions** (search any of the 2 470 functions; callers on the left, callees on the right, re-centre on click, owner shown with how it was matched) and **Modules** (function counts, owner keys, inbound/outbound call edges). It is generated from `src/**` by a dependency-free lexer that blanks comments/literals before parsing, understands functions, arrow/const definitions, class and object methods and assigned handlers, and resolves every call site through an explicit ladder: `this.m()` to the enclosing class, `X.m()` to an imported namespace or a known instance's class, parameter calls as injected callbacks, imports through re-export barrels, then same-file, then a unique name — with anything else (prototype calls like `array.push`, `Set.add`, `callbacks.logEvent`, globals) classified as **external** rather than guessed. Ambiguity is now reserved for a same-name free function with no import and no receiver, and that count is **0** across 11 069 call sites (585 before the ladder existed, when method calls and function-typed parameters were mistaken for internal functions). Running it showed that only **957 of 2 470 functions had a recorded owner**, so `OWNERSHIP_OVERVIEW.md` gained **112 rows** across two 2026-09-16 passes — first the simulation decisions (friendship/feud webs, school progression, disasters and weather, rival profiles, defense/militia math, apprenticeships, tamed-animal care, the worker transport, spawn/despawn bookkeeping, audio/render owners), then the UI, presentation and data modules, which are owners in their own right (a panel or renderer owns its drawing and may read simulation state but must not write it). Coverage is now **2 470 / 2 470 (100 %)** over 183 ownership rows; the pinned `decisionRegistry` key set was deliberately left untouched (its test asserts the exact key list), following the 2026-09-13 precedent of recording newly-mapped owners in the overview. Recorded in `docs/tools/README.md`.

- **Docs corrected — the youth-love window is 12–17 with a 4-year age gap, as the code already did** — two youth-love bounds had drifted from the implementation in opposite directions, and both were settled the same way: the constant is intended, the document was the artifact. `YOUTH_LOVE_MIN_AGE` had already been ruled **12** (not the documented 14) on 2026-09-13 because the 14–17 band is too narrow for the game's pace — a nice feature that never happened in play — and `docs/archive/YOUTH_LOVE_FEATURE.md` §3's age-gap row and §8's clear row still said the pair "may differ by no more than 2 years" while `YOUTH_LOVE_MAX_AGE_GAP` is **4**; the owner re-confirmed both values (2026-09-16) with the same reason, so the two document rows now say 4 and cite the ruling, and both constants carry it in a comment so a later reader — or audit — does not file them as defects (a 2026-09-13 audit did exactly that for the age floor). No code, save or migration change: the constants were already these values. The 4-year gap is also what bounds the youth-love→courtship wait fixed in the same pass, since the younger partner is then at most that many years from the adult floor. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-youth-love-handoff-unreachable-and-courtship-not-exclusive.md`.

- **Fixed: youth sweethearts now really do grow up together, and a courtship is one pair** — the last two lifecycle/social audit findings (F3/F5), both in the relationship paths. **F3:** ages advance in whole in-game years, so a pair one year apart reaches 19/18 on the day the younger partner turns 18 — and `reconcileYouthLove` cleared the link the moment either partner was past 18, which `advanceYouthLove` runs *before* its promotion loop. The documented handoff ("At age 18 for both settlers, the pair enter the existing adult courtship path with carried progress", `docs/archive/YOUTH_LOVE_FEATURE.md`) therefore fired only for pairs whose 18th birthdays fall in the same year; everyone else was dropped silently, with no "grew apart" notice and no carried progress — for a one-year gap, on the very day the pair came of age. The cap is gone: an existing link survives the older partner's 18th birthday while the younger is still 12–17 (the document's "one is 18 and one is 17 → keep the link temporarily") and the pair hands off on the first daily pass where both are adults, carrying the documented 25–70 progress into mutual courtship. The wait is bounded by the same age gap that allowed the pair to form, and a link that cannot promote — death, one-sided, prison, an adult partner, a gap over the limit — is still cleared. **F5:** `isCourtshipCandidate` asked whether a settler *may* court, never whether they were *free*, so a third settler could be offered someone already in a mutual courtship and `humanTick` then overwrote that partner's link — orphaning the previous settler's half, which no code path ever repaired. `courtshipProgress` is per-settler, so the orphan kept its progress and the 💕 badge, and the next pair inherited the abandoned pair's progress; death cleanup, divorce and the removal sweep all cleared `partnerId` / `affairPartnerId` / `youthLove*` but never the courtship fields. A courtship is now exclusive — the same one-route-at-a-time rule youth love, affairs and marriage already follow (`isEligibleToCourt` refuses a youth link, an affair is a single partner) — with the caller's own mutual partner still re-selectable; a new pair starts at zero; a new daily `reconcileCourtships` dissolves a pair that can no longer complete so exclusivity can never lock anyone out; death, divorce and removal clear both halves; and the nightly `activeCourtships` diagnostic counts only pairs whose two sides name each other. Same-seed 360-day totals moved as expected for a deliberate relationship-path change (marriages 48 → 54, divorces 33 → 40, births 27 → 24, settlers 69 → 67); the two fixes share the `humanRelationships` stream, so one seed cannot attribute the movement between them. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-youth-love-handoff-unreachable-and-courtship-not-exclusive.md`.

- **Fixed: the tick delta rewound presentation randomness and shipped two dead spawn/death lists** — the last two worker-boundary audit findings (F4/F6), both in what the tick payload carries. **F4:** applying a delta calls `restoreSimRng(delta.simRng)` on the main thread, and that snapshot is the **worker's** — which never draws the presentation streams. `restoreSimRng` treated its registry as a closed world (delete anything the snapshot omits, rewind the rest), so `rendererShake`, `weatherFx`, `sfx`, `ambientAudio` and `introScreen` sat frozen at the last world upload: weather-particle respawn positions, screen shake and sfx variation replayed the same values on every applied tick (~3×/s), and a stream created after the upload was deleted so the seed's first draw returned. Presentation owners now live in their own registry behind a new `getPresentationRng(owner)`, which `snapshotSimRng`/`restoreSimRng` never read or write; the five consumers were repointed, `setSimSeed`/`resetSimRng` clear both registries, and `tests/simRng.presentationStreams.test.ts` (3) pins the boundary — including a lockstep check that a restore leaves a live presentation stream running while a simulation stream in the same snapshot is still rewound. **F6:** `SimTickDelta` shipped `diedIds` and `newEntities`, computed from a pre-tick alive-id set, cloned and transferred every tick, and read by nobody — their only consumer, `EntityCatalog.applyTickDelta`, had **zero call sites** because the catalog is rebuilt from the authoritative world. Both fields are gone, along with the `aliveBefore` parameter that existed to compute them, the now-unused `aliveIdSet` helper, the worker's per-tick/per-command `before` bookkeeping, and the dead consumer itself; `aliveEntities` remains the complete spawn/death truth. Neither change touches a simulation roll: the 360-day seed-12345 run ends with identical totals (settlers 69, births 27, marriages 48, divorces 33, scandal events 183). Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-tick-delta-restores-presentation-rng-and-ships-dead-spawn-lists.md`.

- **Cleanup batch — dead command surface, a loop-lifecycle wipe, a lying test header, an unguarded wire-code map, and the artifact gate the hygiene reports asked for** — from the worker-boundary audit's cheap findings. **F7:** `assignResident` and `recordGuidedCampaignChoice` were validated and dispatched by the worker but sent by nobody (`assignWorker` already routes residences, and the guided-campaign panel only exposes `onStart`), so both variants are gone from the command union, the OPS set, the validator and the dispatch — unreachable protocol that looked supported. **F8.1:** `GameLoop.stop()` cleared its listener set while `BUG_TRACKER.md` #9 records the opposite, so a stopped-then-restarted loop would have run with the UI unsubscribed; the set now belongs to the instance. **F8.2:** the header of `tests/workerBoundary.closure.test.ts` listed `activeEvent`, `bigNews`, `floatingTexts` and `nextFloatingTextId` as "deliberately NOT compared" while its key array does compare them — the comment now matches the array and records why `activeEvent` is vacuous in a 240-tick fixture. **F8.3:** `ENTITY_CODE_TO_TYPE` is derived from `ENTITY_TYPE_CODE`, so a new `EntityType` can no longer compile without a wire code (the old hand-written reverse map would silently render it as code 255). Alongside them, `npm run check:source` now also fails on ZIP/gzip content and on zero-byte files under `src/`, `tests/`, `scripts/` or `config/` — the class the stale-archive and stray-artifact reports could not be guarded against before — and `gameTypes.ts`'s inline `import('./beautyGrid')` type became a top-level `import type`, which removes the **2-module runtime import cycle** the repaired gate had just surfaced (the 9-module component was fixed the same day — see the entry above). Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-worker-protocol-and-guard-drift.md`, `…-runtime-import-cycles-in-the-game-module-graph.md`, `…-stale-source-archives-hidden-in-src.md` and `…-stray-duplicate-artifacts.md`.

- **Developer: the import-graph gate checked nothing — it does now** — `npm run audit:deps*` printed `✔ no dependency violations found (0 modules, 0 dependencies cruised)` and exited 0 on every run, because `dependency-cruiser@18.3.1` refuses to attach its TypeScript transpiler for the repository's `typescript@7.0.2` (it supports `<7.0.0`); the dependency half of `npm run audit` therefore verified nothing. All four commands now run `scripts/check-import-cycles.mjs`, which parses the sources itself (static and dynamic imports, `export … from`, type-only statements kept separate), resolves relative and `@/` specifiers (including Vite `?raw` assets and the Node worker's emitted-JS path), runs Tarjan's SCC algorithm and **asserts its own coverage**: a cruise under 50 modules or with no resolved dependency fails loudly instead of printing a tick. It reports **321 modules / 1 463 runtime dependencies** (was 0/0), reports type-only cycles as warnings, checks the not-to-test layering rule, and `--strict` (wired as `audit:deps:cycles:strict`) makes a runtime cycle fatal while type-only cycles stay reported — the type graph around `WorldState` is mutual by construction and cannot be cut without weakening types. `dependency-cruiser` and `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` are deliberately kept for the day it supports TypeScript 7; the empty `dependency-graph.dot` output is gone, and the repaired gate's first real cruise reports **two runtime import cycles** (a 2-module `beautyGrid ↔ gameTypes` pair and a 9-module component through `dayCycle`/`workforce`) plus one type-only cycle — filed as `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-runtime-import-cycles-in-the-game-module-graph.md`. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-dependency-cruiser-gate-cruises-zero-modules.md`.

- **Fixed: the trade panel disabled the coin→materials rescue routes and told the player "Need Market"** — `ProgressTabPanel` gated "Establish Route" on its own `marketOk && repOk` copy and never asked the trade owner, whose `isMaterialPurchaseRoute` exempts gold → wood/stone/food from the Market requirement precisely because a Market costs the wood and stone the colony is trying to obtain; `App.tsx` repeated the same copy for `tradeReadyCount`, so the HUD agreed with the wrong rule and a colony with gold and no timber had no visible way out. Both call sites now use `canEstablishTradeRoute(state, route.id)` and the disabled route shows the owner's own `blockReason`, so the three rescue routes are offered again while the reputation requirement and the already-established rule still bind. `tests/tradeRoutes.eligibility.test.ts` (6) pins the owner rule — which had no test at all — and guards both call sites against restating it. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-material-purchase-trade-routes-unreachable.md`.

- **Repository hygiene — the dead weight the "double code" audit found is gone** — `src/components/tabPanels.zip`, `src/components/components.txt` and `src/hooks/hooks.txt` were **ZIP archives of live sources** masquerading as notes (seven members had already drifted from the compiled files, and `npm run check:source` cannot see that class); `src/image.png` and `tests/log` were byte-identical 1 MB copies of one unreferenced PNG (`sha256 c62058aa…10d613`); `scripts/run-full-year.mjs`, `scripts/repro-worker-stall.mjs` and `scripts/smoke-build.mjs` were superseded `.mjs` twins of the `.mts` scripts `package.json` actually runs; `scripts/tsx perf-all.ts` was a 0-byte file created by a mistyped command; and `scripts/graph.mjs` + its `npm run graph` entry could never run because `import madge` was never declared (dependency-cruiser via `audit:deps*` and the cycle scripts are the real import-graph surface). All ten files are deleted, no file in the tree referenced any of them, `src/components/` and `src/hooks/` now contain only `.ts`/`.tsx`, and knip's unlisted-dependency list is empty. The remaining gate gap is recorded as a follow-up in the reports: `scripts/check-source-shadow-files.mjs` still cannot see archive/snapshot duplicates inside `src/` or zero-byte files under `src/`/`scripts/`. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-stale-source-archives-hidden-in-src.md`, `…-stray-duplicate-artifacts.md` and `…-graph-script-imports-unlisted-madge.md`.

- **Worker-boundary audit batch — loading a save could freeze the whole simulation, plus five more boundary defects** — from the 2026-09-16 worker-boundary audit (`docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/game-worker.md`), each with a regression test or a stated evidence trail. **F1 (critical, reported from play as "the savegame failed loading"):** `adoptWorldSession` raised `workerBooting` and then queued the import chain, whose first statement polled that same flag while being the only code that clears it — so an in-game **Load game** never posted `importSave`, neither the worker nor the main-thread fallback ticked, and every player command accumulated in `deferredWorkerCommands` forever, with the worker still simulating the old world. The chain now waits on the host handshake (`GameWorkerHost.whenReady()`, new) and lowers the flag for the session that raised it in a `finally`, so a stop mid-import no longer leaves it raised either. **F2 (high):** `patchUi` assigned `bigNews`, `floatingTexts`, `activeEvent` and `nextFloatingTextId` from the host's snapshot — up to four pipelined ticks behind — so a Big News banner could flash for one tick and vanish and an event card could be replaced by `null` before the player answered it; the worker now adopts only the player-authored fields through a new pure `applyWorkerUiPatch` and floors the floating-text allocator at its own value. **F3:** `activeEvent` was the one `WorldState` field the tick writes, the delta shipped and the prep rollback payload omitted — a failed tick could leave an event latched; it is now in `SimPrepKeys`/extract/apply. **F5:** `applySimPrep` ended with `invalidateWorldRuntimeCaches`, which drops `scentGrid` — accumulated wolf scent, not a derived index — so every rollback zeroed the trail; the live grid is carried across the rebuild. **F9:** `setPaused`/`setSpeed`/`patchUiState` posted without the `try/catch` their four sibling messages have, so a `DataCloneError` escaped into a React handler; one `postControl` helper now raises a worker fault and clears the last-sent cache so the value is retried. **F10:** a session swap during worker boot disposed the worker without scheduling recovery, leaving that session on the main thread forever; it now schedules the same recovery every other failure path uses. `tests/gameLoop.sessionSwapWorker.test.ts` (3), `tests/workerUiPatch.merge.test.ts` (4) and `tests/simPrep.rollbackFields.test.ts` (3) pin the new behaviour, and the 360-day gate is unchanged (settlers 77, births 35, marriages 53, divorces 36). The audit's four remaining findings are recorded below rather than fixed: presentation RNG streams swept into the sim snapshot (F4), `diedIds`/`newEntities` shipped to nobody (F6), two command ops with no sender (F7) and three guard/doc drifts (F8). Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-loading-a-save-freezes-the-sim-worker.md`, `…-ui-patch-rewinds-worker-authored-big-news.md`, `…-prep-rollback-misses-the-active-event-and-the-scent-field.md` and `…-worker-control-posts-throw-and-boot-swap-drops-the-worker.md`.

- **Simulation audit batch — four confirmed defects in lifecycle, relationships and the daily layer** — from the 2026-09-16 lifecycle/social audit (`docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/sim-lifecycle-social.md`), each with a regression test that fails against the old code. **F1 (school day ~3.3× too cheap):** `creditChildSchoolDay` compared the `schoolTicksToday` **tick** counter against `WORK_HOURS_PER_DAY * 0.5` **hours**, so ~1.7 in-game hours of attendance credited a whole school day and `schoolDays` reached its maturation boost, graduation days and schoolyard-bond cadence far too early; the threshold is now `SCHOOL_DAY_MIN_TICKS` (16 ticks at 3 ticks/hour), converted once from the named hour value. **F2 (the "shared home" friendship had no implementation):** the daily friendship pass grouped its home bump by `homeBuildingId`, which is the **workplace** in this codebase, so settlers sharing a House — spouses included — gained nothing while coworkers were counted through two paths; the pass now builds three groups from the three fields that mean what the documented rule says (residence, workplace building, job type), additively, so no existing friendship growth was removed. **F4 (a feud survived its counterpart's death):** the missing-counterpart deletion sat below the "lower id takes responsibility" guard, so a higher-id survivor kept `feud_<deadId>` forever — never decayed, never deleted, never logging "settled their feud"; the cleanup now runs before the guard, exactly as the neighbouring friendship pass already did. **F6 (daily immigrants bypassed the spatial grid):** the immigration spawn pushed the newcomer into `state.entities`/`allAlive` and only updated the id maps, and because the daily layer runs after the tick's `assertSpatialGridInvariants`, the newcomer stayed invisible to social/hunt grid queries for the rest of the tick; it now goes through the canonical `pushNewEntity` path that births already use. `tests/education.schoolDayTicks.test.ts` (4), `tests/relationships.sharedHomeFriendship.test.ts` (3), `tests/relationships.feudCleanup.test.ts` (3) and `tests/dailyImmigration.gridSync.test.ts` (2) pin all four; the 360-day run on seed 12345 finishes with totals identical to the pre-fix run, so none of the four is a balance change. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-school-day-credited-from-a-tick-counter.md`, `…-shared-home-friendship-keyed-on-the-workplace.md`, `…-feud-against-a-removed-settler-never-pruned.md` and `…-daily-immigrant-bypasses-the-spatial-grid.md`.

- **Balance — an in-game day is 48 real seconds at 1× again** — `BASE_TICKS_PER_SECOND` had been reverted to `3` by the damaged-revision event, so the shipped "slower baseline pacing" was silently absent: at 1× a day ran in 24 real seconds, and every rationale written against 48 s — the two-year election-term decision, the building-progress ramp, the build-bar comment, `villageLeadership.ts`, and `scripts/probe-day-budget.mts`'s printed real-time table — was off by a factor of two. It is back to `1.5` (0.5× ≈ 96 s, 2× ≈ 24 s, 10× ≈ 4.8 s), the constant is exported, and the missing guard is now `tests/gameLoop.pacingContract.test.ts` (3): a silent revert fails a test instead of passing a fully green gate, which is exactly how this regression survived. Scheduler-only — 72 ticks/day, saved `tick` values, the worker protocol and every simulation cadence are unchanged, and no save migrates. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-baseline-pacing-reverted-to-24s-day.md`.

- **Simulation rolls stopped falling back to raw `Math.random`** — ten default parameters (`rng: () => number = Math.random`) still let a rule run on the host's global RNG: four in `simulation/humanRelationships.ts` (schoolyard gossip, schoolyard bonds, youth love, and the **amicable divorce**), four in `moonHowler.ts` (rite outcome, new-moon curse, church cures, the cycle), `rivalProfiles.selectRivalDailyAction`, and `frontierCombat.rollRivalOutgoingRaidResponse`. Every production caller omitted the argument, so a rival's daily decision, a rival's answer to a raid, and a settler's no-scandal divorce drew from the **global** `Math.random` instead of an owner stream — `worldGen` does install a seeded `__global__` stream for a new world, but that override deliberately backs off when something already replaced `Math.random` (a test spy, a host patch), and either way the draws stayed outside the per-domain streams: not owner-scoped, not restored per domain, and native and irreproducible whenever the override was missing. Each default is now its owner stream (`getSimRng('humanRelationships' | 'moonHowler' | 'rivalProfiles' | 'frontierCombat')`, the pattern the Moon Howler production path and `nameLoader` already used), and the injectable parameter is unchanged, so tests still force an outcome by passing `() => 0`. `tests/simRng.seededDefaults.test.ts` (5) pins two of the defaults to their owner stream, shows a replaced global `Math.random` cannot steer them, and keeps `Math.random(` out of every file under `src/` except `simRng.ts`, which owns the seeded global override — `worldGen`'s `nativeRandom()` map-seed draw stays deliberately native. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-simulation-rolls-fall-back-to-raw-math-random.md`; roadmap **T3** no longer has a raw fallback to migrate.

- **Fixed: the caught-in-the-act affair path was unreachable, so no scandal could become a feud** — the only certain exposure route (a spouse walking in on the pair) can only happen at the cheater's own home, and both `isValidAffairTrystSite` and the two `humanTick` encounter guards treated that home as off limits, so the tryst site and the walk-in site were the same place and each rule excluded it. The same predicate counted a spouse who merely *should* be home as a walk-in, while the exposure roll sets `chance = 1` for that flag — so had the site ever been selectable, every home tryst would have been an automatic divorce rather than a witnessed catch. An **empty** marital home is now a valid tryst site (refused only when the spouse is within the same 22 px `AFFAIR_SPOUSE_BLOCK_RADIUS` the other affair gates use), the walk-in requires the spouse to be physically there inside the 55 px arrival window, `isMaritalHomeOccupiedBySpouse` replaces `isAtMaritalHome` in both guards, and `wouldWalkInOnMaritalAffair` drops the "due home" test along with the `hourOfDay` argument of `tryExposeCaughtAffair` / `tryExposeCaughtAffairForPair`. A catch is a catch again: scandal, divorce, and — through `startFeud`, whose only production caller is the caught-cheater divorce — a feud on both sides. `tests/affairCaught.reachability.test.ts` (8) drives the real rule and the real tick, including the control that nothing is caught when the spouse is nowhere near. Same-seed A/B (seed 12345, fix off → on): caught scandals 21 → 21, divorces 38 → 39, affairs 54 → 39, exposures 89 → 91, settlers 73 → 74 — the rule is coherent but the drama is unchanged, because the rendezvous still sends a pair to the **paramour's** residence and so rarely visits the cheater's home; that gap is recorded as an open roadmap item. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-caught-in-the-act-affair-route-unreachable.md`.

- **Fixed: loading a save rebuilt the valley at 1/100 scale, so the colony looked lost** — reported from play as “the savegame failed loading”, with the save attached. The world data was intact (`_version 0.6.4`, 1 202 entities, 290 humans, 102 buildings, tick 13 542); the bug was the map. A save stores the valley compactly and regenerates the terrain on load, and `WorldMap.width/height` are the **tile** grid while `generateWorldMap(width, height, …)` takes **pixels** — the tile counts were passed straight through, so a 1600×1200 px colony came back as a 160×120 px strip (16×12 tiles, `tileSize` reading 100 px instead of 10) with **1197 of its 1202 entities outside the map**. Nothing threw, which is why it presented as a failed load. `restoreWorldMapFromSave` now converts tiles → pixels (`× TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE`), and the attached Large-map save restores at full size with 0 entities off-map and the terrain identical tile for tile. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-compact-save-restores-valley-at-wrong-scale.md`; `tests/saveWorldMap.compact.test.ts` (3) pins preset, default and custom-sized maps, and `tests/workerBoundary.closure.test.ts` now asserts `worldMap` explicitly — the key loop could not see it, because a compact map is not one of `WORLD_STATE_SAVE_KEYS`.
- **Fixed: a refused save now says why** — every load failure used to collapse into a guessed message (“file may be corrupted” on one path, “Save from a different build” on the other) with the real cause only in the console, which is exactly why the report above had to be diagnosed outside the browser. `parseSaveJson` now returns a `reason` (`empty` / `unreadable` / `malformed` / `version-mismatch`) with a `detail` carrying both versions, `describeSaveReadFailure` turns it into one precise sentence, and both Load paths (browser slot and file) show that instead of a guess. `tests/saveRead.failureReason.test.ts` (4) covers the four causes. `scripts/inspect-save.mts` was added alongside it to run a sent-in save through the real load path and report why it fails.

- **New: a school shows which children are in it** — the player could staff a School (capacity 10) but never see who was enrolled, because attendance is deliberately not stored: every tick each player child walks to the nearest **staffed** school with a free seat and seats are handed out in world order through a per-tick reservation map (`humanTick`’s `schoolReserved`). The building inspector now shows **Pupils n/10** with each child’s name, whether they are **in class** right now (physically inside during class hours) or merely enrolled, their education tier, a ⚠️ when the classroom is full, and a specific empty hint that distinguishes “no teacher assigned” from “no pupils yet”. The list comes from `buildSchoolRosters` / `getSchoolRoster` in `education.ts`, which replays the tick’s own rule through the same `findNearestStaffedSchool` + `SCHOOL_MAX_CHILDREN` accounting — so the panel cannot disagree with where the children actually walk — and `describeSchoolRoster` owns the player-facing strings, following the `describeHotelStatus` / `describeTownHallPerks` convention. `tests/school.roster.test.ts` (6) pins capacity and overflow to a second school, the unstaffed case, that only living player children enrol, the “in class” window, and the exact inspector text. No save field, schema or simulation change: attendance stays derived.

- **Settlers become markers when you zoom out — the village-wide view stops being a crowd** — above `HUMAN_MIN_SCREEN_PX` (30 px) the sprite path stopped shrinking, so at village zoom every settler claimed 30 px of screen plus a contact shadow, a walk frame, a status badge, a speech bubble and often a name plate; at 200+ citizens that is the “crazy mess”. Below the new `HUMAN_SPRITE_MIN_ZOOM` (1.15) each settler is now one small dot drawn in their own class colour — visitors cyan, rivals orange, the leader gold, larger and crowned — and the dot path skips the sprite lookup, walk frame, shadow, bob, badge, bubble and label, so the frame cost falls with the noise. The threshold sits between the zoom presets on purpose: 0.5 / 0.75 / 1.0 show dots, 1.25× and closer show people. A **selected** settler always keeps the full sprite (and their ring), so the inspector keeps pointing at somebody, and the entity-layer cache already invalidates on any zoom change, so crossing the threshold rebuilds the layer. Awake night-goers are unaffected, and this composes with the sleep rule above: after dark the map holds only the people who are actually out. `tests/humanSprites.zoomMarker.test.ts` (4) pins the switch at every preset, the selection exemption, the marker size bounds and the per-class colours, and the browser acceptance harness gained a **far-zoom pass** (`scripts/browser-smoke.mjs` sets the 50% preset, captures `valley-far-zoom.png`, then restores `CAMERA_ZOOM_DEFAULT`) so the marker path is exercised in the real bundle — it reported the frame painted (3 960 distinct colours, not blank) with 0 console errors. Frame-rate gain is structural (fewer canvas operations per citizen), not yet measured on a 200-citizen colony.

- **Fixed: settlers talked — and floated a speech bubble — while asleep at home** — there is no `sleeping` flag in the simulation; “asleep” is just the night routine leaving a settler near their house, and the ambient-chatter roll in `humanTick` never checked it while the dialogue picker actively pushed `'home'`/`'sleep'` lines at night (`humanChat.ts`), so a sleeper kept a bubble for the whole 2.5 h+ line. One new predicate in the schedule owner — `isAsleepAtHome(human, buildings, hour)` = the night window (`isNightHour`, 20:00–06:00) plus the residency owner’s 55 px proximity rule (`isNearResidence`) — now gates the ambient roll and ends any running ambient chat (`endAmbientHumanChat`; a scripted dialogue session is exempt, so a story beat is never cut mid-line). Because a sleeper is indoors, the renderer also stops drawing them — body, shadow and bubble — for the night, unless the player has them selected: at 200+ citizens the nightly crowd leaves the map instead of milling about, which is the first slice of the large-village readability work. Awake night-goers (tavern, night shift, visitors, anyone still walking home) are untouched. `tests/humanChat.sleepAtHome.test.ts` (6) pins it, including the control that the same settler does speak away from home on the same tick. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-settlers-talk-and-bubble-while-asleep-at-home.md`.

- **Male settler class ladder — ten characters from the delivered V2 spritesheet** — `public/sprites/new_male_set/V2/Spritesheet_V2_10_males.png` (1536×1024, two rows of five, poorest to richest) is cut into ten tightly cropped RGBA sprites in `public/sprites/new_male_set/`, named for the ladder: **Poor Labourer · Farmhand · Craftsman · Pioneer · Shopkeeper · Clerk · Merchant · Prosperous Farmer · Wealthy Gentry · Aristocrat**. Transparency was verified rather than assumed: the sheet is 56.1% fully transparent with **zero** fully-opaque pixels and transparent borders, and the ten cuts measure exactly like the shipped female art (max alpha 254, mean body alpha ≈246, all four corners alpha 0), so no background removal was needed — `scripts/cut-sprite-sheet.mjs` reproduces the cut, reports per-sprite alpha, and applies the same 6 px padding the female cuts use. The wiring now mirrors the female ladder one-for-one: `PREVIEW_WALK_SHEET_PATHS.male`, `HUMAN_VARIANT_LABELS.male`, the new `HUMAN_MALE_CLASS_COUNT = 10`, and the loader's human-sprite set; male variants are a **weighted social draw** like the female ones, over the single shared `Social.CLASS_LADDER_WEIGHTS` (renamed from `FEMALE_CLASS_WEIGHTS` — one definition serves both ladders), so ~47% of villagers are the bottom two classes and ~1.5% the top two and a gentleman's coat reads as an event the way a grand dress does. The five superseded male files are kept under `new_male_set/legacy/` for rollback (no asset deleted) and the sheet stays as the regeneration source. `tests/humanSprites.maleLadder.test.ts` (3) pins ladder length, on-disk asset existence, the weighted shape for both genders, and out-of-range variant wrapping.

- **Verified: the worker boundary is closed, and now pinned** — an end-to-end audit measured both directions of the host ↔ worker transport by reproducing the real path (`GameWorkerHost.queueFullWorldUpload` / `gameWorker.ts` handlers) including the `postMessage` serialization step: a 87-key world census against the save allow-list, the prep snapshot and the tick delta; a mature-world hand-off that confirms the class-typed runtime caches (`mobileGrid`, `grassGrid`, `treeGrid`, `scentGrid`, `humanSocialGrid`, `adjacency`) are stripped and `entityById` is rebuilt; simRng positions resuming in place from a wire snapshot; 2 880 ticks and three commands pushed through deltas and diffed field by field; a failed-tick rollback compared against the exact pre-tick world; and a worker-authored colony saved, reloaded and compared key by key. **No information loss was found** — the 13 measured differences are all inert: `undefined`-valued key presence at three materialization points, the display world's extra `simRng` snapshot (strictly richer), and four documented load-time normalizations (`paused: true` on load, `ensureFullTradeRoutes`, `seedTutorialSeenForExistingState`, `migrateHumanAges` birth-date backfill, with `age` and every `ENTITY_PERSISTED_FIELDS` value intact). `tests/workerBoundary.closure.test.ts` (5) now pins the closure — delta completeness over 240 ticks, mature-world hand-off, command-delta completeness, exact prep rollback, and save/load field survival with the documented normalizations asserted rather than skipped — and `scripts/worker-boundary-audit.mts` reproduces the measurement and fails only on an unclassified difference. Test- and tooling-only: no `src` file changed.

- **Fixed: the working tree’s gates were red — `npm run build` did not compile** — five errors blocked the production build: `frontierCombat.ts` had lost the closing brace of `launchRaidOnRival` (TS1005), `gameLoop.ts` imported `./simWorker/gameWorkerHost` while the file on disk is `GameWorkerHost.ts` (TS1261), `simRng.ts` carried an unused `WorldState` type import (TS6196), and `moonHowler.ts` plus `worldGen.ts` referenced a `pregnancyDueProgress` member that neither `Entity['moonHowlerSaved']` (`gameTypes.ts`) nor `CreateEntityOptions` (`entityFactory.ts`) declares (TS2561/TS2551). The declaring files carry the older revision, so the repair is on the referencing side: the Moon Howler snapshot keeps the omission the simulation audit already records as an unresolved design question (a cursed pregnant settler returns with the `humanLifecycle` fallback due date), and `worldGen.createImmigrantSettler` keeps relying on `entityFactory`’s constructor guarantee instead of passing a value no option declares. Two `as any` casts on `generateWorldMap` in `saveLoad.ts` were removed as well — both calls match the function’s declared overloads — so Oxlint is back to **0 warnings / 0 errors**. `npm run build`, `npm run test:types`, `npm run lint` (321 files) and `npm test` (143 files / 796 tests) all pass, and the 360-day headless gate (`npm run test:full-year`, seed 12345) completes with its invariants satisfied. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-production-build-red-five-compile-errors.md`.
- **Fixed: the diplomacy militia answer named the wrong requirement** — the `border_dispute` → `militia` gate in `getDiplomacyChoiceEligibility` refused with “Need weapons”, while the L33 contract (and the float `respondToDiplomacyEvent` shows the player) pins “Need spears”; `tests/low-2-events-defense.test.ts` was the only failing case. The sibling “show strength” rule keeps its own wording — it is a different answer and no test or documented contract pins it. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-diplomacy-militia-answer-reports-wrong-block-reason.md`.
- **Fixed: an unharmed autumn herd grew the remembered size** — a herd that left the valley with no losses still wrote `migrationNextHerdSize` (`base + 2`, capped) and announced “they will return fatter next autumn”, so “let them pass” no longer brought back the documented baseline herd (`README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` “fat as ever”). `tickMigration`’s departure block is back to the single `if (lost > 0)` shrink, which `tests/migration.herds.test.ts` pins; whether an unharmed herd should ever recover a previously shrunk memory stays the design decision the simulation audit left open. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-unharmed-autumn-herd-grows-the-remembered-size.md`.
- **Fixed: boot preloaded a transition overlay the build does not ship** — `preloadAllSprites()` requested `/sprites/terrain/sand_water_overlay.png`, so every boot logged `Asset preload failed — continuing with fallbacks … Failed to load sprite` and `npm run test:browser` failed on that new console error. The sheet is optional by design: `terrainAtlas` gates the bake on `getSprite(SAND_WATER_OVERLAY_PATH) != null`, and `terrainLayerNeedsRebuild` requests exactly one replacement bake when it becomes ready (pinned by `tests/terrainLayer.overlayCache.test.ts`). The art is not in `public/sprites/terrain/` — `ASSET_REGISTER.md` lists only the water fills and `docs/private/terrain-transition-overlay-and-large-cache-2026-08-21.md` authorizes no asset work — so the path is omitted from the preload list with the reason recorded beside it, exactly as the mountain paths already are; restoring it is a one-line change when the sheet ships. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-sand-water-overlay-sprite-missing.md`.
- **Large-file decommissioning — completed slices** — reduced the simulation’s largest god-file responsibilities by extracting building commands from `buildingActions.ts`, daily orchestration from `App.tsx`, daily-system ownership from `tickLayerDaily.ts`, and residency ownership from `residency.ts` into focused modules with compatibility facades where needed.
- **Residency ownership** — completed selection and scoring (`residencySelection.ts`) plus occupant, partner-transition, event/death/recruitment, and import/load reconciliation (`residencyReconciliation.ts`). The `residency.ts` compatibility facade was then **retired**: all nine callers now import the four focused owners directly (`residencyOccupancy.ts`, `householdComposition.ts`, `residencySelection.ts`, `residencyReconciliation.ts`) and `dayCycle.ts` keeps the compatibility re-exports, so no import path or save field changed.
- **Truth and regression coverage** — updated ownership governance and the decommissioning plan, preserved worker-command and save/load boundaries, and validated the completed residency slices with TypeScript, Oxlint, targeted simulation tests, deterministic long-run coverage, and production build checks.
- **Constants — one declared home per number** — `gameConstants.ts` is now the single source of truth, organised into named sections (`Time`, `Human`, `Animal`, `Prison`, `Social`, `Famine`, `ValleyEcology`) and imported by the modules that own the behaviour instead of being restated locally. Every entry states *why* it holds that value, including values that come from playtesting rather than a formula. `docs/CONSTANTS_GUIDELINE.md` records the standard and `docs/plans/constant-centralization-plan.md` tracks the migration; the remaining categories (ecology, settlement, production, world-gen, moon, story) are follow-up slices.
- **Colony larder — one meal rule, one owner** — the meal rule (1 food for 65 energy, hungry settler, meal-check hour) existed twice in `humanTick.ts` with different guards, and only one copy verified that the eater was a player settler. It is now `tryEatColonyMeal()` in `simulation/humanNeeds.ts`, so the player-settler gate belongs to the rule rather than to each call path, and the restore amount is `Human.MEAL_ENERGY_RESTORE`. The same pass extracted the exhaustion-death sequence (`killFromExhaustion()`), the map-edge clamp (`mapBounds.ts`, shared with `tickLayerSystems.ts`), and collapsed a duplicated leisure steer, removing all four `humanTick.ts` self-clones (duplicate scan 62 → 57 clones at default sensitivity, 0 at the project's configured thresholds). Death causes, food arithmetic, cadence, and map-boundary behaviour are unchanged.
- **Elections — the term token now tells the truth** — the scheduled election was named and stored as `decennial` ("every 10 years") long after terms had been shortened to 5 and then to `ELECTION_INTERVAL_YEARS` (2). The token is now `'term'` across the reason type, `ElectionCeremonyState`, and `tryStartTermElectionCeremony()`; the leadership panel's copy and the leadership type comments match the real cadence. Saves written under the old name are migrated on load (`validateVillageLeaderOnLoad` rewrites `decennial` → `term`), so **no save-version bump is required** and existing colonies keep their head of village.
- **Famine desperation — the foot-bite joke** — with the larder genuinely empty, a settler at or below 30% energy may lunge at a neighbour's foot (15% daily attempt, 40% to land). Deliberately **non-lethal**: nobody dies and nobody loses energy, but the victim is not amused, so the pair loses 8 friendship on a miss and 16 when it lands. The event is logged as a scandal, and the Traveling Theatre can stage *The Famine Foot* from it.
- **Prison guard duty — 24-hour coverage with a real consequence** — one staffed guard covers an 8-hour shift, so a completed Prison needs 3 `prison_guard`s for full coverage. While the Prison holds prisoners with coverage short, each unguarded hour carries a 5% escape chance; an escape frees one prisoner early rather than removing them from the colony. New daily owner `prisonGuardDuty.ts` on its own deterministic RNG stream.
- **Leader residency survives marriage changes** — a remarriage or divorce could leave the leader's household stranded outside the manor. `leaderHouse.ts` now runs an idempotent daily `syncLeaderHouseResidency()`: it evicts non-household occupants, moves the leader's household in, re-homes anyone evicted, and writes the change to the chronicle **once** (no repeat events on later days).
- **Leader's House sits on its ground** — the manor's sprite anchor moved `0.97 → 0.836` to match the painted art's base (~91.6% of frame) above its 86 px transparent bottom band, so the building no longer reads as offset from its footprint.
- **Valley ecology parked, not deleted** — the Stable → Strained → Damaged → Collapse ladder is dormant behind `ValleyEcology.ENABLED = false`, because its strain messages gave the player no agency and had no real consequence. While parked, the effective stage always reads `stable`: no transitions, notifications, focus hints, or hidden hunt/farm/illness effects, while wildlife counts and Nature information stay visible. The ladder is kept in code and re-enabling it is a one-flag change. The lumber-mill "nearby tree" yield multiplier was deleted outright (`treeProximity.ts` removed).
- **Female settler class ladder — ten characters** — the ten cut female portraits (Mudlark → Factory Hand → Scullery Maid → Pioneer → Shop Assistant → Governess → Merchant's Wife → Wealthy Gentry → High Society → Aristocrat) are wired as a weighted social ladder: about 47% of village women are the bottom two classes and about 1.5% the top two, so a grand court dress in the wilderness reads as a real event. Male settlers keep their existing eight variants until matching art exists.
- **Dead code and misleading aliases removed** — deleted the dead exports `ASSIGN_PULSES_PER_DAY`, `getTraitDef`, `hasTraits`, and the unused private `spatialGrid.cellIndex()`, dropped the orphaned `Entity`/`TICKS_PER_DAY` imports and the `YearlyStats.population.trees` field, and removed the re-export aliases that made unused names look used. Oxlint reports 0 warnings / 0 errors.
- **Fixed: speed and pause controls reverted on the next command** — choosing 5×, 10×, or 0.5× (or pausing) was undone by the next ordinary action, because every command rebuilt the display world from a deep clone of the worker's last received snapshot, and `speed`/`paused` are fields of that world. The snapshot predates the click (its `setSpeed`/`setPaused` message is still in flight), so the chosen value was silently replaced by the old one and never resent — `mutateWorld` only forwards a control when it changed. The two player-authored controls are now carried across the display rebuild (`carryPresentationControls`), so the choice outranks a stale snapshot while every authoritative simulation field still comes from the worker. Pause had the mirror-image failure (display read "playing" while the worker still ignored ticks). Regression test `tests/gameLoop.speedControl.test.ts` fails with `expected 1 to be 5` when the carry-over is removed.
- **Elections — term rationale recorded** — the reason `ELECTION_INTERVAL_YEARS` is 2 rather than 10 is now documented beside the constant: it is a session-length decision from playtesting, not a lore figure (a day is 48 real seconds at 1×, so a 10-year term spans many sittings at any speed a player sustains, and higher multipliers are nominal because at most `MAX_PIPELINE_DEPTH` = 4 worker ticks can be in flight).
- **Fixed: a jailed leader lost the office** — a scandal arrest stamped the offender's occupation to `settler` with no office guard, and `releasePrisoners()` emptied the prison fields without restoring anything, so a leader could leave prison as a plain settler while still holding `villageLeaderId` (caught by the full-year invariant gate at day 60, root-caused with a temporary per-tick probe to the exact arrest tick). The office now survives both transitions, ordinary work is re-assigned afterwards by the normal assignment layer, and the Moon Howler revert path — which had the same shape, defaulting the occupation to `settler` unless a job slot happened to be free — is hardened the same way. This is a regression fix for the 2026-08-28 leader-occupation report, whose earlier fix only stamped the office at handover and load.
- **Auto-play phase 2 — civic infrastructure** — new step 8 (`decideCivic`) builds the **Town Hall** and then a **Blacksmith**, first copy only and one at a time. The Town Hall is what made the old step 7 (festival) unreachable in practice, and carries taxes, trade, elections, and the scandal buffer; the Blacksmith is what gives the researched forge something to do and what gates the Mine in step 9. Both are research-gated and the gate is not restated — `canPlaceBuilding` refuses a locked building, so the step simply waits until the owner research lands (the placement owner requires the research *node* to be `researched`, not just listed in `unlockedTechs`). Two regression tests cover the build once `forestry_1` is researched and the wait before it.
- **Auto-play now builds the economy instead of idling** — the bot's ladder only ever built Houses and food producers, so it spent its opening wood and gold, `canAfford` then failed for every step, and it sat idle for the rest of the run; nothing in the ladder produced wood, stone, iron, or gold. New step 8 (`decideIndustry`) builds the first **Lumber Mill** when wood drops under 120, a **Quarry** under 60 stone, a **Mine** under 20 iron (only once a Blacksmith stands — there is nothing to forge otherwise) and a **Store** under 80 gold, one at a time, and only when `canAfford` and `canPlaceBuilding` agree, so a research-gated building simply waits its turn. Two regression tests cover the shortage build and the Mine's smith gate.
- **Fixed: notifications never left the screen on their own** — ordinary toasts had no timer, so they only disappeared when the player clicked the ✕ and otherwise piled up on screen; Big News already had a wall-clock auto-dismiss. `useTransientGameFeedback` now schedules one timer per visible notification (`NOTIFICATION_DISPLAY_MS = 12 s`) and dismisses it through the same durable `dismissedNotificationIds` path, without the click feedback a manual dismiss plays. Stale timers are cleared when a notification disappears and on unmount.
- **Fixed: settlers could be named from the 10-name boot fallback** — every naming site (`entityFactory`, `worldGen`, births, immigration) draws from whatever pool is installed *at that moment*, and the loader's eager load-on-import had been removed (so one test could observe the pre-load state), leaving those sites on the embedded fallback until something else called `loadNames()`. The load starts on import again; the explicit `await loadNames()` calls at app boot and in the worker are unchanged, and the loader's own test now imports a fresh module instance and asserts the contract that matters — census pool in, boot/legacy markers upgraded — instead of a transient pre-load state.
- **Fixed: the hunting arrow came out of the building** — a staffed Hunting Spot resolves its kill in the daily economy layer, and that used to emit the hunt visual from `building.x` / `building.y` with `hunterId: building.id`, so the shot read as an automatic tower firing (the defect half that survived the arrow-shape fix in `de98bd6`). The shot now leaves the **live assigned hunter's position**, and an unstaffed Hunting Spot emits nothing at all — kill resolution, damage, reward, and wildlife removal are untouched. Regression test `tests/huntVisuals.origin.test.ts` drives the real `initGame` + `gameTick` path and fails against the old building-anchored emission.
- **Fixed: three child sprites shipped with an opaque pale rectangle** — `child_boy_bakersboy.png`, `child_boy_merchantson.png`, and `child_girl_poorservant.png` were 100 % opaque with a pale matte baked into the pixels, so those children rendered as a pasted rectangle over the terrain. The matte was removed offline (border flood-fill matched to the art's own near-white tone, art-side as the report prescribes — no runtime keying was added): each file now measures 40–59 % transparent with under 0.25 % near-white opaque coverage, and enclosed light details survive. The requested asset-contract test is still missing (the Node test tier has no PNG decoder); how the sprites read over grass/forest/water/snow at play zoom is still a human check.
- **Developer: `npm run build` no longer writes `*.tsbuildinfo` — and no longer hunts for emitted `.js`** — the build ran `tsc -b`, whose up-to-date check looks for each project's *emitted* output, so a `noEmit` project was permanently "out of date because output file 'src/App.js' does not exist" (a full re-check on every run) while the incremental file was dropped beside the tsconfigs — `tsconfig.app.tsbuildinfo` and `tsconfig.node.tsbuildinfo` in the repository root. `build` now runs the two projects the same way `test:types` already runs the test project — `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit && tsc -p tsconfig.node.json --noEmit` — with identical coverage and no build-info file at all. The two stray root files were deleted, both configs still point `tsBuildInfoFile` at `node_modules/.cache/` so a manual `tsc -b` cannot litter the root again, and `*.tsbuildinfo` stays in `.gitignore` as a safety net.
- **Mine retune — ores only, and available from day one** — a Mine now extracts **iron or gold**, chosen per mine, and stone is the Quarry's job (the Quarry already produced stone only; the Mine's old `stone` default is gone). The Mine no longer requires the **Deep Mining** research, so a new colony can mine immediately, and it costs **30 wood · 15 stone · 15 gold** (was 40 · 20 · 25). `MINE_ORES` is the single list behind the picker, the worker command union, and the command validator; `mineOreForMode` returns gold only when the player chose it, so a save written while the Mine still had a `stone` mode extracts iron instead of minting gold. The two research labels that would otherwise lie were corrected: **Deep Mining** now reads "Quarry yield +20%" (it never unlocked the Mine again) and **Refining** reads "Mine output +30% · unlocks Iron Pickaxes forge order".
- **Fixed: the build was red — `assignResident` did not match its owner** — the working tree added a worker command `{ op: 'assignResident'; buildingId; humanId }` routed to `assignResidentToBuilding(...)`, but that residency owner takes only a building id (it re-runs *automatic* assignment; settlers pick homes themselves) and the main-thread route calls it two-argument. With no sender anywhere setting `humanId`, the command now matches its owner: the project typecheck exits 0 again. A future per-resident "assign this settler" action belongs in the residency owner first.
- **Fixed: the camera clamp showed an empty ring around the world again** — `viewState.clampCameraTarget` gained a `viewportW !== worldW` guard and a 5% margin, so a viewport exactly the size of the world skipped the viewport-aware branch (cap `worldW`, ring visible) and the overscroll allowance was 2.5× the documented value. The branch is now taken whenever a viewport is supplied, and the margin is back to the documented 2%; the three `tests/cameraClamp.viewport.test.ts` regression cases are green.
- **Fixed: the name loader started a file read as an import side effect** — `nameLoader.ts` ended with a module-level `loadNames()`, so importing it for any reason began an async disk/fetch load and raced the boot-fallback contract (a timing-dependent red in `tests/nameLoader.poolUpgrade.test.ts`). Removed: `App.tsx` (`Promise.all([preloadAllSprites(), loadNames(), …])` and the pre-`initGame` await) and `gameWorker.ts` already load names explicitly.
- **Tests: two contracts re-pinned to the implementations they cover** — `pathfinding.test.ts` now asserts the deliberate endpoint snapping (a blocked start or goal resolves to the walkable tile beside it, and a genuinely unreachable goal still returns `null`) instead of the old fail-outright contract, and `humanMovement.ts` again includes both endpoint tiles in the *no-entity* `commutePathCacheKey` fallback, so different commutes cannot collide while the per-entity key keeps its tile-stable behaviour for the sole caller.
- **Auto-play builds the Leader's House first** — the bot's first colony act is now the **free** Leader's House (`BUILDING_CONFIGS.LeaderHouse.cost` is all zeroes and the building is unique), which world generation never places, so a fresh colony gets its official residence before staffing, housing, or food. Cards are still answered first (that is the documented invariant), and the step retires itself the moment a house stands or is on the way.
- **Fixed: auto-play starved the colony ladder on an unanswerable card** — the bot proposed the card answer it *preferred* rather than one the card owner accepts. `respondToRaidEvent` refuses `defend` without spears and `payoff` without the tribute food, and `respondToStoryEvent` **re-queues** a refused answer, so the card stayed open while the bot re-proposed the same refused answer every in-game hour: no Farm, no staffing, no housing, no research — exactly the reported "doesn't build farms or other things". Each card owner now exposes its own answer eligibility (`getRaidChoiceEligibility` in `frontierCombat.ts` — which `respondToRaidEvent` itself now applies — plus `getStoryChoiceEligibility` in `storyEvents.ts`, fed by the theatre, deer-parliament, wedding, and invention-fair owners), the same contract `getDiplomacyChoiceEligibility` already provided, so no accept rule is restated in the bot. When no answer can land, the bot leaves the card and gets on with the colony.
- **Fixed: the Leader's House's beds counted as spare settler housing** — `decideHousing` asked `populationGrowth.getOpenBeds`, which counts every residence including the Leader's House. Its 12 beds are reserved for the leader's household (`leaderHouse.syncLeaderHouseResidency` evicts and re-homes anyone else), so once one stood the guard read "9 beds free" for three homeless settlers and the bot never built a House again. The new `getOpenPlayerBeds` counts only beds a settler may be assigned; `getOpenBeds` keeps its existing meaning for population and diagnostics.
- **New: in-app virtual player (🤖 Auto-play)** — a pure, stateless decision engine (`virtualPlayer.ts`) plus its driver hook (`useVirtualPlayer.ts`) that plays the real game **on screen**: at most one real `WorkerCommand` per in-game hour, dispatched through the player's own door (`applyGameAction`, so with the worker active it travels the real worker transport), never mutating `WorldState`. It answers open cards first (raid → outgoing war-band → diplomacy → Village Request → story), then staffs an empty workplace, houses the homeless, tops up food, starts affordable research, and hosts a festival; every placement is validated with the game's own `canPlaceBuilding` rules and anchored on `getPlayerCampCenter`, with tuning in a documented `gameConstants.VirtualPlayer` section. The header shows the current decision plus a five-act history, and the FPS meter — now reporting current / min / avg with a sample count — switches on with it beside a `settlers · buildings · acts` readout, so frame cost can be watched as the colony grows. The Auto-play toggle is **dev-only** (`import.meta.env.DEV`); Village Request / diplomacy answers now respect gold and eligibility gates so a failed accept cannot stall the hourly act.
- **New: Family tree in Valley Overview → Village** — browse settlers by surname, then open a stamboom for anyone: grandparents, aunts/uncles, parents, spouse, siblings, children, nephews/nieces, and grandchildren (English labels). “Find on map” jumps to the selected person.
- **Fixed: Village/dashboard “Go →” buttons did nothing** — `GameOverlays` uses `pointer-events-none`, and `GameDashboard` never re-enabled clicks, so concern Go buttons were dead. Dashboard now has `pointer-events-auto`, closes before navigating, and Valley overview Go/Place actions that need the map close the overlay first.
- **UI: one Overview button opens the Valley overview** — the right column is a single **Overview** control. Inside the overlay you pick Village, Frontier, Nature, Progress, Log, or More. Hotkeys (V/F/N/P/L/M) and alerts still jump to the matching panel. The narrow rail hides while the overlay is open.
- **Fixed: intro music kept playing into the game** — leaving the title screen only called `introMusic.stop()`, while bootstrap gesture listeners still invoked `ensureIntroAudio()` during map setup (director `gameplayActive` was still false), and an in-flight `start()` could finish after `stop()` and revive the track. Intro is now explicitly dismissed when leaving the title (`setIntroAudioAllowed(false)`), `ensureIntroAudio` respects that flag, and `stop()` bumps a generation so late starts cannot resume.
- **Fixed: settler names stayed on the tiny boot pool** — `ensureNamesLoaded()` installed a 10-name embedded fallback and `fixDefaultNames` only rewrote John/Mary/Smith, so after the full lists loaded most founding settlers kept Elijah/Batten-style defaults. Full-file loads now mark the pool as `full`, and `fixDefaultNames` upgrades boot-fallback names once those lists are ready. The boot markers were also changed to strings **absent from the census files** (the old set — including Whitaker — overlapped `last-names.txt`, so a real file draw looked like a default). Legacy overlapping boot names are upgraded once; later Whitaker/Elijah draws from the files are kept. New games await `loadNames()` before `initGame` so founders are named from the lists.
- **Fixed: Nature tab listed trees as wildlife** — trees are scenery/resources, not livable wildlife; the Trees bar is removed from the Nature wildlife population panel (yearly stats already dropped `population.trees`).
- **Fixed: missing mountain sprites no longer preload** — boot no longer requests `/sprites/mountains/*.png` until that art ships (stamp already no-ops when the image is absent).
- **Package manager** — `pnpm` removed from runtime `dependencies`; `packageManager` field set; conflicting `yarn.lock` / regenerated `package-lock.json` removed so `pnpm-lock.yaml` is the single lockfile. **Superseded:** the repository settled on **npm** — `package.json`'s `packageManager` is now an empty string and `package-lock.json` is the single lockfile (`AGENTS.md` §4). The orphaned `pnpm-lock.yaml` was deleted in the 2026-09-20 recovery, still listing the `dependency-cruiser`/`ts-call-graph` devDependencies that had already been removed.
- **Regression tests added in this working tree** — `tests/virtualPlayer.test.ts` (15 — the auto-play ladder and its once-per-in-game-hour cadence rule), `tests/conceptionEvent.labelling.test.ts` (4 — an expectation is no longer logged as a delivery), `tests/humanNeeds.mealRule.test.ts` (9 — every larder-meal gate, including the visitor refusal), `tests/gameLoop.speedControl.test.ts` (3 — speed and pause survive a display rebuild; fails `expected 1 to be 5` without the fix), `tests/villageLeadership.legacyElectionToken.test.ts` (3 — the `decennial` → `term` save migration), `tests/leaderRemarriage.residency.test.ts` (2 — the leader's household after a remarriage), `tests/famineDesperation.test.ts` (3 — the foot-bite is non-lethal). Updated: `tests/renderer.presentationLayout.test.ts` (the manor anchor is asserted against the old full-frame value no longer being used), `tests/huntLogic.test.ts` (the parked valley no longer cuts hunt yield), `tests/electionGossip.dedup.test.ts` (renamed term token). Suite: **95 files / 507 tests**, local-only — `tests/` is gitignored by design and is never committed or pushed.
- **Fixed: a conception was logged as a birth** — the "expecting" announcement re-used the `'birth'` event type, so every consumer that filters on births inherited the error: a 360-day run at seed 12345 reported **56** birth-typed events against **32** completed deliveries, the Chronicle's "Births" filter doubled up, and the council report, the `first_birth` tutorial watch, and the rumour ledger's family category all treated an expectation as a child. Expectations now carry their own `'conception'` type: the Chronicle gains a **Conceptions (expecting)** filter (🤰) beside **Births**, and the council report prints `Births N · Expecting M · …` from two separate buckets. The scope is the label, not the pregnancy — the conception owner and the daily cadence are unchanged, `birthsCompletedThisInterval` always counted deliveries, and the log passes through load unchanged, so **no save migration or version bump is required** (historical `'birth'` entries keep reading as births). The affair helper deliberately stays `'scandal'`: the secret is the event the Chronicle and scandal feed must show, and it never inflated births. `EventLogPanel`'s exhaustive `Record<GameEventLog['type'], …>` icon/colour maps mean a future event type cannot be added without deciding how it looks. Regression test `tests/conceptionEvent.labelling.test.ts` pins the married writer, the youth-love writer, the council line, and a real delivery.
- **Balance — affairs are rarer** — the per-pair daily tryst chance was halved (0.14 with a church, 0.20 without), because a same-seed year at the old values produced 55 established affairs, 176 scandal events, 60 exposures, 28 imprisonments and 67 divorces, so scandal drama dominated the social simulation. The multipliers (festival 1.4, performers 1.35, cohabiting 1.55) are deliberately unchanged so the size of the change is attributable to one knob, and every value now lives in `gameConstants.ts` as a documented `Relationship` section. Same-seed year after: 40 affairs, 84 marriages, 34 divorces, 20 caught scandals.
- **Known behaviour: courtship is a one-day process** — the base courtship rate is 4 per in-game **hour** (`PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE = 1/TICKS_PER_HOUR`), i.e. 96 per 72-tick day against the 100 needed to marry, and there was no cooldown after a divorce, so a settler could divorce and remarry within two days. (The "~96–144/day" figure this entry used to quote was unreliable in both directions: 96 is only reached while a pair stays within 10 units on every tick — otherwise they chase and gain nothing — while the festival/performers/cohabitation/trait multipliers the range ignored can push the rate past 13 per tick, so the fast case is ~2 in-game hours, not a day.) Resolved 2026-09-17: the church is out of the formula, work-shift courting is cut to 15 %, and a 1-day courtship cooldown now applies — see the entry above and `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-church-accelerates-courtship.md`.
- **Private bug records preserved** — reproducible defects found in this working tree (leader remarriage residency, Leader's House anchor, duplicated larder rule, stale election token, speed/pause revert, broken full-year gate, conception logged as a birth, jailed leader losing office, and the four missing mountain sprite paths) each have a local record under `BUG_REPORTS/` with root cause, fix, regression test, and save/migration impact. Three remain open and are documented as such: the three child sprites without an alpha channel, the Hunting Spot arrow still emitted from the building, and the unshipped `/sprites/mountains/*.png` art (found by the new browser tier).
- **Browser acceptance harness — a test tier the project did not have** — every existing check runs in Node (`vitest`, `tsc`, `oxlint`), so nothing could see the Pixi/WebGL terrain, the real DOM, real fonts, the viewport, or the module worker — and two of the three open defect records are pure presentation defects. `scripts/browser-smoke.mjs` now boots the **production build** in a real Chrome (the one already installed; **zero new dependencies** — Node's `fetch`, `WebSocket` and `zlib` plus the Chrome DevTools Protocol), walks the real player path (intro → "Settle the valley"), dismisses the Quick-start overlay, proves the freeze lifts, captures labelled screenshots plus a JSON report, surfaces the browser's own console errors, page exceptions, failed requests and 4xx/5xx responses, and decodes the PNGs itself to say whether the valley actually painted. It fails the run on anything new, and exits non-zero, so it gates like `npm test`. Measured on the real build: WebGL2 present (`ANGLE … D3D11`), 3 300–3 500 distinct sampled colours, HUD clock advancing `09:00 → 11:00` unprompted.
- **New defect found by the browser tier** — the first five runs reported one console error on every boot: `Failed to load sprite: /sprites/mountains/45.png`. `public/sprites/mountains/` does not exist, yet `spriteLoader.ts` preloads four directional mountain paths and `terrainLayer.ts` stamps them, so the game asks for art it does not ship and falls back silently. Recorded as `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-10-mountain-sprites-missing.md` (open — the fix depends on whether the art is coming or abandoned), and listed in the harness's `KNOWN_CONSOLE_ERRORS` so the gate is green while the defect stays visible as `knownConsoleErrors`.
- **Determinism limit measured, not assumed** — a screenshot baseline is the obvious first idea and it does not work here: the shipping UI generates a random map every run (`worldGen.initGame`: `seed ?? Math.floor(Math.random() * 1_000_000)`, and `MapSetupScreen` never offers a seed). The harness therefore installs a deterministic PRNG before app scripts run to pin the **map layout**, and measures what is left: two same-seed runs drift **30.3 %** of sampled pixels over the full frame (water shimmer, foliage, camera easing) but only **0.5 %** over the HUD strip — so the baseline gate is region-scoped to the HUD and the full-frame number is reported as information. Pinning the map is also not pinning the colony: `nameLoader.ts:99` still draws settler names from the global `Math.random` stream, so two same-seed runs produced different village-head names (Jude Shah vs Paul Roach) — direct evidence for roadmap **T3**, whose job is exactly to move the remaining modules onto `simRng` owner streams.
- **Evaluation recorded** — `docs/plans/browser-testing-evaluation.md` answers the "is modern-web.dev something for us?" question with measurements: the *browser tier* is worth adopting, the *runner* (`@web/test-runner`) is not, because Vitest already is the Vite-native runner and a second one would duplicate config, module pipelines, coverage and mocking for no gain. The doc lists five slices (B1 smoke/console truth, B2 screenshot evidence, B3 viewport/DPI matrix, B4 worker-in-browser, B5 per-scene fixtures), the measured findings, the costs, and the honest limits of what the harness does not yet drive.
- **Auto-play phase 3 — the rest of the player action surface** — the bot's ladder grew from nine steps to seventeen so Auto-play can carry a normal game end to end, and every new step retires itself (already repaired, already queued, already set, nobody eligible) so nothing can monopolise its hourly act. After the existing colony work it now: **repairs** the worst-damaged completed player building below 80 % health (`decideRepairs`, priced by the maintenance owner's new `getRepairBuildingEligibility`); **queues forge orders** — iron spears → shields → pickaxes — for a staffed Blacksmith, gated on the forge owner's existing `getForgeBlockReason`, so research, the forge chain, a busy smith, an already-forged order and the iron cost stay the owner's call; **tunes buildings** (`decideBuildingTuning` — the Mine digs gold only while the treasury is under 80 and iron otherwise, the Hunting Spot keeps `auto` unless food is short and then takes deer, and the Workshop runs the richest recipe `canAffordWorkshopRecipe` allows); **recruits a settler** (`decideRecruitment` — one morning window a day at in-game hour 9, and only with two spare assignable beds, nobody homeless, a five-day larder and the new `getRecruitSettlerEligibility`); **works its visitors** (`decideVisitorRelations` — one audience per visit, the smith's deliver quest once the goods are in store, then a trade priced by the new `getVisitorTradeEligibility`); **fills manual workplaces** (`decideManualStaffing` — one explicit `assignWorker` for a Church/Prison/Barracks/School/Town Hall with an open slot and an idle adult, never a blanket `autoStaffWorkers`); **keeps the colony up** (`decideUpkeep` — expanding a standing home when settlers are homeless with no plot left to build on, and taming a creature a Taming Post can reach from a five-day larder); and **manages its neighbours** (`decideRivalRelations` — a food gift to a tense rival, then a truce, then an open trade route, and a raid only against a tense rival holding three times the march provisions, so the bot is not a warmonger).
